non-generating elements
. The rest of the elements of
{\bm{X}}
and
{\bm{Y}}
are termed
generating elements
. Let
(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{p}_{b})
, for some
b>0
, be the equivalence classes of
\mathfrak{P}_{\bm{X}}
, the partition associated with
{\bm{X}}
. For
w\in[b]
, we say that
\mathfrak{p}_{w}
is non-generating if it contains
at least one non-generating
element of
{\bm{X}}
. Otherwise
\mathfrak{p}_{w}
will be said to be generating. The same convention is adopted for
{\bm{Y}}
.

We are now ready to derive the desired upper bound on (
6.13
). We proceed as follows:

•

Fix a couple of pair partitions
\mathfrak{P}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}
. Note that, as
k\leqslant K_{0}
, the number of choices of
(\mathfrak{P},\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}})
is bounded above by
(K_{0}h)^{4K_{0}h}
.

•

We next evaluate the innermost sum in (
6.13
), fixing
{\bm{q}}_{\#}
. Since
\mathfrak{P}_{\bm{X}}=\mathfrak{P}
one only needs to sum over the possible (common) values of the equivalence classes of
\mathfrak{P}
.

•

Let
(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{p}_{b})
be the equivalence classes of
\mathfrak{P}
and without loss of generality assume that
(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{p}_{b_{0}})
are generating for some
b_{0}\leqslant b
. Notice that the number of elements of
{\bm{X}}
is
2kh
. If
\mathfrak{P}_{\bm{X}}=\mathfrak{P}
for some
{\bm{X}}
and
\mathfrak{P}
is a pair partition then
b_{0}\leqslant(k+N_{+}-\widehat{m}_{2})h
.

•

As
{\bm{X}}\subset[N]
the total number of possible values of
\{\mathfrak{p}_{w}\}_{w=1}^{b_{0}}
is bounded above by
N^{b_{0}}\leqslant N^{(k+N_{+}-\widehat{m}_{2})h}
.

•

On the other hand, upon recalling (
5.39
) we find that

\prod_{i=1}^{2h}\mathfrak{I}(X^{(i)},Y^{(i)},q^{(i)})=1\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\mathfrak{p}_{w}\geqslant N-\overline{\bm{q}}_{\#},\quad w\in[b]\setminus[b_{0}+1],

where
\overline{\bm{q}}_{\#}:=\sum_{i=1}^{2h}q^{(i)}
. Thus, the number of choices for the of non-generating equivalences classes is bounded above by
\overline{\bm{q}}_{\#}^{b}\leqslant\overline{\bm{q}}_{\#}^{K_{0}h}
.

•

We repeat the same idea as above to compute the second sum in (
6.13
).

Putting all the above pieces together we now obtain from (
6.13
) that

(6.14)

\mathfrak{T}_{k}(z)\leqslant 2\widehat{C}_{\ref {lem:comb-bound-tube-1}}^{2h\widetilde{m}K_{0}}\cdot(2K_{0}h)^{8K_{0}h}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{2K_{0}h}\cdot N^{2(k+N_{+}-\widehat{m}_{2})\gamma^{\prime}h}\left[\sum_{{\bm{q}}_{\#}}\overline{\bm{q}}_{\#}^{K_{0}h}\prod_{i=1}^{2h}\cdot\mathfrak{d}_{q^{(i)}}\right].

Applying (
5.50
) and (
5.54
) one can note that the sum over
{\bm{q}}_{\#}
in (
6.14
) is bounded by
\widetilde{C}^{\widetilde{m}K_{0}h}
, for some constant
\widetilde{C}<\infty
, depending only on
\widehat{m}_{2}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
. Together, this yields (
6.10
) and completes the proof of
(
6.8
).

To prove (
6.9
) we simply note that, again by Lemma
5.13
, (
6.13
) continues to hold for any
z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(2)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
with
N^{2h(\gamma^{\prime}-1)\cdot(k+N_{+}-\widehat{m}_{2})}
replaced by one. Therefore, repeating the rest of the arguments we obtain (
6.9
) and complete the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.2.
Step 2: uniform upper bounds on non-dominant terms

In this section we derive uniform upper bounds on the non-dominant terms in the expansion (
5.6
). The following is the main result of this section.

Theorem 6.5
.

Let Assumption
1.1
hold.
Fix parameters
\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
such that
(\gamma-1)\vee 1<\gamma^{\prime}<\gamma
and
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/\varepsilon_{0}
is sufficiently small. Let
d\geqslant 0
. Then,
for all large
N
,

(6.15)

\max_{s\in\{1,2\}}\mathbf{P}\left[\sup_{z\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}}\left|\sum\limits_{k\neq\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|\geqslant N^{-(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})/4}\right]\leqslant 1/N.

Furthermore, for all large
N
,

(6.16)

\mathbf{P}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(2)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}}\left|\sum\limits_{k\neq\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|\geqslant N^{-(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})/4}\right]\leqslant 1/N.

The difference between (
6.15
) and (
6.16
) is that the former yields bounds for tubes with diminishing width (in
N
), while the latter provides bounds for certain tubes of fixed width. The bound (
6.16
) will be used in the proof of Theorem
5.4
.

To prove Theorem
6.5
we will use the uniform bounds on the moments of
\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)
that were derived in Section
6.1
. We will also need a bound on the second moment of the supremum of the derivatives of
\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Consider the same setup as in Theorem
6.5
. Then there exist constants
0<c_{\ref {lem:der-bd}},C_{\ref {lem:der-bd}}<\infty
, depending only on
\gamma^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
, such that for any
d\geqslant 0
and
k\in[N]
we have that

(6.17)

\max_{s\in\{1,2\}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sup_{z\in\left(\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd}}\log N/N}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|^{2}\right]\leqslant C_{\ref {lem:der-bd}}\left(\frac{N}{\log N}\right)^{3}

and

(6.18)

\mathbf{E}\left[\sup_{z\in\left(\mathcal{T}^{d,(2)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd}}\log N/N}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|^{2}\right]\leqslant C_{\ref {lem:der-bd}}\left(\frac{N}{\log N}\right)^{4}.

The proof of Theorem
6.5
will also require estimates on the
non-random
term in the expansion of
\det(P_{z}^{\delta})
, as follows.
Recall the notation
\widehat{d}
, see (
5.33
), and that
P_{z}^{\delta}=P_{N,\gamma}^{Q}-zI_{N}
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Consider the same setup as in Lemma
6.1
. We have the following bounds.

(a)

If
\widehat{d}>0
then, for all large
N
,

(6.19)

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}|\widehat{\det}_{0}(z)|\leqslant\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{4}\right)^{N},\quad s\in\{1,2\}.

(b)

Fix
d\geqslant 0
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}>0
. Let
\widehat{d}\geqslant 0
. Then there exists a constant
c_{\star}\in(0,1)
so that,
with
X_{\star}:=[N]\setminus[N-\widehat{m}_{2}+N_{+}]
and
Y_{\star}:=[\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}]
,

(6.20)

c_{\star}\leqslant\inf_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}{|\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}(X_{\star},Y_{\star},z)|}\leqslant\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}{|\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}(X_{\star},Y_{\star},z)|}\leqslant c_{\star}^{-1},\quad s\in\{1,2\}.

Remark 6.8
.

Note that if
\widehat{d}=0
then
X_{\star}=Y_{\star}=\emptyset
. Therefore, in that case
\widehat{\det}_{0}(z)=\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}(X_{\star},Y_{\star},z)
(see (
5.9
)-(
5.10
) and (
5.34
)-(
5.36
)). Thus, for
\widehat{d}=0
Lemma
6.7
(ii) provides a lower bound on the (dominant term)
\widehat{\det}_{0}(z)
.

Equipped with Lemmas
6.6
and
6.7
we now prove Theorem
6.5
.

Proof of Theorem
6.5
(assuming Lemmas
6.6
and
6.7
) .

Fix
s\in\{1,2\}
and

K_{0}=K_{0}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\widetilde{m})=\lceil 30/(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})\rceil+\widetilde{m}
.
We first show that for any
k
such that
k+N_{+}\geqslant K_{0}
,

(6.21)

\max_{s\in\{1,2\}}\mathbf{P}\left[\sup_{z\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\left|\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|\geqslant N^{-2}\right]\leqslant 2/N^{3}.

To see (
6.21
), denote

\mathcal{F}(z):=\left\{|\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)|\leqslant N^{-\frac{(k+N_{+}-\widehat{m}_{2})(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})}{4}}\right\}

and

\mathcal{F}_{0}:=\left\{\sup_{z\in\left(\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd}}\log N/N}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|\geqslant N^{3}\right\}.

Applying Lemma
6.6
(in particular (
6.17
)) and Markov’s inequality we obtain that

(6.22)

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{F}_{0})\leqslant N^{-3}.

From our choice of
K_{0}
and Lemma
6.2
(i) it follows that

(6.23)

\sup_{z\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{F}(z)^{c})\leqslant N^{-15}.

Let
\mathcal{N}
be a net of
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
of mesh size
N^{-6}
, which has
cardinality at most
O(N^{12})
. Then, (
6.22
)-(
6.23
) yield that

(6.24)

\mathbf{P}(\cup_{z\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{F}(z)^{c}\cup\mathcal{F}_{0})\leqslant 2/N^{3}.

On the other hand, we find that for all large
N
and
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}
the ball
D(z^{\prime},N^{-6})
is contained in the
(c_{\ref {lem:der-bd}}\log N/N)
-blow up of
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
. Thus, using the triangle inequality and the first order Taylor series expansion, we see that on the event
\cap_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}}\mathcal{F}(z^{\prime})\cap\mathcal{F}_{0}^{c}
,

(6.25)

\displaystyle|\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}}|\widehat{\det}_{k}(z^{\prime})|+\mathrm{dist\,}(z,\mathcal{N})\cdot\sup_{z^{\prime}\in\left(\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd}}\log N/N}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\widehat{\det}_{k}(z^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant N^{-\frac{1}{4}(k+N_{+}-\widehat{m}_{2})\cdot(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})}+N^{-3}\leqslant N^{-2},

for all large
N
, where in the last step follows from the facts that
k+N_{+}\geqslant K_{0}
and our choice of
K_{0}
. Therefore, from (
6.24
) we now have (
6.21
).

Next we claim that

(6.26)

\mathbf{P}\left[\sup_{z\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\left|\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|\geqslant N^{-2}\right]\leqslant 2/N^{3},

for any
k\in\mathds{N}
such that
k+N_{+}<\widehat{m}_{2}
. Indeed, by Lemma
6.1
, we see that (
6.23
) continues to hold in this case. Thus, repeating the same proof as for (
6.21
), we obtain (
6.26
).

Now we aim to show that

(6.27)

\mathbf{P}\left[\sup_{z\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\left|\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|\geqslant 2N^{-\frac{1}{2}(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})}\right]\leqslant 2/N^{3},

for any
k
such that
\widehat{m}_{2}+1\leqslant k+N_{+}\leqslant K_{0}
.
To this end, we set
h_{0}=h_{0}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\widetilde{m},{\sf g}_{0})=\lceil 15/(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})\rceil
. We apply Lemma
6.4
with this
h_{0}
and Markov’s inequality to find that (
6.23
) continues to hold for any
\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}+1\leqslant k\leqslant K_{0}-N_{+}
. Thus, repeating the same argument as above, yet again, we further observe that (
6.25
) also holds, except for the last step. Since
\gamma^{\prime}>\gamma-1
the last step there can be replaced by
2N^{-\frac{1}{2}(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})}
. Therefore we have (
6.27
).

Combining the probability estimates (
6.21
), (
6.26
), and (
6.27
), and using a union bound we deduce that

\mathbf{P}\left[\sup_{z\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d,(s)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\left|\sum\limits_{k\notin\{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+},0\}}\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}N^{-(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})/4}\right]\leqslant 3/N^{2},

for all large
N
. If
\widehat{d}=0
then, by (
6.44
),
\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}=0
, and hence we have the desired probability bound. If
\widehat{d}>0
then upon using Lemma
6.7
(a) the proof of (
6.15
) completes. The proof of (
6.16
), being similar, is omitted.
∎

We now proceed to the proof of Lemma
6.6
, which uses the bounds derived in Lemmas
6.1
and
6.2
, and Cauchy’s integral formula for smooth functions. Cauchy’s integral formula allows us to control the second moment of the supremum of a random analytic function (and its derivative) on a nice domain by controlling the supremum of the second moment of the same analytic function on a slightly enlarged domain. To carry out this scheme, we first show that the blow up of any tube
\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}
,
s=1,2
, away from the bad set
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
, is again contained in a union of the tubes with slightly modified parameters.

Lemma 6.9
.

Fix
0<\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}<\varepsilon_{0}
, and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
. We have the following geometric properties of the tubes.

(i)

There exists a constant
C_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}<\infty
such that

(6.28)

\mathcal{T}^{d}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\subset(p(S^{1}))^{C_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}}.

In particular,
\mathcal{T}^{d}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
is a bounded set.

(ii)

Fix
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}>0
such that
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
. There exists some constant
\varepsilon_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}
, depending only on
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
, such that, for any
\beta\in(0,1/2)
, and
\varepsilon\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}\varepsilon_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}
, we have

(6.29)

\left(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(1)}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{\beta\varepsilon}\subset\left(\mathcal{T}_{(1+\beta)\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(1)}\cup\mathcal{T}_{\beta\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime},(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}^{d-{\sf g}_{0},(2)}\right)\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2},

for
d\geqslant 1
, while for
d\geqslant 0
we have

(6.30)

\left(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(2)}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{\beta\varepsilon}\subset\left(\mathcal{T}_{(1+\beta)\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(2)}\cup\mathcal{T}_{\beta\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime},(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}^{d+{\sf g}_{0},(1)}\right)\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2}.

Proof.

We begin with the proof of part (i). We observe that
z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
implies that there exists a root
\eta(z)
of
p_{z}(\cdot)=0
such that
||\eta(z)|-1|\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
. Set
z_{0}:=p(\eta(z)/|\eta(z)|)\in p(S^{1})
. By the triangle inequality and uniform boundedness of
p^{\prime}(\cdot)
in a compact neighborhood of
S^{1}
, one has that
|z-z_{0}|\leqslant C_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
for some
C_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}<\infty
depending on
p
,
yielding (
6.28
).

Turning to the proof of part (ii) we pick any
z_{\star}
belonging to the set on the
lhs
of (
6.29
). By definition, there exists a
z_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(1)}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
such that
|z_{\star}-z_{0}|\leqslant\beta\varepsilon
. Thus, for
\varepsilon\leqslant\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2
, we have that
z_{\star}\notin\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2}
. On the other hand,
noting that for
j\in[\widetilde{m}]
, the maps
z\mapsto\eta_{j}(z)
are analytic outside
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2}
and using that
\mathcal{T}^{d}
is a bounded set, we deduce that

(6.31)

\max_{j\in[\widetilde{m}]}|\eta_{j}(z_{0})-\eta_{j}(z_{\star})|\leqslant O_{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}(1)\cdot|z_{\star}-z_{0}|\leqslant\beta\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}/4,

where in the last inequality we chose
\varepsilon\leqslant\varepsilon_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
with

\varepsilon_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}
a sufficiently small constant, depending only on
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
.

Since
z_{0}\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(1)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
, by (
6.31
) we deduce that

(6.32)

\max\left\{|\eta_{m_{-}+{\sf g}_{0}+1}(z_{\star})|,|\eta_{m_{-}}(z_{\star})|^{-1}\right\}\leqslant 1-\varepsilon_{0}(1-\beta/2),

and

(6.33)

{1-\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}(1+\beta/2)}\leqslant|\eta_{m_{-}+1}(z_{\star})|=|\eta_{m_{-}+{\sf g}_{0}}(z_{\star})|\leqslant 1+\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}\beta/2,

where we also use that the roots can be partitioned into blocks, each of size
{\sf g}_{0}
, such that the roots in each of those block have the same moduli.

Now there are three possibilities: If
|\eta_{m_{-}+1}(z_{\star})|<1
, in which case
z_{\star}\in\mathcal{S}_{d}
, and therefore by (
6.32
)-(
6.33
) we have that

z_{\star}\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(1)}_{(1+\beta)\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}
. If
|\eta_{m_{-}+{\sf g}_{0}}(z_{\star})|>1
then
z_{\star}\in\mathcal{S}^{d-{\sf g}_{0}}
, and consequently
z_{\star}\in\mathcal{T}^{d-{\sf g}_{0},(2)}_{\beta\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime},(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}
.
Finally, if
|\eta_{m_{-}+1}(z_{\star})|=1
, by the maximum modulus principle, there exists a sequence
\{z_{n}\}_{n\in\mathds{N}}
such that
|z-z_{n}|\leqslant 1/n
and
|\eta_{m_{-}+1}(z_{n})|<1
for all
n\in\mathds{N}
. It is clear that
z_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{d}
, and by the continuity of the roots the bound in (
6.32
) and the lower bound in (
6.33
) holds for
z_{n}
, for all large
n
, with
\beta/2
replaced by
\beta
. This, in particular, shows that
z_{n}\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(1)}_{(1+\beta)\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}
for all large
n
. Therefore,
\mathcal{T}^{d,(1)}_{(1+\beta)\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}
being a closed set the limit
z_{\star}
must also be in
\mathcal{T}^{d,(1)}_{(1+\beta)\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}
. This completes the proof of
(
6.29
). The proof of (
6.30
) being similar, details are omitted.
∎

The following lemma is proved by a standard volumetric estimate, that we omit.

Lemma 6.10
.

For any
\upepsilon,\beta\in(0,1]
there exists a net of
p(S^{1})^{\upepsilon}
of mesh size
\beta\upepsilon
and of cardinality at most
O(\beta^{-2}\upepsilon^{-1})
.

We are now ready to provide the proof of Lemma
6.6
.

Proof of Lemma
6.6
.

Fix
d\geqslant 0
and
s\in\{1,2\}
. This fixes
\widehat{m}_{1}
and
\widehat{d}
(see (
5.12
) and (
5.33
)). Fix
\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}>0
such that
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}<\varepsilon_{0}
is sufficiently small and
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
. The precise choice of
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
will be specified later.
Set
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
and let
\beta\in(0,1/16)
.
Pick any
z\in\mathds{T}^{\beta}:=(\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})^{\beta\varepsilon}
.
From Lemmas
6.9
(i)-(ii) and
6.10
we deduce that there exist a finite collection
\{z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{b}\}\subset\mathds{T}^{\beta}
such that

(6.34)

\cup_{i=1}^{b}D(z_{i},\beta\varepsilon)\supset\mathds{T}^{\beta}

with

(6.35)

b=O(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime-2}).

Hence, it suffices to derive bounds on the derivative of
\widehat{\det}_{k}(\cdot)
on each
\mathds{D}_{i}^{\beta}:=D(z_{i},\beta\varepsilon)
for
i\in[b]
.

Turning to do that we let
\mathds{D}:=D(0,R)\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
, where
R:=2\max_{\eta\in S^{1}}|p(1/\eta)|
, and consider a smooth cutoff function
\upchi:\mathds{D}\mapsto\mathds{C}
such that

\upchi\equiv\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\mbox{ on }\mathds{D}_{i}^{2\beta},\\
0&\mbox{on }\mathds{D}\setminus\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}.\end{array}\right.

This implies that the derivative of
\upchi(\cdot)
is non-zero only on
\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}\setminus\mathds{D}_{i}^{2\beta}
. Since
\mathrm{dist\,}(\mathds{D}^{2\beta}_{i},\partial\mathds{D}^{3\beta}_{i})=\beta\varepsilon
one can choose
\upchi
such that the absolute value of its anti-holomorphic derivative
\partial_{\bar{w}}\upchi(w)
is at most
O(\beta^{-1}\varepsilon^{-1})
in
\mathds{D}
.
Further let
\Xi:\mathds{D}\mapsto\mathds{C}
be some (possibly random) holomorphic function. Then, applying Cauchy’s integral formula for the smooth function
z\mapsto\Xi(z)\cdot\upchi(z)
on the domain
\mathds{D}
(e.g. see
[
38
, Theorem 1.2.1]
) we obtain that for any
z\in\mathds{D}_{i}^{\beta}
,

\Xi(z)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}\setminus\mathds{D}_{i}^{2\beta}}\frac{\Xi(w)\cdot\partial_{\bar{w}}\upchi(w)}{w-z}dL(w),

where
L(\cdot)
is the two-dimensional Lebesgue measure. By the bounded convergence theorem we also have that

\frac{d}{dz}\Xi(z)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}\setminus\mathds{D}_{i}^{2\beta}}\frac{\Xi(w)\cdot\partial_{\bar{w}}\upchi(w)}{(w-z)^{2}}dL(w).

Now, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Fubini’s Theorem we further deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathds{D}_{i}^{\beta}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\Xi(z)\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{L(\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta})}{\pi^{2}\cdot\mathrm{dist\,}(\mathds{D}^{\beta}_{i},\partial\mathds{D}_{i}^{2\beta})^{4}}\cdot\int_{\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}\setminus\mathds{D}_{i}^{2\beta}}\mathbf{E}\left[|\Xi(w)|^{2}\right]\cdot\left|\partial_{\bar{w}}\upchi(w)\right|^{2}dL(w)

(6.36)

\displaystyle=O(\beta^{-2}\varepsilon^{-2})\cdot\sup_{w\in\mathds{D}^{3\beta}_{i}}\mathbf{E}\left[|\Xi(w)|^{2}\right].

To complete the proof of the lemma we now proceed to apply (
6.2
) with appropriate choices of
\Xi(\cdot)
and
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
.

We note that the roots of
p_{z}(\cdot)=0
are analytic in
z
for
z\in\mathds{D}
. Therefore, there exists a reordering of the indices of the roots
\{\eta_{j}(z)\}_{j\in[\widetilde{m}]}
, to be denoted by
\{\widehat{\eta}_{j}(z)\}_{j\in[\widetilde{m}]}
, such that the maps
z\mapsto\widehat{\eta}_{j}(z)
are holomorphic on
\mathds{D}
. We set

(6.37)

\Xi(z)=\Xi_{k}(z):=\frac{\det_{k}(z)}{a_{-N_{-}}^{N}N^{-\gamma\widehat{d}}\prod_{j=1}^{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}\widehat{\eta}_{j}(z)^{N}}.

We claim that the holomorphic functions
\{\widehat{\eta}_{j}(z)\}_{j\in[\widetilde{m}]}
can be chosen in such a way so that

(6.38)

\prod_{j=1}^{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}\widehat{\eta}_{j}(z)=\prod_{j=1}^{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}\eta_{j}(z)\qquad\mbox{ for }z\in\mathds{D}^{3\beta}_{i}.

Indeed, pick any
\widehat{z}\in\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}
. By the Implicit function theorem it is immediate that
\{\widehat{\eta}_{j}(z)\}_{j\in[\widetilde{m}]}
can be chosen such that (
6.38
) holds for
z=\widehat{z}
. Also, note that by Lemma
6.9
(ii) we have that

(6.39)

\mathds{D}^{3\beta}_{i}\subset\mathds{T}^{4\beta}\subset\left(\mathcal{T}_{(1+4\beta)\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},(1-4\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(s)}\cup\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime},(1-4\beta)\varepsilon_{0}}^{d+(-1)^{s}{\sf g}_{0},(3-s)}\right)\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2}.

The inclusion (
6.39
) and
\beta<1/16
imply
that there are no roots of
p_{z}(\cdot)=0
in
\mathds{D}^{3\beta}_{i}

with moduli between
1+2\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
and
1+\varepsilon_{0}/2

(choose
\varepsilon_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
such that
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/\varepsilon_{0}<1/8
).
Hence, (
6.38
) must continue to hold for all
z
in the connected domain
\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}
, for otherwise the image of the continuous map

|\widehat{\eta}_{j}(z)|
from
\mathds{D}^{3\beta}_{i}
to
\mathds{R}_{+}
would be disconnected for
some
j
.
It further follows from (
6.39
) that
\widehat{d}(\cdot)\equiv\widehat{d}
and
\widehat{m}_{1}(\cdot)\equiv\widehat{m}_{1}
on
\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}
.
This means that
\widehat{\det}_{k}(\cdot)\equiv\Xi_{k}(\cdot)
on
\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}
(recall (
5.32
)-(
5.35
)). Since this holds for every
i\in[b]
we use (
6.34
) and (
6.39
) to deduce from (
6.2
) that

(6.40)

\mathbf{E}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathds{T}^{\beta}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)\right|^{2}\right]=O(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime})^{4})\cdot\sup_{w\in\mathds{T}^{4\beta}}\mathbf{E}\left[|\widehat{\det}_{k}(w)|^{2}\right].

To complete the proofs of (
6.17
)-(
6.18
) it remains to find an upper bound on the
rhs
of (
6.40
).

We first
consider the proof of (
6.18
). Set
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime}-1)\log N/N
. With this choice of
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
, as
\beta\leqslant 1/16
, we obtain from (
6.39
) that
\mathds{T}^{4\beta}\subset\mathcal{T}^{d,(2)}_{2\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}/2}\cup\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{d+{\sf g}_{0},(1)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}/2}
. Apply
Lemmas
6.1
,
6.2
(i) and

6.2
(iii) with
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
replaced by

2\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
and
\varepsilon_{0}/2
to derive that

(6.41)

\sup_{w\in\mathds{T}^{4\beta}}\mathbf{E}\left[|\widehat{\det}_{k}(z)|^{2}\right]=O\left(1\right).

Plugging this bound in (
6.40
) we obtain (
6.18
).

The proof of (
6.17
) is similar. Set

\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}=\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime}-1)\log N/N
and choose
\beta
such that
(1+3\beta)\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime\prime}-1)\log N/N
for some
\gamma^{\prime\prime}<\gamma
. Apply Lemmas
6.1
,
6.2
(i) and
6.2
(iii) with
\gamma^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime},
and
\varepsilon_{0}/2
instead of
\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},
and
\varepsilon_{0}
, respectively to deduce that (
6.41
) continues to hold for
\mathds{T}^{\beta}=(\mathcal{T}^{d,(1)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})^{\beta\varepsilon}
. The desired bound now follows from (
6.40
).
∎

We end this section with the proof of Lemma
6.7
.

Proof of Lemma
6.7
.

We first prove
part (a). The proof is a direct consequence of Widom’s formula for the determinant of a finitely banded Toeplitz matrix. Indeed, by
[
15
, Theorem 2.8]
, for
s\in\{1,2\}
, we have, recalling that
\widetilde{N}=N_{+}+N_{-}
, that

\widehat{\det}_{0}(z)=N^{\gamma(\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+})}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}\eta_{j}(z)^{-N}\cdot(-1)^{NN_{-}}\cdot\left[\sum_{\mathcal{I}\in\binom{[\widetilde{N}]}{N_{-}}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{I}}(z)\cdot\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\eta_{i}(z)^{N}\right],

where for any
\mathcal{I}\subset[\widetilde{N}]

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{I}}(z):=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}\in\mathcal{I}\\
j_{2}\in[\widetilde{N}]\setminus\mathcal{I}\end{subarray}}\frac{\eta_{j_{1}}(z)}{\eta_{j_{1}}(z)-\eta_{j_{2}}(z)}.

Observe that that
\inf_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}}|\eta_{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}(z)|\geqslant 1/2
. Thus, now by the continuity of the maps
z\mapsto\eta_{j}(z)
, for
j\in[\widetilde{m}]
, as
\mathcal{T}^{d}
is a bounded set and we work off
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
, it follows that

(6.42)

0<\inf_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}|\mathcal{C}_{[\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}]}(z)|\leqslant\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}|\mathcal{C}_{[\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}]}(z)|\leqslant\max_{\mathcal{I}\subset[N_{+}+N_{-}]}\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}|\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{I}}(z)|<\infty.

On the other hand, recalling the definition of the tube
\mathcal{T}^{d}
,
it is evident that

(6.43)

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}}\left\{\max_{{j_{1}\in[\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}],\,j_{2}\notin[\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}]}}\left(\left|\frac{\eta_{j_{2}}(z)}{\eta_{j_{1}}(z)}\right|\vee|\eta_{j_{2}}(z)|\right)\right\}\leqslant\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{3}\right).

Notice that, for
s\in\{1,2\}

(6.44)

\widehat{d}>0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}>0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}<N_{-}.

Therefore, as
\widehat{d}>0
, we have that
\mathcal{I}\setminus[\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}]\neq\emptyset
for any set
\mathcal{I}\subset[\widetilde{N}]
of cardinality
N_{-}
.
Thus, (
6.43
) now implies that

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}}\left|\prod_{j=1}^{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}\eta_{j}(z)^{-N}\cdot\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\eta_{i}(z)^{N}\right|\leqslant\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{3}\right)^{N},

for all such subsets
\mathcal{I}
. Using this together with (
6.42
) we now have part (a).

Turning to the proof of
part (b), we recall (
5.8
) to observe that
P_{\widehat{N}_{+}}(p,z;\widetilde{N})[X_{\star}^{c};Y_{\star}^{c}+N_{+}]
is a
(\widehat{N}_{+}-\widehat{m}_{2})
-dimensional Toeplitz matrix with symbol

p_{\star}(\tau):=\tau^{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}\left[\sum_{j=-N_{-}}^{N_{+}}a_{j}\tau^{j}-z\right].

Notice that the roots of
p_{\star}(1/\cdot)
coincide with those of

p_{z}(\cdot)
. Therefore, again by
[
15
, Theorem 2.5]
, using that
\widetilde{N}=\widehat{m}_{1}+\widehat{m}_{2}+{\sf g}_{0}
, from (
5.10
) and (
5.36
) we deduce that

(6.45)

{\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}(X_{\star},Y_{\star},z)}=(-1)^{\widehat{N}(\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0})}a_{-N_{-}}^{-(\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+})}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}\eta_{j}(z)^{-(\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+})}\sum_{\mathcal{I}\in\binom{[\widetilde{N}]}{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{I}}(z)\cdot\frac{\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\eta_{i}(z)^{\widehat{N}}}{\prod_{j=1}^{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}\eta_{j}(z)^{\widehat{N}}},

where we use the shorthand
\widehat{N}:=\widehat{N}_{+}-\widehat{m}_{2}\,=N+N_{+}-\widehat{m}_{2}
.

Upon using (
6.42
) one finds that the summand in the
rhs
of (
6.45
) for
\mathcal{I}=[\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}]
is uniformly bounded below by
3c_{\star}
for some constant
c_{\star}>0
. On the other hand,
for
\mathcal{I}\neq[\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}]
,
using (
6.42
)-(
6.43
) we deduce that it is exponentially small (in
\widehat{N}
and hence in
N
) compared to
c_{\star}
. Therefore, the sum in (
6.45
) is uniformly bounded below by
2c_{\star}
. Using
now that
\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}}\max_{j\in[\widetilde{m}]}|\eta_{j}(z)|<\infty
,
and shrinking
c_{\star}
if needed, complete
the proof of the lower bound in (
6.20
) completes. The proof of the upper bound is immediate from (
6.45
), the above discussion, and the fact that
\inf_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}}|\eta_{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}(z)|\geqslant 1/2
. We omit further details.
∎

6.3.
Step 3: uniform lower bound on the dominant term

In this section we prove a uniform lower bound on the dominant term in the expansion of
\det(P_{z}^{\delta})
. The following is the main result of this section.

Theorem 6.11
.

Fix
\varepsilon_{0},\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
.
Assume that
\widehat{d}>0
and that the
entries of
Q
satisfy Assumption
1.2
.
Then, there exists

C_{\ref {thm:dom-lbd}}=C_{\ref {thm:dom-lbd}}(p,\upeta,\varepsilon_{0},\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0})<\infty
so that for all
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
sufficiently small,

\mathbf{P}\left(\inf_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}|\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}^{(s)}(z)|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{3\upeta/4}\right)\leqslant C_{\ref {thm:dom-lbd}}(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{\upeta/3},\quad s\in\{1,2\}.

The proof of
Theorem
6.11
uses
the following anti-concentration bound for certain polynomials in
independent random variables such that the degree of each of those random variables in those polynomials is at most one. This is a generalization of
[
9
, Proposition 4.1]
.

Lemma 6.12
.

Fix
k\in\mathds{N}
and let
\{U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
be a sequence of
independent complex-valued random variables, whose Lévy concentration functions satisfy the bound (
1.4
) with
\upeta\in(0,1]
.
Let
\{Z_{\mathcal{I}};\mathcal{I}\in\binom{[n]}{k}\}
be another collection of random variables which is jointly independent of the collection
\{U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
. Define

\mathcal{U}_{k}:=\sum_{\mathcal{I}\subset[k]}Z_{\mathcal{I}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}U_{i}.

Then, for any constant
c_{\star}>0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,c_{\star}{e^{-1}}]
we
have that

\mathbf{P}\left(|\mathcal{U}_{k}|\leqslant\varepsilon\right)\leqslant\bar{C}_{\ref {lem:anti-conc}}\cdot\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{c_{\star}}\right)^{(1+\upeta)}\cdot\left(\log\left(\frac{c_{\star}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)^{k-1}+\mathbf{P}\left(|Z_{[k]}|\leqslant c_{\star}\right),

where

\bar{C}_{\ref {lem:anti-conc}}<\infty
is some large constant
depending only on
\upeta
and
C_{\ref {assump:anticonc}}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.12
.

We will show that

\displaystyle\,\mathbf{P}\left(|\mathcal{U}_{k}|\leqslant\varepsilon\big|Z_{[k]}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{|Z_{[k]}|>c_{\star}\}}

(6.46)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\,\mathbf{P}\left(|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{k}|\leqslant c_{\star}^{-1}\varepsilon\big|Z_{[k]}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}_{\{|Z_{[k]}|>c_{\star}\}}\leqslant\bar{C}_{\ref {lem:anti-conc}}\cdot\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{c_{\star}}\right)^{(1+\upeta)}\left(\log\left(\frac{c_{\star}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)^{k-1},

where

\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{k}:=\sum_{\mathcal{I}\subset[k]}\widehat{Z}_{\mathcal{I}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}U_{i}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\widehat{Z}_{\mathcal{I}}:=\frac{Z_{\mathcal{I}}}{Z_{[k]}},\text{ for }\mathcal{I}\subset[k].

(Notice that
\{\widehat{Z}_{\mathcal{I}};\mathcal{I}\subset[k]\}
are well defined on the event
\{|Z_{[k]}|>c_{\star}\}
, and hence so is
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{k}
.)
Taking the expectation over
Z_{[k]}
in (
6.3
) together with a union bound immediately yields Lemma
6.12
. The first inequality in (
6.3
) is straightforward from the definition of
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{k}
. So, we only need to prove the second inequality in (
6.3
).

Turning to that task, for
j\in[k]
, set
\mathcal{J}_{j}:=\{j,j+1,\ldots,k\}
. Define

\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j}:=\sum_{\mathcal{I}:\,\mathcal{I}\supset\mathcal{J}_{j+1}}\widehat{Z}_{\mathcal{I}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{j+1}}U_{i}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{j}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{I}:\,\mathcal{I}\supset\mathcal{J}_{j+2}\\
(j+1)\notin\mathcal{I}\end{subarray}}\widehat{Z}_{\mathcal{I}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}\setminus\mathcal{J}_{j+2}}U_{i},\quad\mbox{ for }j\in[k-1]\cup\{0\},

where
\mathcal{J}_{k+1}=\emptyset
. We will prove inductively that, for any
j\in[k]
, and all
t\in(0,e^{-1}],

(6.47)

\mathbf{P}\left(|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j}|\leqslant t\big|Z_{[k]}\right)\leqslant{(6(C_{\ref {eq:levy-bd}}\vee 1)e^{1+\upeta})}^{j}t^{1+\upeta}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{t}\right)\right)^{j-2}.

Plugging
t=c_{\star}^{-1}\varepsilon
and
j=k
in (
6.47
) yields (
6.3
).

Proceeding to the proof of (
6.47
), we start with
j=1
. As
\widehat{Z}_{[k]}=1
we have that

\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{1}=U_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{0}.

Thus, as
U_{1}
is independent of
\{Z_{\mathcal{I}},\mathcal{I}\subset[k]\}
, by (
1.4
) we have that

\mathbf{P}\left(|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{1}|\leqslant t\big|Z_{[k]}\right)\leqslant\mathcal{L}(U_{1},t)\leqslant C_{\ref {assump:anticonc}}t^{1+\upeta}.

To prove (
6.47
) for an arbitrary
j\in[k]
, upon using induction we note that

(6.48)

\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j}=U_{j}\cdot\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j-1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{j-1},\qquad\text{ for }j\in[k].

Observe that by our assumption the random variables
\{\mathcal{U}_{j-1},\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{j-1}\}
are independent of
U_{j}
for any
j\in[k]
. This, in particular, allows us to use the anti-concentration property of
U_{j}
to derive the same for
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j}
.

To complete the proof of (
6.47
) by induction, assume it holds for
j=j_{*}
, and set
C_{j}={(6(C_{\ref {eq:levy-bd}}\vee 1)e^{1+\upeta})}^{j}
. The induction hypothesis yields that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}+1}\right|\leqslant t\big|Z_{[k]}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}\right|\leqslant t\big|Z_{[k]}\right)+\mathbf{E}\left[\mathbf{P}\left(\left|U_{{j_{*}}}+\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}}{\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}}\right|\leqslant\frac{t}{|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}|}\bigg|\,\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}},\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}},Z_{[k]}\right)\cdot{\bm{1}}\left(\left|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}\right|\geqslant t\right)\bigg|Z_{[k]}\right]

(6.49)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:levy-bd}}}{{\leqslant}}C_{j_{*}}t^{1+\upeta}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{t}\right)\right)^{j_{*}-2}+C_{\ref {assump:anticonc}}{t^{1+\upeta}}\cdot\mathbf{E}\left[|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}|^{-(1+\upeta)}{\bm{1}}\left(\left|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}\right|\geqslant t\right)\big|Z_{[k]}\right],

where we have used that the triplet
\{\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}},\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}},Z_{[k]}\}
is independent of
U_{{j_{*}}}
.
Using integration by parts, for any probability measure
\mu
supported on
[0,\infty)
we have that

\int_{t}^{{e^{-1}}}x^{-(1+\upeta)}d\mu(x)={e^{1+\upeta}}\mu([t,1])+(1+\upeta)\cdot\int_{t}^{{e^{-1}}}\frac{\mu([t,s])}{s^{2+\upeta}}ds.

Therefore, using the induction hypothesis and the fact that
\upeta\in(0,1]
, we have that

\mathbf{E}\left[|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}|^{-(1+\upeta)}{\bm{1}}\left(\left|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}\right|\geqslant t\right)\big|Z_{[k]}\right]\leqslant{e^{1+\upeta}}+\mathbf{E}\left[|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}|^{-(1+\upeta)}{\bm{1}}\left(\left|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}\right|\in[t,{e^{-1}}]\right)\big|Z_{[k]}\right]\\
\leqslant 2{e^{1+\upeta}}+2\int_{t}^{{e^{-1}}}\frac{\mathbf{P}\left(|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j_{*}}|\leqslant s|Z_{[k]}\right)}{s^{2+\eta}}ds\leqslant 2{e^{1+\upeta}}+2C_{j_{*}}\int_{t}^{{e^{-1}}}s^{-1}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)^{j_{*}-2}ds\\
\leqslant 2{e}^{1+\upeta}+\frac{2C_{j_{*}}}{(j_{*}-1)\vee 1}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{t}\right)\right)^{j_{*}-1}.

Combining the above with (
6.3
) and using that
\log(1/t)\geqslant 1
for
t\leqslant e^{-1}
,
we establish (
6.47
) for
j=j_{*}+1
. This concludes the induction argument and hence the proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

Lemmas
6.7
(b) and
6.12
yield easily an anti-concentration bound for
\det_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(\cdot)
per fixed
z
, as follows.

Corollary 6.13
.

Consider the same setup as in Theorem
6.11
. Then for
s\in\{1,2\}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,c_{\star}{e^{-1}}]
we have

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\mathbf{P}\left(|\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z)|\leqslant\varepsilon\right)\leqslant\bar{C}_{\ref {lem:anti-conc}}\cdot\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{c_{\star}}\right)^{(1+\upeta)}\cdot\left(\log\left(\frac{c_{\star}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)^{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}-1},

where
c_{\star}
is as in Lemma
6.7
(b).

Proof.

Recall (
5.9
), and (
5.32
)-(
5.36
). Fix
s\in\{1,2\}
. We note that

(6.50)

\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z)=(-1)^{\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma_{X_{\star}})\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma_{Y_{\star}})}\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}(X_{\star},Y_{\star},z)\cdot\det(Q[X_{\star};Y_{\star}])\\
+\sum(-1)^{\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma_{X})\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma_{Y})}\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}(X,Y,z)\cdot\det(Q[X;Y]),

where
X_{\star}
and
Y_{\star}
are as in Lemma
6.7
(b), and the sum in the
rhs
of (
6.50
) is over subsets
X,Y\subset[N]
such that
|X|=|Y|=\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}
and
(X,Y)\neq(X_{\star},Y_{\star})
. It is easy to see to that
\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z)
is a homogeneous polynomial in the entries of
Q
, which are jointly independent, such that the degree of each of its entry in that polynomial is at most one. Hence, Lemma
6.12
is applicable. By Lemma
6.7
(b) we have uniform lower bound on
\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}(X_{\star},Y_{\star},z)
. Therefore, upon applying Lemma
6.12
with
k=\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}
and the roles of
\{U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
being played by the diagonal entries of the sub matrix
Q[X_{\star},Y_{\star}]
,
we derive the desired anti-concentration bound.
∎

Corollary
6.13
is not sufficient to yield Theorem
6.11
. As discussed in Section
6.2
, we also need a bound on the supremum of the derivative of
{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z)
. This is derived below.

Lemma 6.14
.

Let Assumption
1.1
hold. Fix
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
. There exists some constant
c_{\ref {lem:der-bd-dom}}>0
, depending only on
p,\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
, such that for all

\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}>0
such that
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/\varepsilon_{0}
is small enough,

(6.51)

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{E}\left[\sup_{z\in\left(\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd-dom}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z)\right|^{2}\right]=O(1),\quad s\in\{1,2\}.

Proof.

We borrow ingredients from the proof of Lemma
6.6
. Let
s=2
. The proof for
s=1
being the same will be omitted.

Our starting point is (
6.40
). Fix
k=\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}
. As in the proof of Lemma
6.6
we set
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
and fix
\beta\in(0,1/8)
. Set
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
. By (
6.39
)
\mathds{T}^{4\beta}
is contained in the union of
\mathcal{T}^{d,(2)}_{2\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}/2}
and
\mathcal{T}^{d+{\sf g}_{0},(1)}_{2\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}/2}
. Apply Lemma
6.2
(ii) for
\mathcal{T}^{d,(2)}
and
\mathcal{T}^{d+{\sf g}_{0},(1)}
with
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
being replaced by
2\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
, and
\varepsilon_{0}/2
, respectively to deduce that (
6.41
) holds under the current setup. Therefore, the desired bound follows from (
6.40
). This finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.15
.

From (
6.40
)-(
6.41
) we have that the
rhs
of (
6.51
) is
O((\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{-3})
. On the other hand Lemma
6.2
(ii), and hence (
6.41
), hold as soon as
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/\varepsilon_{0}\leqslant c
where
c>0
is some constant depending only on the degree of the Laurent polynomial. This allows us to use Theorem
6.14
for
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}=c\varepsilon_{0}
and extend the result for
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}<c\varepsilon_{0}
, by noting that the sets
(\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})^{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd-dom}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}}
are increasing in
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
. It in particular enables us to claim that the
rhs
of (
6.51
) is
O(\varepsilon_{0}^{-3})
. This observation will be used in the proof of Theorem
6.11
to claim that the constant
C_{\ref {thm:dom-lbd}}
does not depend on
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
6.11
.

Proof of Theorem
6.11
.

Fix
s\in\{1,2\}
. Similar to the proof of Theorem
6.5
we will also use a covering argument.

Write

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(z):=\left\{|\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z)|\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}\varepsilon_{\star}^{-1}\right\}

and

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{0}:=\left\{\sup_{z\in\left(\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd-dom}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z)\right|\geqslant\varepsilon_{\star}^{-1}\right\},

where
\varepsilon_{\star}=(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{\upeta/4}
. By Lemmas
6.9
(i) and
6.10
there exists a net
\mathcal{N}_{\star}
of
\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
with mesh size
{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd-dom}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}}/2
such that
|\mathcal{N}_{\star}|=O(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime-1})
. Therefore, applying Corollary
6.13
and Lemma
6.14
(see also Remark
6.15
) we have that

(6.52)

\mathbf{P}\left(\cup_{z\in\mathcal{N}_{\star}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(z)\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\right)=O\left((\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{\upeta/3}\right),

for any
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small. On the other hand, for any
z\in\mathcal{N}_{\star}
we have that
D(z,c_{\ref {lem:der-bd-dom}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/2)\subset(\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})^{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd-dom}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/2}
. Therefore, by the first order Taylor series expansion and triangle inequality it follows that on the event
\cap_{z\in\mathcal{N}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(z)^{c}\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{0}^{c}
, for any
z^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(s)}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}

\displaystyle|\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\geqslant\inf_{z\in\mathcal{N}_{\star}}|\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z)|-\mathrm{dist\,}(z^{\prime},\mathcal{N}_{\star})\cdot\sup_{z\in\left(\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{c_{\ref {lem:der-bd-dom}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\widehat{\det}_{\widehat{m}_{2}-N_{+}}(z)\right|

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{\star}^{-1}=\frac{1}{2}(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{3\upeta/4}.

This together with (
6.52
) completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

6.4.
Step 4: combining bounds from Sections
6.1
-
6.3

In this short section we combine the results obtained in the previous three sections and complete the proofs of Theorems
5.4
and
5.7
. We start with the proof of Theorem
5.4
. The estimates derived Sections
6.1
-
6.3
are for the tubes which are defined through the roots
p_{z}(\cdot)=0
, while Theorem
5.4
is to be proved for regions that are defined via the spectral parameter
z
. Therefore, we will need to prove some additional geometric features of the tubes which will allow us to relate those to regions defined through
z
. These will also be used later in Section
7
. Stating these results
requires the following notation.

For any
\upepsilon>0
and set
\mathcal{B}\subset\mathds{C}
we write
\mathcal{B}^{-\upepsilon}:=((\mathcal{B}^{c})^{\upepsilon})^{c}
.
For any collection of
({\sf g}_{0}+1)
distinct indices
{\bm{i}}:=\{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{{\sf g}_{0}+1}\}\subset[\widetilde{m}]
and
c>0
we set

\mathcal{W}_{\bm{i}}(c):=\left\{z\in\mathds{C}:||\eta_{i_{j}}(z)|-1|+||\eta_{i_{j}^{\prime}}(z)|-1|\leqslant c,\mbox{ for all }j\neq j^{\prime}\in[{\sf g}_{0}+1]\right\}.

and

\mathcal{W}(c):=\left\{z:\exists\,\eta=\eta(z)\text{ such that }p_{z}(-\eta)=0\text{ and }||\eta|-1|\leqslant c\right\}.

Lemma 6.16
.

Fix
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
. We have the following geometric properties:

(i)

There exists a constant
C_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}<\infty
such that for any
\upepsilon>0
,

(p(S^{1}))^{\upepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\subset\mathcal{W}(C_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}\upepsilon)\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}.

(ii)

There exists a constant
c_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}>0
such that for any
c\leqslant c_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}
,

(6.53)

\left(\bigcup_{{\bm{i}}\subset[\widetilde{m}]:|{\bm{i}}|={\sf g}_{0}+1}\mathcal{W}_{{\bm{i}}}(c\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0})\right)\cap(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})^{c}=\emptyset.

(iii)

Fix
0<\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
such that
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}+\varepsilon_{0}\leqslant c_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
. Then, for any
\upepsilon>0
such that
\upepsilon\leqslant C_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
,

(p(S^{1}))^{\upepsilon}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\subset\left(\bigcup_{d}\mathcal{T}^{d}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\right)\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}).

(iv)

Let
\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
, and
\upepsilon
be as in part (iii). Then,

(6.54)

(\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\upepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\subset(\mathcal{T}^{{\sf g}_{0},(1)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\cup\mathcal{T}^{-{\sf g}_{0},(1)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}).

(v)

Let
\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
, and
\upepsilon
be as in part (iii). Then,

({\mathcal{S}}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{0}^{-\upepsilon})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\subset\mathcal{T}^{0,(2)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}.

Proof.

The proof of part (i) is immediate. Fix
z^{\prime}\in(p(S^{1}))^{\upepsilon}
. Then there exists
\widehat{z}\in p(S^{1})
such that
|z^{\prime}-\widehat{z}|\leqslant\upepsilon
. This, in particular implies that there exists
i\in[\widetilde{m}]
such that
\eta_{i}(\widehat{z})\in S^{1}
. Therefore, by the triangle inequality,

(6.55)

||\eta_{i}(z^{\prime})|-1|\leqslant|\eta_{i}(z^{\prime})-\eta_{i}(\widehat{z})|\leqslant\max_{i\in[\widetilde{m}]}\sup_{z\in(p(S^{1}))^{2\upepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\left|\frac{d}{dz}\eta_{i}(z)\right|\cdot|z^{\prime}-\widehat{z}|.

Since
p(S^{1})
is a bounded set, and the roots are analytic outside a neighborhood of
\mathcal{B}_{2}
we have that the supremum of the derivative of the roots is bounded
(p(S^{1}))^{2\upepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
. This yields part (i).

Next we turn to prove (ii). Let us first prove (
6.53
) for Laurent polynomials
p(\cdot)
such that
{\sf g}(p)={\sf g}_{0}=1
. To this end, fix an arbitrary constant
c>0
. Assume that (
6.53
) does not hold for this chosen
c
. Then there exist a
z\notin\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
and a pair
i\neq j\in[\widetilde{m}]
such that

||\eta_{i}(z)|-1|+|\eta_{j}(z)|-1|\leqslant c\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}.

Therefore, if (
6.53
) does not hold for any
c>0
then we obtain a pair
i\neq j\in[\widetilde{m}]
, a sequence
\{z_{n}\}
, and a subsequence of integers
\{n_{k}\}
such that

(6.56)

||\eta_{i}(z_{n_{k}})|-1|+||\eta_{j}(z_{n_{k}})|-1|\leqslant\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/n_{k}\quad\text{ and }\quad z_{n_{k}}\in\mathds{C}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}),\qquad\forall\,k.

Since for each of these
z_{n_{k}}
there exists a root of
p_{z_{n_{k}}}(\cdot)=0
that is close to the unit circle,
it follows that
\{z_{n_{k}}\}
is a bounded sequence
(see also the proof of Lemma
6.9
(i)), which
thus possesses
a converging subsequence, and hence may be assumed to
converge to some
z_{\star}\notin\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
. Now, by the continuity of the maps
z\mapsto\eta_{i}(z)
and
z\mapsto\eta_{j}(z)
we derive from (
6.56
) that
|\eta_{i}(z_{\star})|=|\eta_{j}(z_{\star})|=1
. As
z_{\star}\notin\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
we further deduce that
\eta_{i}(z_{\star})\neq\eta_{j}(z_{\star})
. This, in particular, implies that there exist
\eta\neq\eta^{\prime}\in S^{1}
such that
p(-\eta)=p(-\eta^{\prime})=z_{\star}
, and hence
z_{\star}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}
. This is a contradiction. Therefore, we have (
6.53
) for
{\sf g}_{0}=1
.

To prove (
6.53
) for a Laurent polynomial
p(\cdot)
with
{\sf g}(p)={\sf g}_{0}>1
we note that for such
p(\cdot)
there exists a Laurent polynomial
\widehat{p}(\cdot)
, with
{\sf g}(\widehat{p})=1
, such that
p(\eta)=\widehat{p}(\eta^{{\sf g}_{0}})
for
\eta\in\mathds{C}
. This means that the roots of
p_{z}(\cdot)=0
are obtained by taking the
{\sf g}_{0}
-th roots of those of
\widehat{p}_{z}(\cdot)=0
. Furthermore, the set of intersection points and branch points of
p(\cdot)
and
\widehat{p}(\cdot)
are identical. Since (
6.53
) holds for
\widehat{p}(\cdot)
, upon shrinking
c_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}
it continues to hold for
p(\cdot)
.

The proof of (iii) is also straightforward. Indeed, by parts (i) and (ii) we have

(6.57)

(p(S^{1}))^{C_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\subset\mathcal{W}(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})=\mathcal{W}(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})\cap\left(\bigcup_{{\bm{i}}\subset[\widetilde{m}]:|{\bm{i}}|={\sf g}_{0}+1}\mathcal{W}_{{\bm{i}}}(c_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0})\right)^{c}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})

Note that by the definition of
\mathcal{W}(\cdot)
it follows that any
z
belonging to rightmost set in (
6.57
) must have a root
-\eta(z)
of
p_{z}(\cdot)=0
such that
||\eta(z)|-1|\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
. As
{\sf g}(p)={\sf g}_{0}
there are
{\sf g}_{0}
such roots. Recalling the definition of
\mathcal{W}_{{\bm{i}}}(\cdot)
we deduce that distance of all other roots from
S^{1}
must be at least
\varepsilon_{0}
. Hence,
z\in\mathcal{T}^{d}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
for some
d
. This yields part (iii).

Turning to prove part (iv) we begin with the proof of the following intermediate result:

(6.58)

(\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\upepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\subset(p(S^{1}))^{\upepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{0})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}).

for any
\upepsilon<\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2
.
To see (
6.58
),
we note that given any
z
belonging to the set on the
lhs
of (
6.58
) there exists
z_{\star}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
such that
|z-z_{\star}|\leqslant\upepsilon
. Define
z_{\star}(t):=z_{\star}(1-t)+tz
,
t\in[0,1]
. We claim that there exists
t_{\star}\in(0,1]
such that
z_{\star}(t_{\star})\in p(S^{1})
. This will indeed show that
z\in(p(S^{1}))^{\upepsilon}
.

To prove the existence of
t_{\star}
, as
z_{\star}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
, we see that,
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z_{\star})|>1>|\eta_{N_{-}+1}(z_{\star})|
. As
z\notin{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
, we have either
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z)|\leqslant 1
or
|\eta_{N_{-}+1}(z)|\geqslant 1
. Assume
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z)|\leqslant 1
. If an equality holds then we set
t_{\star}=1
. So, assume further
that
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z)|<1
.
Since the map
z_{0}\mapsto|\eta_{N_{-}}(z_{0})|
is well defined and continuous for
z_{0}\notin\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2}
, the existence of
t_{\star}\in(0,1)
such that
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z_{\star}(t_{\star}))|=1
, and hence
z_{\star}(t_{\star})\in p(S^{1})
, is immediate from the intermediate value theorem. Similarly for the case
|\eta_{N_{-}+1}(z)|\geqslant 1
one can repeat the above argument to find
t_{\star}\in(0,1]
such that
|\eta_{N_{-}+1}(z_{\star}(t_{\star}))|=1
implying again
z_{\star}(t_{\star})\in p(S^{1})
. This yields (
6.58
).

Returning to the proof of (
6.54
) we apply part (iii) to deduce that for any
\upepsilon>0
such that
\upepsilon\leqslant C_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
we have

(\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\upepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\subset\left(\cup_{d}\mathcal{T}^{d}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\right)\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}).

Thus, upon noting that as
{\sf g}(p)={\sf g}_{0}
the winding number should be a multiple of
{\sf g}_{0}
, it suffices to show that

(6.59)

(\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\upepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0})\bigcap\left(\cup_{d:|d|\geqslant 2{\sf g}_{0}}\mathcal{T}^{d}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\cup\mathcal{T}^{\pm{\sf g}_{0},(2)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\right)=\emptyset.

Assume that
there exists
z\in(\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\upepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0})\cap\mathcal{T}^{d}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
for some
d
such that
|d|\geqslant 2{\sf g}_{0}
. Again we need to split to the cases
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z)|\leqslant 1
and
|\eta_{N_{-}+1}(z)|\geqslant 1
.
We first consider the case
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z)|\leqslant 1
.
Recall that

d=m_{+}-N_{+}=N_{-}-m_{-}
. By the definition of the tube
\mathcal{T}^{d}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
we note that
d\geqslant{2\sf g}_{0}
implies that
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z)|\leqslant 1-\varepsilon_{0}
. On the other hand,
d\leqslant-2{\sf g}_{0}
implies that
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z)|^{-1}\leqslant 1-\varepsilon_{0}
. However, we also recall from above that
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z)|\leqslant 1
implies that there exists
z_{\star}(t_{\star})\in p(S^{1})
such that
|\eta_{N_{-}}(z_{\star}(t_{\star}))|=1
and
|z-z_{\star}(t_{\star})|\leqslant\upepsilon
. As
\upepsilon\leqslant C_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
, and
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}/2
, this together with (
6.55
) indeed yields a contradiction. A similar argument works for the case
|\eta_{N_{-}+1}(z)|\geqslant 1
. One can repeat the same argument again to further show that
(\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\upepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0})\cap\mathcal{T}^{\pm{\sf g}_{0},(2)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}=\emptyset
. We omit the details.

Finally we proceed to prove part (v). Repeating an argument similar to the proof of (
6.58
) we obtain that

(6.60)

({\mathcal{S}}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{0}^{-\upepsilon})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\subset(p(S^{1}))^{\upepsilon}\cap\mathcal{S}_{0})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}).

Now by part (iii) the set on the
rhs
of (
6.60
) must be contained in the union of the tubes. However, the only tube that has nonempty intersection with
\mathcal{S}_{0}
is
\mathcal{T}^{0,(2)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
.
This yields part (v) and hence the proof of the lemma is now complete.
∎

We are ready to prove Theorem
5.4
.

Proof of Theorem
5.4
.

Fix
\gamma^{\prime}>1
such that
\gamma-1<\gamma^{\prime}<\gamma
. Set
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime}-1)\log N/N
,
\varepsilon_{0}=c_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2
, some arbitrary constant
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}>0
such that
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}/\varepsilon_{0}
sufficiently small,
\upepsilon_{1}=C_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
, and
\upepsilon_{2}=C_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}^{-1}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
. By Lemma
6.16
(iv)-(v) we note that

(\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\upepsilon_{1}}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\subset(\mathcal{T}^{{\sf g}_{0},(1)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\cup\mathcal{T}^{-{\sf g}_{0},(1)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})

and

({\mathcal{S}}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{0}^{-\upepsilon_{2}})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\subset\mathcal{T}^{0,(2)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}.

Therefore, by
[
9
, Theorem 1.1]
and a union bound we now derive that

(6.61)

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\in\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\upepsilon_{1}}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}):\det(P_{z}^{\delta})=0\right)\\
\leqslant\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\in(\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\upepsilon_{1}}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{0}^{-\upepsilon_{2}})\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}):\det(P_{z}^{\delta})=0\right)\\
\leqslant\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left[\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{\pm{\sf g}_{0},(1)}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}:\det(P_{z}^{\delta})=0\right)+\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\in\mathcal{T}^{0,(2)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}:\det(P_{z}^{\delta})=0\right)\right].

It remains to show that each of the terms in the
rhs
of (
6.61
) equals zero in the limit. For
d\in\{0,{\sf g}_{0}\}
, upon recalling (
5.6
), this follows from Theorem
6.5
, Lemma
6.7
(b) (see also Remark
6.8
), and the triangle inequality. To see that the same holds for
d=-{\sf g}_{0}
, as
P_{N}^{\sf T}
is a Toeplitz matrix with symbol
\widetilde{p}(\cdot)=p(1/\cdot)
and hence
{\rm ind}_{p(S^{1})}(\cdot)=-{\rm ind}_{\widetilde{p}(S^{1})}(\cdot)
, we apply Theorem
6.5
and Lemma
6.7
(c) for
(P_{z}^{\delta})^{\sf T}
.
This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

We end the section with the proof of Theorem
5.7
.

Proof of Theorem
5.7
.

The case
d=0
is covered by Theorem
5.4
. Consider the case
d>0
. Fix
s\in\{1,2\}
. Since
0<\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}<1
we observe that, for
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}=N^{-(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})/8}
,

\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(s)}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\subset\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(s)}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}},

for all large
N
. Therefore, applying Theorems
6.5
and
6.11
(with
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
replaced by
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}
), and upon using the triangle inequality it is immediate that

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}^{d,(s)}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}:\det(P_{z}^{\delta})=0\right)\leqslant 2N^{-\upeta(\gamma-\gamma^{\prime})/32},

for all large
N
, yielding the desired result for
d>0
. To prove the same for
d<0
, as
\det(P_{z}^{\delta})=\det((P_{z}^{\delta})^{\sf T})
and
P_{N}^{\sf T}
is a Toeplitz matrix with symbol
p(1/\cdot)
, we work with
(P_{z}^{\delta})^{\sf T}
and proceed same as above.
This finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.17
.

Theorem
5.7
and Lemma
6.16
(ii) imply,
upon recalling the definitions of the tube
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\gamma^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}^{d}
and
{\sf g}_{0}
, that with probability approaching one, for any eigenvalue
z\notin\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
one has that

p^{-1}(z)\cap(S^{1}+D(0,C_{\gamma}N^{-1}\log N))=\emptyset,

for some appropriately chosen
C_{\gamma}
such that
\lim_{\gamma\to 1}C_{\gamma}=0
and
\lim_{\gamma\to\infty}C_{\gamma}=\infty
.

7.
Location of the bulk of the eigenvalues

In this section we prove that away from the bad sets,
the bulk of the spectrum of
P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}
is contained in tubes of width
O(\log N/N)
around
p(S^{1})
, with high probability, see Theorem
7.1
. We also prove
in Theorem
7.4

an upper bound on the number of eigenvalues of
P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}
residing in the ball
D(x,\upalpha\log N/N)
, where the distance of
x
from the spectral curve is of the order
\log N/N
and
\upalpha<\infty
is some large constant. The latter estimate will we used in the proof of Corollary
1.7
.

Before stating the results, recall the bad sets of Definition
1.3
, and define for
\upepsilon,\upalpha,\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
,

(7.1)

\Omega_{\upepsilon}:=\{z\in\mathds{C}:{\rm dist}(z,p(S^{1}))>\upepsilon\},\quad{\mathcal{N}}_{\upalpha}:=\{z:z\in\Omega_{\upalpha\log N/N}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\;\mbox{an eigenvalue of
$P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}$}\}.

The first main result of this section is the following.

Theorem 7.1
.

Fix
\gamma>1
and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
. Let Assumptions
1.1
and
1.2
hold. Then
there exists a constant
C_{\ref {thm-thintube}}<\infty
, depending only on
p(\cdot)
,
\gamma
, and
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
, so that
for any
\upalpha>0
, we have

(7.2)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(|{\mathcal{N}}_{\upalpha}|\leqslant\frac{C_{\ref {thm-thintube}}N}{\upalpha}\right)=1.

To prove Theorem
7.1
we will use Jensen’s formula. For an analytic function
f
, let

n_{f}(x,r)
denote the number of roots in a ball of radius
r
around
x
.
If
f(x)\neq 0
, then we have

(7.3)

\int_{0}^{r}\frac{n_{f}(x,s)}{s}ds=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log|f(x+re^{i\theta})|d\theta-\log|f(x)|.

In particular, we obtain from (
7.3
) the bound

(7.4)

n_{f}(x,ur)\leqslant\frac{1}{2(1-u)\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log|f(x+re^{i\theta})|d\theta-\frac{1}{1-u}\log|f(x)|,

valid for
u\in(0,1)
.
We will apply (
7.4
) with
f(z)=\det(P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}-zI_{N})
.
Introduce the function

(7.5)

\phi_{\infty}(z):=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log|z-p(e^{i\theta})|d\theta.

To prove Theorem
7.1
we will need the following two lemmas. Recall that
P_{z}^{\delta}=P_{N,\gamma}^{Q}-zI_{N}
.

Lemma 7.2

(Upper bound on determinant)
.

Consider the setup as in Theorem
7.1
.
Then
there exist constants
0<c_{\ref {lem-detUB}},C_{\ref {lem-detUB}}<\infty
, depending only on
\gamma,\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
and
p(\cdot)
, such that

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{A}_{UB}^{\complement})\leqslant N^{-3},

for all large
N
, where

\mathcal{A}_{UB}:=\bigcap_{z\in\Omega_{1/N}\cap(p(S^{1}))^{c_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})}\left\{\log|\det(P_{z}^{\delta})|\leqslant N\phi_{\infty}(z)+C_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\log N\right\}.

We also need the following complementary lower bounds.

Lemma 7.3

(Lower bound on determinant)
.

Consider the setup of Theorem
7.1
. Then,
there exists a constant
C_{\ref {lem-detLB}}<\infty
depending only on
\gamma,\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
, and
p(\cdot)
, such that

(7.6)

\max_{z\in(p(S^{1}))^{c_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{A}_{z,LB}^{\complement})\leqslant 1/N^{3},

where

\mathcal{A}_{z,LB}:=\left\{\log|\det(P_{z}^{\delta})|\geqslant N\phi_{\infty}(z)-C_{\ref {lem-detLB}}\log N\right\}.

Using these two lemmas, whose proofs are postponed,
we now prove Theorem
7.1
.

Proof of Theorem
7.1
(assuming Lemmas
7.2
and
7.3
).

Let
R:=2\max_{z\in S^{1}}|p(z)|
. For
i\in\mathds{N}
such that

i\leqslant i_{\star}:=

\lceil\log(c_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}N/(4\upalpha\log N))/\log 2\rceil
, set

\Omega_{\upalpha,i}:=\bigg\{z\in D(0,R)\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}):\\
\left(\frac{3\upalpha}{4}+2^{i-3}\upalpha\right)\cdot\frac{\log N}{N}\leqslant\mathrm{dist\,}(z,p(S^{1}))\leqslant\left(\frac{3\upalpha}{4}+2^{i-2}\upalpha\right)\cdot\frac{\log N}{N}\bigg\},

and define
\mathcal{N}_{\upalpha,i}:=\mathcal{N}_{\upalpha}\cap\Omega_{\alpha,i}
. From our choice of
i_{\star}
it is clear that

(7.7)

\left(D(0,R)\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\right)\setminus\left(\cup_{i=1}^{i_{\star}}\Omega_{\upalpha,i}\right)\subset\left(D(0,R)\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}})\right)\cap\Omega_{c_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/32}.

By
[
9
, Theorem 1.1]
we have that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(|\mathcal{N}_{\upalpha}\cap D(0,R)^{\complement}|>0\right)\leqslant\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\exists z\notin D(0,R):z\mbox{ is an eigenvalue of }P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}\right)=0.

On the other hand, we have from

[
65
, Corollary 2.2]
or
[
8
, Theorem 1.2]
that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(|\mathcal{N}_{\upalpha}\cap\Omega_{c_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/32}|\geqslant\frac{C_{0}^{\prime}N}{2\upalpha}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\left\{z\in\Omega_{c_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/32}:z\mbox{ is an eigenvalue of }P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}\right\}\right|\geqslant\frac{C_{0}^{\prime}N}{2\upalpha}\right)=0,

for any
\upalpha>0
.
Hence, in light of (
7.7
) it suffices to show that

(7.8)

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(|\mathcal{N}_{\upalpha,i}|=O(2^{-i}\upalpha^{-1}N)\mbox{ for all }i\in[i_{\star}]\right)=1.

Turning to prove (
7.8
), we cover
\Omega_{\upalpha,i}
by a collection of balls

D_{i,j}:=D(z_{i,j},r_{i})
,
j\in N_{i}
,
with
z_{i,j}\in\Omega_{\upalpha,i}
and
r_{i}=2^{i-4}\upalpha\cdot(\log N/N)
. As
z_{i,j}\in\Omega_{\upalpha,i}
we have that
\mathrm{dist\,}(z_{i,j},\mathcal{B}_{1}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2})\geqslant\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
. For
i\leqslant i_{\star}
we also have that
r_{i}\leqslant r_{i_{\star}}\leqslant\widetilde{c}_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\varepsilon_{0}/32
.
Therefore, there exists an absolute constant
u\in(0,1)
, such that for any
i\leqslant i_{\star}
and
j\in[N_{i}]
,

\mathrm{dist\,}(D(z_{i,j},u^{-1}r_{i}),\mathcal{B}_{1}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2})\geqslant\widetilde{3}\varepsilon_{0}/4.

On the other hand, we note that

(3\upalpha/4)\cdot\log N/N\leqslant\mathrm{dist\,}(D(z_{i,j},u^{-1}r_{i}),p(S^{1}))\leqslant c_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/4.

The last two observations together imply that

(7.9)

\bigcup_{i=1}^{i_{\star}}\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{i}}D(z_{i,j},u^{-1}r_{i})\subset\Omega_{1/{(2N)}}\cap(p(S^{1}))^{c_{\ref {lem-detUB}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/4}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2}).

This allows us to apply Lemmas
7.2
and
7.3
. Now, using
(
7.4
), we have with

n_{i,j}:=|\{z\in D_{i,j}:\mbox{\rm$z$ is an eigenvalue of $P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}$}\}|

that

n_{i,j}\leqslant\frac{1}{2(1-u)\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log|\det(P^{\delta}-(z_{i,j}+u^{-1}r_{i,j}e^{i\theta})I_{N}|d\theta-\frac{1}{1-u}\log|\det(P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}-z_{i,j}I_{N})|,

where
u\in(0,1)
is as above. Let
\mathcal{A}_{UB}
be as in Lemma
7.2
with
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
replaced by
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2
. Then, on the event
\mathcal{A}_{UB}\cap\mathcal{A}_{z_{i,j},LB}
, as
\phi_{\infty}(z)
is harmonic off
p(S^{1})
, by (
7.9
) we have that

n_{i,j}\leqslant(1-u)^{-1}(C_{\ref {lem-detUB}}+C_{\ref {lem-detLB}})\log N.

By Lemma
6.10
it follows that
N_{i}
, the number of balls of radius
r_{i}
needed to cover

\Omega_{\upalpha,i}
satisfies the bound

(7.10)

N_{i}\leqslant C2^{-i}\cdot\upalpha^{-1}\cdot({N}/{\log N}),

for some universal constant
C<\infty
. Thus, on
\mathcal{A}_{\star}:=\mathcal{A}_{UB}\cap\cap_{i,j}\mathcal{A}_{z_{i,j},LB}
,

|\mathcal{N}_{\upalpha,i}|\leqslant\sum_{j\in N_{i}}n_{i,j}\leqslant(1-u)^{-1}(C_{\ref {lem-detUB}}+C_{\ref {lem-detLB}})CN\cdot{2^{-i}\upalpha^{-1}}.

Therefore (
7.8
) and hence the theorem follows if
we can prove
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{A}_{\star})=1-o(1)
. Note however that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{A}_{\star})

\displaystyle\geqslant 1-\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{A}_{UB}^{\complement})-\sum_{i=1}^{i_{\star}}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{i}}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{A}_{z_{i,j},LB}^{\complement})\geqslant 1-N^{-3}-N^{-3}\left(\sum_{i\leqslant i_{\star}}N_{i}\right)=1-o(1),

where we used (
7.10
) and Lemmas
7.2
and
7.3
with
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
replaced by
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2
.
∎

Using the same ideas, we next provide a local upper bound on the number of eigenvalues.

Theorem 7.4
.

Consider the setup as in Theorem
7.1
. Fix
0<\underline{\upalpha}<\overline{\upalpha}<\infty
.
Then, there exists a constant
C_{\ref {thm-thintube2}}<\infty
depending only on
\gamma,\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
, and
p(\cdot)
,
such that for any
z\in(p(S^{1}))^{\overline{\upalpha}\log N/N}
,

\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\mathcal{N}_{\underline{\upalpha},\overline{\upalpha},z}\right|\geqslant{C_{\ref {thm-thintube2}}\underline{\upalpha}^{-2}\overline{\upalpha}^{2}\cdot\log N}\right)\leqslant N^{-2},

for all large
N
, with

\mathcal{N}_{\underline{\upalpha},\overline{\upalpha},z}:=\left\{z^{\prime}\in D(z,\overline{\upalpha}\log N/N)\cap\Omega_{\underline{\upalpha}\log N/N}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}):z^{\prime}\mbox{ is an eigenvalue of }P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}\right\}.

Proof.

We follow the same strategy as in the proof of Theorem
7.1
. Fix
z\in(p(S^{1}))^{\overline{\upalpha}\log N/N}
. For
i\in[\overline{i}_{\star}]
, where
\overline{i}_{\star}:=\lceil\log(8\overline{\upalpha}/\underline{\upalpha})/\log 2\rceil
, we set

\overline{\Omega}_{\underline{\upalpha},\overline{\upalpha},i}:=\bigg\{z^{\prime}\in D(z,\overline{\upalpha}\log N/N)\cap\Omega_{\underline{\upalpha}\log N/N}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}):\\
\left(\frac{3\underline{\upalpha}}{4}+2^{i-3}\underline{\upalpha}\right)\cdot\frac{\log N}{N}\leqslant\mathrm{dist\,}(z^{\prime},p(S^{1}))\leqslant\left(\frac{3\underline{\upalpha}}{4}+2^{i-2}\underline{\upalpha}\right)\cdot\frac{\log N}{N}\bigg\}.

Since
\mathrm{dist\,}(z,p(S^{1}))\leqslant\overline{\upalpha}\log N/N
, we notice that

\bigcup_{i=1}^{\overline{i}_{\star}}\overline{\Omega}_{\underline{\upalpha},\overline{\upalpha},i}\supset D(z,\overline{\upalpha}\log N/N)\cap\Omega_{\underline{\upalpha}\log N/N}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}).

Thus, it suffices to bound the number of eigenvalues in
\cup_{i=1}^{\overline{i}_{\star}}\overline{\Omega}_{\underline{\upalpha},\overline{\upalpha},i}
.

As in the proof of Theorem
7.1
we cover
\overline{\Omega}_{\underline{\upalpha},\overline{\upalpha},i}
by a collection of balls
D_{i,j}=D(z_{i,j},\overline{r}_{i})
,
j\in\overline{N}_{i}
,
z_{i,j}\in\overline{\Omega}_{\underline{\upalpha},\overline{\upalpha},i}
and
\overline{r}_{i}=2^{i-4}\underline{\upalpha}\cdot(\log N/N)
. By our choice of
\overline{i}_{\star}
we procure a
u\in(0,1)
such that

D(z_{i,j},u^{-1}\overline{r}_{i})\subset(p(S^{1}))^{3\overline{\upalpha}\log N/N}\cap\Omega_{1/(2N)}\setminus(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2}\cup\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}),\quad\mbox{ for all }j\in\overline{N}_{i}\mbox{ and }i\in[\overline{i}_{\star}].

Hence, arguing similarly as in the proof of Theorem
7.1
we deduce that the number of eigenvalues of
P^{Q}_{N,\gamma}
in
D_{i,j}
is
O(\log N)
for all
j\in\overline{N}_{i}
and
i\in[\overline{i}_{\star}]
, on a set with probability at least
1-1/N^{2}
.

To complete the proof we use a volumetric argument, yet again, to find that

\overline{N}_{i}=O\left(2^{-2i}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\upalpha}}{\underline{\upalpha}}\right)^{2}\right).

This, together with the choice of
\overline{i}_{\star}
indeed yields the desired bound on
\mathcal{N}_{\underline{\upalpha},\overline{\upalpha},z}
.
∎

We now turn to the proof of Lemma
7.2
. Recall
\phi_{\infty}(z)
, see (
7.5
), and define

\widetilde{\det}_{k}(z):=\frac{\det_{k}(z)}{a_{-N_{-}}^{N}\prod_{i=1}^{m_{-}}\eta_{i}(z)^{N}}=\frac{\det_{k}(z)}{\exp(N\phi_{\infty}(z))},

where the last equality follows upon recalling that
\{-\eta_{i}(z)\}_{i=1}^{m_{-}}
are the roots of
p_{z}(\cdot)=0
that are greater than or equal to one in modulus, and from the fact that

\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\log|\eta-e^{i\theta}|d\theta=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\log|\eta|&\mbox{ if }|\eta|\geqslant 1,\\
0&\mbox{ otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

In the lemma below we derive a bound on the supremum of the second moment
\widetilde{\det}_{k}(\cdot)
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let Assumption
1.1
hold.
Fix
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0},\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}>0
such that
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/\varepsilon_{0}
is sufficiently small. Fix
k\in[N]\cup\{0\}
and
d\geqslant 0
. Then for any
\varepsilon\leqslant\varepsilon_{\ref {lem:tube-blow-up}}\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/2
,
we have

\sup_{z\in\left(\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\right)^{\varepsilon}}\mathbf{E}\left[\left|\widetilde{\det}_{k}(z)\right|^{2}\right]=O(1),\quad s\in\{1,2\}.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.9
it suffices to show that

(7.11)

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}}\mathbf{E}\left[\left|\widetilde{\det}_{k}(z)\right|^{2}\right]=O(1).

We begin with the proof of (
7.11
) for
k\in[N]
and
s=1
. Recall from (
5.12
) that for this choice of
s
,
we have that
\widehat{m}_{1}=m_{-}
. Since
Q
satisfies Assumption
1.1
, we then observe that

(7.12)

\mathbf{E}\left[\left|\widetilde{\det}_{k}(z)\right|^{2}\right]=N^{-2\gamma k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X,Y\subset[N]\\
|X|=|Y|=k\end{subarray}}|\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}(X,Y,z)|^{2}\cdot|\eta_{\widehat{m}_{1}+1}(z)|^{2{\sf g}_{0}N}\cdot\mathbf{E}\left[\left|\det\left(Q\left[X;Y\right]\right)\right|^{2}\right].

By Lemma
5.13
(i)-(ii), for any
k\in[N]
, as
|\eta_{\widehat{m}_{1}+1}(\cdot)|\leqslant 1
on
\mathcal{T}^{d,(1)}
, we have that

(7.13)

|\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}(X,Y,z)|^{2}\cdot|\eta_{\widehat{m}_{1}+1}(z)|^{2{\sf g}_{0}N}\leqslant C^{\widetilde{m}(k+N_{+})},

for some constant
C<\infty
. Since the second moment of the determinant of any
k\times k
matrix with entries satisfying Assumption
1.1
is
k!
and the number of ways one can choose two subsets of
[N]
that are cardinality
k
is
\binom{N}{k}^{2}
the claimed upper bound, for
s=1
, now follows upon using that
\gamma>1
and plugging the bound (
7.13
) in (
7.12
).

To prove (
7.11
) for
s=2
we observe that
\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}=m_{-}
, and thus
\widetilde{\det}_{k}(\cdot)=N^{-\gamma d}\widehat{\det}_{k}(\cdot)
on
\mathcal{T}^{d,(2)}
. Hence, the bound for any
k\in[N]
is immediate from Lemmas
6.1
and
6.2
(iii).

To prove (
7.11
) for
k=0
we recall (
5.12
) to notice that
\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}=m_{-}
on
\mathcal{T}^{d,(2)}
, while on
\mathcal{T}^{d,(1)}
we have
\widehat{m}_{1}=m_{-}
. Therefore, as
|\widehat{\eta}_{\widehat{m}_{1}+1}(\cdot)|\leqslant 1
on
\mathcal{T}^{d,(1)}
, we find that

\left|\prod_{j=1}^{\widehat{m}_{1}+{\sf g}_{0}}\eta_{j}(\cdot)\right|\leqslant\left|\prod_{j=1}^{m_{-}}\eta_{j}(\cdot)\right|

on
\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}
for any
s\in\{1,2\}
. This, in turn, implies that
|\widetilde{\det}_{k}(\cdot)|\leqslant|\widehat{\det}_{k}(\cdot)|
on
\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}
(recall (
5.32
) and (
5.35
)). Thus, the bound for
k=0
is immediate from Lemma
6.7
(i)-(ii).
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

Set
\varepsilon_{0}=c_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}/2
. Fix
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}>0
so that
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/\varepsilon_{0}
is small enough for Lemma
7.5
to hold. Now set
c_{\ref {lem-detUB}}=C_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}^{-1}c_{\ref {lem:tube-geo}}\cdot(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/\varepsilon_{0})/2
. Applying Lemma
6.16
(iii) and Markov’s inequality we find that it suffices to show that for all
d\in\mathds{Z}

(7.14)

\max_{s\in\{1,2\}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\cap\Omega_{1/N}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}}|\widetilde{\det}_{k}(z)|^{2}\right]=O(N^{2}).

The case
d<0
can be dealt by consider the transpose of
P_{N}
. Hence, we will prove (
7.14
) only for
d\geqslant 0
.
Toward this end, we use Lemma
7.5
and ideas from the proof of Lemma
6.6
. Fix
d\geqslant 0
,
s\in\{1,2\}
,
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime\prime}=1/(4N)
, and
\beta\in(0,1/8)
. Let
\mathds{D},\varepsilon,b
, and
\mathds{D}_{i}^{\beta}
, for
i\in[b]
be as in the proof of Lemma
6.6
, where now the centers
z_{i}
of the disks
\mathds{D}_{i}^{\beta}
are restricted to be inside
