\widetilde{\mathds{T}}:=\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}\cap\Omega_{1/N}
. Notice that
\cup_{i=1}^{b}\mathds{D}_{i}^{\beta}\supset\widetilde{\mathds{T}}
.

Applying Cauchy’s integral formula for smooth functions, and proceeding as in the proof of (
6.2
) we obtain that

(7.15)

\mathbf{E}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathds{D}_{i}^{\beta}}\left|\Xi(z)\right|^{2}\right]=O(1)\cdot\sup_{w\in\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}}\mathbf{E}\left[|\Xi(w)|^{2}\right],

for any random holomorphic function
\Xi:\mathds{D}\mapsto\mathds{C}
. To complete the proof it remains to argue that the map
z\mapsto\widetilde{\det}_{k}(z)
is analytic on
\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}
for each
i\in[b]
.

To this end, observe that
\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}\subset\Omega_{1/(2N)}
. Hence, the map
z\mapsto m_{-}(z)
is constant on
\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}
. From the definition of the tubes, and Lemma
6.9
(ii) it further follows that

(7.16)

|\eta_{m_{-}}(\cdot)|\geqslant|\eta_{m_{-}+1}(\cdot)|+\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}/4\quad\mbox{ on }\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}\subset\widetilde{\mathds{T}}^{3\beta}.

Therefore, arguing as in the proof of Lemma
6.6
we deduce that the map
z\mapsto\widetilde{\det}_{k}(z)
is indeed analytic on
\mathds{D}_{i}^{3\beta}
. Thus, using the bound on
b
(see (
6.35
)), Lemma
7.5
, and (
7.15
) the proof of (
7.14
) is completed.
∎

We now turn to the proof of Lemma
7.3
. The key ingredient will be the anti-concentration bound derived in Lemma
6.12
.
We need to argue that
\det(P_{z}^{\delta})
admits certain specific representation so that Lemma
6.12
is applicable.
To carry out this step we need the following couple of notation.

Fix
X_{0},Y_{0}\subset[N]
such that
|X_{0}|=|Y_{0}|=k_{0}
for some
k_{0}\leqslant N
. For
k\geqslant k_{0}
define

(7.17)

{\det}_{k}^{X_{0},Y_{0}}(z):=N^{-\gamma k}\sum\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}X,Y\subset[N]\\
|X|=|Y|=k\\
X\supset X_{0},Y\supset Y_{0}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma_{X})\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma_{Y})}\cdot(-1)^{\widehat{\operatorname{sgn}}(\sigma_{X})+\widehat{\operatorname{sgn}}(\sigma_{Y})}\mathfrak{D}(X,Y)\\
\cdot\det(Q[X\setminus X_{0};Y\setminus Y_{0}]),

where
\widehat{\operatorname{sgn}}(X)
and
\widehat{\operatorname{sgn}}(Y)
are the signs of the permutations on
X
and
Y
that place all elements of
X_{0}
and
Y_{0}
before those of
X\setminus X_{0}
and
Y\setminus Y_{0}
, respectively, but preserves the order of the elements in each of those individual sets. Also define

\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{X_{0},Y_{0}}(z):=\frac{\det_{k}(z)}{\exp(N\phi_{\infty}(z))}.

Lemma 7.6
.

Consider the setup as in Lemma
7.3
. Fix
d\geqslant 0
and
s\in\{1,2\}
. Let
X_{0},Y_{0}\subset[N]
with
|X_{0}|=|Y_{0}|=d
. The following moment bounds hold for
{\det}_{k}^{X_{0},Y_{0}}(z)
:

(i)

For any
k
such that
d\leqslant k\leqslant N
we have

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}}\mathbf{E}\left[\left|\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{X_{0},Y_{0}}(z)\right|^{2}\right]\leqslant C_{\ref {lem:lbd-det}}^{\widetilde{m}(k+N_{+})}N^{-2\gamma k+2(k-k_{0})},

where
C_{\ref {lem:lbd-det}}<\infty
is some constant.

(ii)

Fix
h,K_{0}\in\mathds{N}
. Then, for any
k\in\mathds{N}
such that
d\leqslant k\leqslant K_{0}-N_{+}
we have

(7.18)

\sup_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}}\mathbf{E}\left[\left|\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{X_{0},Y_{0}}(z)\right|^{2h}\right]\leqslant C_{\ref {lem:lbd-det}}^{\widetilde{m}K_{0}h}\cdot(K_{0}h)^{8K_{0}h}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{2K_{0}h}\cdot N^{-2\gamma hk+2h(k-k_{0})}.

Proof.

The proof of part (i) is similar in nature to that of Lemma
7.5
. Hence, details are omitted.

To prove part (ii) we employ the same combinatorial argument as in the proof Lemma
6.4
. Indeed, similarly to (
6.11
), one can obtain an analogous expression for the
(2h)
-th moment of the absolute value of
\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{X_{0},Y_{0}}(z)
.

Then, using that that entries of
Q
are independent and possess zero mean, one observes that only partitions such that each block has size at least two need to be summed. This forces the number of such partitions to be at most
N^{2(k-d)h}
. Now, use the bound (
7.13
) for
s=1
, while for
s=2
use Lemma
5.13
(i), and proceed as in the proof of Lemma
6.4
. This yields the desired bound.
Further details are omitted.
∎

We now use Lemmas
6.12
and
7.6
to derive Lemma
7.3
.

Proof of Lemma
7.3
.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
7.2
we notice that it suffices to prove the probability bound in (
7.6
) for
z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}
for
d\geqslant 0
,
s\in\{1,2\}
, and some appropriately chosen
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
.

Fix
d\geqslant 0
and
s\in\{1,2\}
. Set
\widetilde{X}_{\star}=[N]\setminus[N-d]
and
\widetilde{Y}_{\star}=[d]
.
We claim that for any
k\geqslant d

(7.19)

\widetilde{\det}_{k}(z)=\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}_{\star},\widetilde{Y}_{\star}}(z)\cdot\det(Q[\widetilde{X}_{\star};\widetilde{Y}_{\star}])+\hbox{\hskip 459.34622pt\hskip-6.94446pt\hbox{$\det$}\hskip-6.94446pt\hskip-459.34622pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-0.8}{\hbox{\raisebox{-4.00868pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{169302}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-459.34622pt\hskip 459.34622pt}_{k}(z),

where
\hbox{\hskip 459.34622pt\hskip-6.94446pt\hbox{$\det$}\hskip-6.94446pt\hskip-459.34622pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-0.8}{\hbox{\raisebox{-4.00868pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{169302}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-459.34622pt\hskip 459.34622pt}_{k}(z)
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
k
in the entries of
Q
such that the degree of each individual entry is at most one, and the total degree of the entries of
Q[\widetilde{X}_{\star};\widetilde{Y}_{\star}]
is strictly less than
d
. To see this claim we recall from (
5.9
) that

(7.20)

\displaystyle{\det}_{k}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X,Y\subset[N]\\
|X|=|Y|=k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma_{X})\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma_{Y})}\mathfrak{D}(X,Y)\cdot N^{-\gamma k}\cdot\det(Q[X;Y]).

Now we split the sum into two parts depending on whether
X\supset\widetilde{X}_{\star}
and
Y\supset\widetilde{Y}_{\star}
. If either
X\not\supset\widetilde{X}_{\star}
or
Y\not\supset\widetilde{Y}_{\star}
then the corresponding term in the
rhs
of (
7.20
) is indeed a polynomial in the entries of
Q
such that the total degree of the entries of
Q[\widetilde{X}_{\star};\widetilde{Y}_{\star}]
is strictly less than
d
. Now fix
X,Y\subset[N]
such that
X\supset\widetilde{X}_{\star}
and
Y\supset\widetilde{Y}_{\star}
. Expand
\det(Q[X;Y])
by writing it as a sum over permutations
\uppi:X\mapsto Y
. The sum over permutations
\uppi
such that
\uppi(\widetilde{X}_{\star})=\widetilde{Y}_{\star}
is indeed a product of
\det(Q[X\setminus\widetilde{X}_{\star};Y\setminus\widetilde{Y}_{\star}])
and
\det(Q[\widetilde{X}_{\star};\widetilde{Y}_{\star}])
(upto some signs). The sum over the rest of the permutations is again a polynomial such that the total degree of the entries of
Q[\widetilde{X}_{\star};\widetilde{Y}_{\star}]
is strictly less than
d
. Combining these observations and upon recalling the definition of
\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}_{\star},\widetilde{Y}_{\star}}(z)
we arrive at (
7.19
).

On the other hand,
\widetilde{\det}_{k}(z)
is a polynomial of total degree less than
d
, for any
k<d
. Since
\det(P_{z}^{\delta})=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\det_{k}(z)
, this together with (
7.19
) imply that

\frac{\det(P_{z}^{\delta})}{\exp(N\phi_{\infty}(z))}=\sum_{\mathcal{I}\subset[d]}Z_{\mathcal{I}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}U_{i},

where
\{U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d}
are the diagonal entries of the sub matrix
Q[\widetilde{X}_{\star};\widetilde{Y}_{\star}]
,
\{Z_{\mathcal{I}},\mathcal{I}\subset[d]\}
is a collection of random variables that are independent of
\{U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d}
, and

Z_{[d]}=\sum_{k\geqslant d}\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}_{\star},\widetilde{Y}_{\star}}(z).

We now apply Lemma
6.12
, and obtain that

(7.21)

\mathbf{P}\left(\frac{\det(P_{z}^{\delta})}{\exp(N\phi_{\infty}(z))}\leqslant N^{-(4+\gamma d)}\right)\leqslant N^{-4}+\mathbf{P}\left(|Z_{[d]}|\leqslant\frac{\overline{c}_{\star}}{2}\cdot N^{-\gamma d}\right),

for all large
N
, and any
\overline{c}_{\star}>0
. It remains to bound the probability that
Z_{[d]}
exceeds
(\overline{c}_{\star}/2)\cdot N^{-\gamma d}
in absolute value.

Turning to do that we set
K_{0}=\lceil\frac{5}{(\gamma-1)}\rceil+d+N_{+}
. Apply Lemma
7.6
(i) and Markov’s inequality to deduce that

\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}_{\star},\widetilde{Y}_{\star}}(z)\right|\geqslant N^{-(2+\gamma d)}\right)\leqslant N^{-5},

for any
k\geqslant K_{0}-N_{+}
.
Apply Lemma
7.6
(ii) with
h=\lceil\frac{6}{(\gamma-1)}\rceil
and Markov’s inequality to further derive that

\mathbf{P}\left(\left|\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}_{\star},\widetilde{Y}_{\star}}(z)\right|\geqslant N^{-\frac{(\gamma-1)}{2}-\gamma d}\right)\leqslant N^{-5},

for any
k\in[N]
such that
d<k<K_{0}-N_{+}
. Hence, by a union bound we obtain that

(7.22)

\mathbf{P}\left(N^{\gamma d}\left|\sum_{k>d}\widetilde{\det}_{k}^{\widetilde{X}_{\star},\widetilde{Y}_{\star}}(z)\right|\geqslant N^{-1}+N^{-\frac{(\gamma-1)}{4}}\right)\leqslant N^{-4}.

Equipped with (
7.21
) and (
7.22
), and upon recalling the definition
Z_{[d]}
we notice that to complete the proof of this lemma it is now enough to show that

(7.23)

N^{\gamma d}\cdot\inf_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}}\left|\widetilde{\det}_{d}^{\widetilde{X}_{\star},\widetilde{Y}_{\star}}(z)\right|=\inf_{z\in\mathcal{T}^{d,(s)}_{\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0}}}\frac{|\mathfrak{D}(\widetilde{X}_{\star},\widetilde{Y}_{\star},z)|}{|a_{-N_{-}}|^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{m_{-}}|\eta_{j}(z)|^{N}}\geqslant\overline{c}_{\star}

for some
\overline{c}_{\star}>0
. To prove (
7.23
) we argue as in the proof of Lemma
6.7
(b). The only difference is that one needs to use (
7.16
) instead of (
6.43
). The rest of the argument is the same. This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

We end this section with the proof of Theorem
1.5
. It is immediate from Theorems
5.4
,
5.7
, and
7.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

We use

\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{\widehat{\Omega}}
to denote the number of eigenvalues of
P_{N,\gamma}^{Q}
in
\widehat{\Omega}
. Fix
\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}>0
such that the Lebesgue measure of the set
p^{-1}(\mathcal{B}_{p}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}\cap p(S^{1}))
is less than
\mu/(16\pi)
(recall Definition
1.3
). Then, by
[
8
, Theorem 1.2]
(see also
[
65
, Theorem 2.1]
) it follows that
\mathbf{P}(\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{\widehat{\Omega}_{1}}\geqslant\mu N/4)\to 0
as
N\to\infty
, where
\widehat{\Omega}_{1}:=\mathcal{B}_{p}^{\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}
. On the other hand, by Theorems
5.4
,
5.7
, and Lemma
6.16
(iii)-(v) it follows that there exists some
0<\widehat{c}_{\gamma}<\infty
such that
\mathbf{P}(\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{\widehat{\Omega}_{2}}\,>0)\to 0
as
N\to\infty
, where
\widehat{\Omega}_{2}:=(p(S^{1}))^{\widehat{c}_{\gamma}\log N/N}\setminus\widehat{\Omega}_{1}
.
Finally, upon choosing
\upalpha=4C_{\ref {thm-thintube}}/\mu
, and applying Theorem
7.1
with this
\upalpha
we deduce that
\mathbf{P}(\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{\widehat{\Omega}_{3}}\geqslant N\mu/4)\to 0
as
N\to\infty
, where
\widehat{\Omega}_{3}:=\Omega_{\upalpha\log N/N}
(recall (
7.1
)).

Setting
\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}}=\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{0}
and
C_{\ref {theo-location}}=\upalpha\vee\widehat{c}_{\gamma}^{-1}
and taking a union bound over
the events that
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{\widehat{\Omega}_{i}}\geqslant N\mu/4

for
i=1,2,3
, completes the proof.
∎

8.
Resolvent estimates close to the spectral curve

Given a symbol
p(\zeta)-z
, with
z\in\mathds{C}
, as in
(
4.16
), we will see that there is an important
distinction between the regime where the spectral parameter
z
is inside a loop of
p(S^{1})
,
i.e. where the winding number
\mathrm{ind}_{p(S^{1})}(z)
of the curve
p(S^{1})
around
z

is non-zero, and the regime where
\mathrm{ind}_{p(S^{1})}(z)=0
. In the former case,
we
will provide in Section
9

a construction of quasimodes, which are approximate
singular vectors, for finite Toeplitz matrices
P_{N}-z
. In regions where the
winding number
is zero, we will be interested in obtaining resolvent estimates. The key difficulty in both regimes
will be in obtaining sufficiently good estimates when
z
is allowed to be at an
N
-dependent
distance from
p(S^{1})
.

In this section will prove the following estimate on the resolvent of a parameter
dependent Toeplitz matrix which, while being also of independent interest,
will be an
essential ingredient in proving a spectral gap for the small singular values, see Section

10.1
.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
\Omega^{\prime}\Subset\mathds{C}
be a non-empty relatively compact open set.
Let
N\in\mathds{N}
, let
\Omega_{N}\Subset\Omega^{\prime}
be a family of non-empty relatively compact open sets.
Let
q_{z}
,
z\in\Omega^{\prime}
, be a family of Laurent
polynomials as in (
4.21
), with
\mathfrak{N}_{z,\pm}=\mathfrak{N}_{\pm}\geqslant 0
independent of

z
in
\Omega_{N}
and satisfying (
4.22
), and with
coefficients
q_{z,n}\in\mathds{C}
for
-\mathfrak{N}_{-}\leqslant n\leqslant\mathfrak{N}_{+}
. Suppose that:

•

There exists a constant
0<C<\infty
such that for all
z\in\Omega^{\prime};

(8.1)

q_{z,\mathfrak{N}_{+}}\neq 0,\quad|q_{z,-\mathfrak{N}_{-}}|\geqslant 1/C,\quad|q_{z,n}|\leqslant C,\text{ for }-\mathfrak{N}_{-}\leqslant n\leqslant\mathfrak{N}_{+}.

•

All roots
\zeta_{z}
of
q_{z}(\zeta)
are simple, for all
z\in\Omega^{\prime}
.

•

There exists a constant
0<C<\infty
such that for all
z\in\Omega^{\prime}
and for any two
distinct roots
\zeta_{z}\neq\omega_{z}
we have

(8.2)

|\zeta_{z}-\omega_{z}|\geqslant 1/C,

and for any root
\zeta_{z}

(8.3)

0<|\zeta_{z}|\leqslant C.

•

0\notin q_{z}(S^{1})
and there exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that for
N>0
large enough

(8.4)

\mathrm{dist\,}(|\zeta_{z}|,1)\geqslant C_{0}\frac{\log N}{N},\quad\text{for all }z\in\Omega_{N},\text{ and all roots }\zeta_{z}.

•

There exists an
m_{0}\geqslant 0
(unrelated to the constants
m_{0}
in (
4.27
) and (
4.28
))
and constants
C_{1}>1,C_{2}>0
such that for
all
N\in\mathds{N}
large enough and all
z\in\Omega_{N}
we have that the roots of
q_{z}(\zeta)

inside
D(0,1)
, of total number
m_{z,+}\geqslant m_{0}
, satisfy

(8.5)

|\zeta_{1}^{+}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{z,+}-m_{0}}^{+}|<1/C_{1}<1-C_{2}\frac{\log N}{N}\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{z,+}-m_{0}+1}^{+}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{z,+}}^{+}|.

•

Let
\widehat{m}_{0}\in\{0,m_{0}\}
and suppose that
for all
N\in\mathds{N}
and
all
z\in\Omega_{N}
we have
\mathrm{ind}_{q_{z}(S^{1})}(0)=\widehat{m}_{0}
.

Then there exists a constant
0<\widetilde{C}<+\infty
such that for
N>0
large enough and all
z\in\Omega_{N}
,

(8.6)

\|P_{N}(q_{z})^{-1}\|\leqslant\widetilde{C}\frac{N^{1+C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}}{\log N}.

Remark 8.2
.

It will be clear from the proof that (
8.6
) holds when
\mathrm{ind}_{q_{z}(S^{1})}(0)=0
even without
the structural assumption (
8.5
). Furthermore, notice that in Theorem
8.1
we
only consider the case of non-negative winding numbers. This is all that we need in the sequel, however
one could prove the same result in the case of negative winding numbers provided that one assume
a similar assumption as (
8.5
) for the roots in
\mathds{C}\backslash\overline{D(0,1)}
.

Proof of Theorem
8.1
.

Throughout the proof, we will assume that
N>0
is sufficiently large so that the assumptions of the theorem
hold. Moreover, all
O(1)
terms and constants are understood to be uniform in
N>0
and
z\in\Omega_{N}
,
without us mentioning it explicitly at each occurrence. In fact the error terms will only depend on the constants
in the hypothesis of the theorem and global constants.

1. Let
z\in\Omega_{N}
. Since
q_{z}
satisfies the assumptions of Case 1 of
Lemma
4.1
, we may order the roots of
q_{z}(\zeta)
as in (
4.26
), and using also (
8.3
) and (
8.4
),

(8.7)

0<|\zeta_{1}^{+}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{z,+}}^{+}|<1<|\zeta_{1}^{-}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{z,-}}^{-}|\leqslant C.

Since
\mathrm{ind}_{q_{z}(S^{1})}(0)=\widehat{m}_{0}
for all
z\in\Omega_{N}
,
it follows from a similar computation as in (
4.34
) that

m_{z,\pm}=\mathfrak{N}_{\pm}\pm\widehat{m}_{0}
are independent of
N
and
z
.
Keeping in mind that
m_{z,\pm}
are independent of
z
, but that the coefficients

q_{z,n}
do depend on
z
, we suppress from now on the
z
subscript of
q_{z}
,

q_{z,n}
and
m_{z,\pm}
. Hence, we are interested in the symbol

(8.8)

q(\zeta)=\sum_{-(m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0})}^{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}q_{j}\zeta^{-j},

where by (
8.1
)

(8.9)

q_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}\neq 0,\quad|q_{-(m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0})}|\geqslant 1/C

2. We turn to estimating
\|P_{N}(q)^{-1}\|
. We begin by inverting

\mathrm{Op}(q):\ell^{2}(\mathds{Z})\to\ell^{2}(\mathds{Z})
, see (
4.17
), which is a convolution operator. We first
search for a fundamental solution
E:\mathds{Z}\to\mathds{C}
to

(8.10)

\mathrm{Op}(q)E=\delta_{0}\quad\text{on }\mathds{Z},

where
\delta_{0}(\nu)=\delta_{0,\nu}
, see Section
1.6
for the Dirac notation. Putting

(8.11)

E(n)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\!\left(\frac{1}{q}\right)\!(n)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{q(\mathrm{e}^{i\xi})}\mathrm{e}^{in\xi}d\xi.

it follows from (
4.5
) that
E
solves (
8.10
). Thus,

(8.12)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{Op}(q)E*v=v,\quad v\in\ell^{2}_{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathds{Z})\\
&E*\mathrm{Op}(q)u=u,\quad u\in\ell^{2}_{\mathrm{comp}}(\mathds{Z}),\end{split}

where
*
denotes the convolution on
\mathds{Z}
and
\ell^{2}_{\mathrm{comp}}
denotes
compactly supported functions in
\ell^{2}
.

Notice that we may factor (
8.8
) as

(8.13)

q(\zeta)=q_{-(m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0})}\prod_{j=1}^{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}(1-\zeta^{+}_{j}/\zeta)\prod_{j=m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}+1}^{m_{+}}(\zeta-\zeta^{+}_{j})\prod_{k=1}^{m_{-}}(\zeta-\zeta_{k}^{-}).

Performing the change of variables
\mathrm{e}^{i\xi}=\zeta\in S^{1}
in (
8.11
), we
get by shrinking
S^{1}
to
0
, the residue theorem and (
8.13
) that for
n\geqslant 1

(8.14)

\begin{split}E(n)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{S^{1}}\frac{\zeta^{n-1}}{q(\zeta)}d\zeta=\sum_{r=1}^{m_{+}}\frac{(\zeta^{+}_{r})^{n-1+m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}}{q_{-(m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0})}\prod_{j\neq r}(\zeta^{+}_{r}-\zeta^{+}_{j})\prod_{k=1}^{m_{-}}(\zeta^{+}_{r}-\zeta_{k}^{-})},\end{split}

Similarly, we get that

(8.15)

\begin{split}E(0)=\sum_{r=1}^{m_{+}}\frac{(\zeta^{+}_{r})^{-1+m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}}{q_{-(m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0})}\prod_{j\neq r}(\zeta^{+}_{r}-\zeta^{+}_{j})\prod_{k=1}^{m_{-}}(\zeta^{+}_{r}-\zeta_{k}^{-})}+\frac{\delta_{m_{+},\widehat{m}_{0}}}{q_{-(m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0})}\prod_{j=1}^{m_{+}}(-\zeta^{+}_{j})\prod_{k=1}^{m_{-}}(-\zeta_{k}^{-})}.\end{split}

By deforming
S^{1}
to
|\zeta|=R
,
R\to\infty
, we obtain similarly that
for
n\leqslant-1
,

(8.16)

E(n)=-\sum_{r=1}^{m_{-}}\frac{(\zeta^{-}_{r})^{n-1+m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}}{q_{-(m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0})}\prod_{j=1}^{m_{+}}(\zeta^{-}_{r}-\zeta^{+}_{j})\prod_{j\neq r}(\zeta_{r}^{-}-\zeta_{j}^{-})}.

Combining (
8.14
)-(
8.16
), (
8.2
),
(
8.7
), (
8.1
) and (
8.9
), we have that

(8.17)

|E(n)|=O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}|^{n-1},&n\geqslant 1,\\
1,&n=0,\\
|\zeta_{1}^{-}|^{n-1},&n\leqslant-1.\end{array}\right.

Here, we
replace
(\zeta_{1}^{-})^{-1}
resp.
\zeta_{m_{+}}
with
0
when

m_{-}=0
resp.
m_{+}=0
. Thus, summing the geometric series and
using (
8.4
) we get that

(8.18)

\|E\|_{\ell^{1}}=O(1)\frac{N}{\log N},

and similarly that

(8.19)

\|E\|_{\ell^{2}}=O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}.

Since
E
is in
\ell^{1}
, see (
8.18
),
the operator of convolution with
E
, denoted
E*
,
is a bounded operator

\ell^{2}\to\ell^{2}
. Therefore,
(
8.12
) can be extended to
u,v\in\ell^{2}(\mathds{Z})
, and
E*
is the inverse of
\mathrm{Op}(q)

on
\ell^{2}(\mathds{Z})
.

3. Next, for
v\in\ell^{2}(\mathds{Z})
with support in
[0,N-1]
, we solve

(8.20)

\mathrm{Op}(q)\psi=0\quad\text{ on }\mathds{Z}

with “boundary” conditions

(8.21)

\psi\!\upharpoonright_{[-(m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}),-1]\cup[N,N+m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0}-1]}=(E*v)\!\upharpoonright_{[-(m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}),-1]\cup[N,N+m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0}-1]}.

We suppose here, and in the sequel, that
m_{\pm}\mp\widehat{m}_{0}>0
. The cases
when
m_{+}=\widehat{m}_{0}
or
m_{-}=\widehat{m}_{0}=0
can be treated similarly, and we shall
comment on these cases as we go along. In (
8.21
) it is understood that when

m_{+}=\widehat{m}_{0}
, then we only have the boundary condition
on
[N,N+m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0}-1]
, and similarly when
m_{-}=\widehat{m}_{0}=0
.

Since
q(\zeta)
satisfies the assumptions of Case 1 of Lemma
4.1
,
it follows from Propositions
4.4
and
4.5
, and the fact that all
roots of
q(\zeta)
are simple, that the general solution to (
8.20
) is of the form

(8.22)

\psi(\nu)=\sum_{j=1}^{m_{+}}a_{j}^{+}(\zeta_{j}^{+})^{\nu}+\sum_{j=1}^{m_{-}}a_{j}^{-}(\zeta_{j}^{-})^{\nu},\quad a^{\pm}_{j}\in\mathds{C}.

By (
8.21
), the coefficients are determined by

(8.23)

\begin{pmatrix}V_{+}\Lambda_{+}^{\widehat{m}_{0}-m_{+}}&B_{+}\Lambda_{-}^{\widehat{m}_{0}-m_{+}}\\
V_{-}\Lambda_{+}^{N}&B_{-}\Lambda_{-}^{N}\\
\end{pmatrix}a=:Ma=c:=(E*v)\!\upharpoonright_{[\widehat{m}_{0}-m_{+},-1]\cup[N,N+m_{-}+\widehat{m}_{0}-1]}.

Here, the right hand side is seen as a vector in
\mathds{C}^{m_{+}+m_{-}}
,
a=(a_{+},a_{-})^{\sf T}
,

a_{\pm}=(a^{\pm}_{1},\dots,a^{\pm}_{m_{\pm}})\in\mathds{C}^{m_{\pm}}
,

\Lambda_{+}:=\mathrm{diag}(\zeta_{1}^{+},\dots,\zeta_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}^{+})
,

\Lambda_{-}:=\mathrm{diag}(\zeta_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}+1}^{+},\dots,\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+},\zeta_{1}^{-},\dots,\zeta_{m_{-}}^{-})
,

(8.24)

V_{\pm}:=\begin{pmatrix}1&\dots&1\\
\zeta_{1}^{+}&\dots&\zeta_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}^{+}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
(\zeta_{1}^{+})^{m_{\pm}\mp\widehat{m}_{0}-1}&\dots&(\zeta_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}^{+})^{m_{\pm}\mp\widehat{m}_{0}-1}\end{pmatrix},

and

(8.25)

B_{\pm}:=\begin{pmatrix}1&\dots&1&1&\dots&1\\
\zeta_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}+1}^{+}&\dots&\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}&\zeta_{1}^{-}&\dots&\zeta_{m_{-}}^{-}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
(\zeta_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}+1}^{+})^{m_{\pm}\mp\widehat{m}_{0}-1}&\dots&(\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+})^{m_{\pm}\mp\widehat{m}_{0}-1}&(\zeta_{1}^{-})^{m_{\pm}\mp\widehat{m}_{0}-1}&\dots&(\zeta_{m_{-}}^{-})^{m_{\pm}\mp\widehat{m}_{0}-1}\end{pmatrix}.

Here, we use the convention that when
\widehat{m}_{0}=0
and
m_{+}>0
, then the first column of
B_{\pm}

is the one containing powers of
\zeta_{1}^{-}
.

Remark 8.3
.

When
m_{+}=\widehat{m}_{0}
then
(
8.23
) is given by
B_{-}\Lambda_{-}^{N}a=Ma=(E*v)\!\upharpoonright_{[N,N+m_{-}-1]}
,
and when
m_{-}=\widehat{m}_{0}=0
then only the first sum in (
8.22
) remains and
(
8.23
) is given by
V_{+}\Lambda_{+}^{-m_{+}}a_{+}=Ma=(E*v)\!\upharpoonright_{[-m_{+},-1]}
.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
8.19
), we have that for any
k\in\mathds{Z}

(8.26)

|E*v(k)|=\left|\sum_{0}^{N-1}E(k-m)v(m)\right|\leqslant\|E\|_{\ell^{2}(\mathds{Z})}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}(\mathds{Z})}\leqslant O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}(\mathds{Z})}.

We put
L:=L_{+}\oplus L_{-}
with

(8.27)

\begin{split}&L_{+}=\mathrm{diag}(G(\zeta_{1}^{+}),\dots,G(\zeta_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}^{+})),\\
&L_{-}=\mathrm{diag}(G(\zeta_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}+1}^{+}),\dots,G(\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}),G(\zeta_{1}^{-}),\dots,G(\zeta_{m_{-}}^{-})),\quad G(x)=\left(\frac{1-|x|^{2N}}{1-|x|^{2}}\right)^{1/2},\end{split}

where we work with the same notational convention for
L_{-}
as in (
8.25
).

Suppose that there exists a constant
0<C<\infty
such that
for
N>0
sufficiently large
M
is bijective and such that for all
z\in\Omega_{N}

(8.28)

\|LM^{-1}\|\leqslant C\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}N^{C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})},

with
C_{2}
as in (
8.5
). We will prove this fact in Step 4 below.
It then follows from the bijectivity of
M
that (
8.20
) with boundary conditions (
8.21
)
has a unique solutions
\psi
of the form (
8.22
) with coefficients determined by
(
8.23
).
Hence, putting

\widetilde{u}=(E*v-\psi)\!\upharpoonright_{[0,N-1]},

we have that
P_{N}(q)\widetilde{u}=v,

so
P_{N}(q)
is surjective, and thus, being a square matrix, bijective.
We get that for
N>0
sufficiently large,

(8.29)

\begin{split}\|\psi\|_{\ell^{2}([0,N-1])}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{re8}}}{{\leqslant}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{+}}|a_{j}^{+}|G(\zeta_{j}^{+})+\sum_{j=1}^{m_{-}}|a_{j}^{-}|G(\zeta_{j}^{-})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny\rm H\"{o}lder}}}{{\leqslant}}\sqrt{m_{+}+m_{-}}\|La\|_{\ell^{2}([1,m_{+}+m_{-}])}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{re9}}}{{\leqslant}}\sqrt{m_{+}+m_{-}}\|LM^{-1}c\|_{\ell^{2}([1,m_{+}+m_{-}])}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{nnre1}}}{{\leqslant}}O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}N^{C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}\,\|c\|_{\ell^{2}([1,m_{+}+m_{-}])}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{nnre2}}}{{\leqslant}}O(1)\frac{N^{1+C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}}{\log N}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}}.\end{split}

Using Young’s convolution
inequality and (
8.18
), we find that for
N>0
sufficiently large

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{\ell^{2}([0,N-1])}&\leqslant\|E*v\|_{\ell^{2}([0,N-1])}+\|\psi\|_{\ell^{2}([0,N-1])}\\
&\leqslant\|E\|_{\ell^{1}}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}}+\|\psi\|_{\ell^{2}([0,N-1])}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{re6.1},\eqref{eq-sout}}}{{\leqslant}}O(1)\frac{N^{1+C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}}{\log N}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}},\end{split}

uniformly for
z\in\Omega_{N}
. This proves (
8.6
), provided that (
8.28
) holds.

4. It remains to prove (
8.28
). By (
8.7
), we have that

(8.30)

\|V_{\pm}\|,\|B_{\pm}\|=O(1).

Since
V_{+},B_{-}
are Vandermonde matrices, we have that

\det V_{+}=\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}(\zeta_{i}^{+}-\zeta_{j}^{+})
and similar expression for

\det B_{-}
given by a finite product of differences of the roots

\zeta_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}+1}^{+},\dots,\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}
and

\zeta_{1}^{-},\dots,\zeta_{m_{-}}^{-}
(resp. just
\zeta_{1}^{-},\dots,\zeta_{m_{-}}^{-}
when

\widehat{m}_{0}=0
). Therefore, (
8.2
) implies that both matrices
are bijective and that their determinants are uniformly bounded from below in modulus.

Together with (
8.7
), we deduce that

(8.31)

\|(V_{+})^{-1}\|,\|(B_{-})^{-1}\|=O(1).

To construct the inverse of
M
,
we proceed via the Schur complement formula and set

\Gamma=B_{-}\Lambda_{-}^{N}-V_{-}\Lambda_{+}^{N+m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}V_{+}^{-1}B_{+}\Lambda_{-}^{\widehat{m}_{0}-m_{+}}.

Using (
8.31
), (
8.30
), (
8.4
), (
8.7
)
and (
8.5
), we have that

(8.32)

\begin{split}\|V_{-}\Lambda_{+}^{N+m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}V_{+}^{-1}&B_{+}\Lambda_{-}^{\widehat{m}_{0}-m_{+}-N}B_{-}^{-1}\|\\
&=O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|\zeta_{m_{+}}|^{N+m_{+}}|\zeta_{1}^{-}|^{-(N+m_{+})},&\widehat{m}_{0}=0,\\
|\zeta_{m_{+}-m_{0}}^{+}|^{N+m_{+}-m_{0}}|\zeta_{m_{+}-m_{0}+1}^{+}|^{m_{0}-(N+m_{+})},&\widehat{m}_{0}=m_{0},\end{array}\right.\\
&=O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}N^{-2C_{0}},&\widehat{m}_{0}=0,\\
\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{1}}N^{C_{2}},&\widehat{m}_{0}=m_{0}\end{array}\right.\quad=:\varepsilon_{N}(\widehat{m}_{0}).\end{split}

Hence, for
N>0
large enough (depending only on the constants in the
assumptions of the theorem) we have that
|\varepsilon_{N}(\widehat{m}_{0})|<1
,
and we assume from now on that this is the case.
By a Neumann series argument we see that

(8.33)

\Gamma^{-1}=\Lambda_{-}^{-N}B_{-}^{-1}(1+O(1)\varepsilon_{N}(\widehat{m}_{0})).

Here the
O(1)
term means an
m_{-}\times m_{-}
matrix with operator norm
bounded by a constant uniformly in
N
and
z\in\Omega_{N}
.

A straightforward computation yields that

(8.34)

M^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda_{+}^{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}V_{+}^{-1}&-\Lambda_{+}^{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}V_{+}^{-1}B_{+}\Lambda_{-}^{\widehat{m}_{0}-m_{+}}\\
0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-\Gamma^{-1}V_{-}\Lambda_{+}^{N_{+}+m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}V_{+}^{-1}&\Gamma^{-1}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Using (
8.27
) and (
8.34
), we obtain that

(8.35)

\displaystyle LM^{-1}=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}L_{+}\Lambda_{+}^{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}V_{+}^{-1}(1+B_{+}\Lambda_{-}^{\widehat{m}_{0}-m_{+}}\Gamma^{-1}V_{-}\Lambda_{+}^{N_{+}+m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}V_{+}^{-1}),&-L_{+}\Lambda_{+}^{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}V_{+}^{-1}B_{+}\Lambda_{-}^{\widehat{m}_{0}-m_{+}}\Gamma^{-1}\\
-L_{-}\Gamma^{-1}V_{-}\Lambda_{+}^{N_{+}+m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}V_{+}^{-1},&L_{-}\Gamma^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

To prove (
8.28
) note that it is sufficient to estimate the norm of
each block of the matrix (
8.35
) separately.
Since
|\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}|\leqslant 1\leqslant|\zeta_{1}^{-}|
and (
8.5
) holds, it follows that

(8.36)

\|\Lambda_{-}^{-n}\|,\|\Lambda_{+}^{n}\|\leqslant O(1),

for any fixed
n\in\mathds{N}
. Using that
|\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}|\leqslant 1
we find also that

(8.37)

\|\Lambda_{+}^{N}\|\leqslant O(1).

However, in view of (
8.5
) and since
1\leqslant|\zeta_{1}^{-}|
we have that

(8.38)

\|\Lambda_{-}^{-N}\|\leqslant O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}|\zeta_{1}^{-}|^{-N},&\widehat{m}_{0}=0,\\
|\zeta^{+}_{m_{+}-m_{0}+1}|^{-N},&\widehat{m}_{0}=m_{0}\end{array}\right.\leqslant O(1)N^{C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}

Continuing, we deduce from (
8.31
), (
8.33
) and (
8.38
) that

(8.39)

\|\Gamma^{-1}\|\leqslant O(1)\|\Lambda_{-}^{-N}B_{-}^{-1}\|\leqslant O(1)N^{C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}

Recall (
8.27
). Then, by (
8.4
),

\|L_{+}\|\leqslant\max_{1\leqslant j\leqslant m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}}G(\zeta_{j}^{+})=O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}.

Combining this with (
8.31
) and (
8.36
), we see that

(8.40)

\|L_{+}\Lambda_{+}^{m_{+}}V_{+}^{-1}\|=O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}.

Hence, using (
8.40
), (
8.31
), (
8.36
), (
8.30
), (
8.39
) and
(
8.37
), we get that

(8.41)

\|L_{+}\Lambda_{+}^{m_{+}}V_{+}^{-1}V_{-}\Lambda_{-}^{-m_{+}}\Gamma^{-1}B_{+}\Lambda_{+}^{N+m_{+}}V_{+}^{-1}\|=O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}N^{C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}

and

(8.42)

\|L_{+}\Lambda_{+}^{m_{+}}V_{+}^{-1}V_{-}\Lambda_{-}^{-m_{+}}\Gamma^{-1}\|=O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}N^{C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}.

Using (
8.4
) and (
8.5
), we find
that

(8.43)

\|L_{-}\Lambda_{-}^{-N}\|=\max\{\max_{j}|\zeta_{j}^{-}|^{-N}G(\zeta_{j}^{-}),\max_{m_{+}-\widehat{m}_{0}+1\leqslant j\leqslant m_{+}}|\zeta_{j}^{+}|^{-N}G(\zeta_{j}^{+})\}=O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}N^{C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}.

Hence, similarly to (
8.41
), but using as well (
8.43
) and (
8.33
), we get that

(8.44)

\|L_{-}\Gamma^{-1}B_{+}\Lambda_{+}^{N+m_{+}}V_{+}^{-1}\|=O(1)\|L_{-}\Lambda_{-}^{-N}\|=O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}N^{C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})},

and

(8.45)

\|L_{-}\Gamma^{-1}\|=O(1)\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}N^{C_{2}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}.

Hence, (
8.35
), (
8.40
)-(
8.42
), (
8.44
), and
(
8.45
) yield (
8.28
) for
N>0
large enough.

Remark 8.4
.

Recall Remark
8.3
. When
m_{+}=\widehat{m}_{0}
we have that

M^{-1}=\Lambda_{-}^{-N}B_{-}^{-1}
and
we obtain (
8.28
) by a similar estimate as in (
8.44
).
When
m_{-}=\widehat{m}_{0}=0

then
M^{-1}=\Lambda_{+}^{m_{+}}V_{+}^{-1}
and we obtain (
8.28
) by (
8.40
).

This completes the proof of Theorem
8.1
.
∎

9.
Quasimodes for banded Toeplitz matrices

In this section we will construct exponentially decaying quasimodes
for the operator

P_{N}-z
, for
z

close
to - however not on - the curve
p(S^{1})

while avoiding a small neighborhood of
the set of bad points, see Definition
1.3
. We will see that the sign of the winding
number
d(z)
will decide whether these quasimodes decay to the left (
d<0
) or to the right (
d>0
).
The construction will be based on exponential states
\mathfrak{z}^{\pm}
, see Proposition

9.3
, determined by the roots of the Laurent
polynomial
p(\zeta)-z
, with a particular focus on the decay rates of these exponential states.
We will separate between those states
\mathfrak{z}^{\pm}
which decay at a fixed rate
r>0
and
those which decay at an
N
-dependent rate
r\asymp\log N/N
, where the former are determined
by the roots of
p(\zeta)-z
“far” away from
p(S^{1})
and the latter are determined by the roots
“close” to
p(S^{1})
.

Throughout this section we fix
z_{0}\in p(S^{1})
such that

(9.1)

dp\neq 0\text{ on }p^{-1}(z_{0}).

Let
\Omega\Subset\mathds{C}
be an open relatively compact simply connected
neighbourhood of
z_{0}
such that

(9.2)

|dp|\geqslant 1/C\text{ on }p^{-1}(z)\text{ for all }z\in\Omega.

Note that by the implicit function theorem all roots
\zeta=\zeta(z)
of
p(\zeta)-z

are simple and depend smoothly on
z\in\Omega
. Hence, after possibly shrinking
\Omega
,
there exists
0<C<\infty
such that for any two distinct roots

\zeta(z)\neq\omega(z)
we have that

(9.3)

|\zeta-\omega|\geqslant\frac{1}{C},

uniformly for
z\in\Omega
. Recall the notation of the roots from
Lemma
4.1
.

Until further notice we will denote the winding number of the curve
p(S^{1})
around a point
z\in\mathds{C}
,
see also (
4.34
), by

(9.4)

d\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}d(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathrm{ind}_{p(S^{1})}(z).

Assumption 9.1
.

Let
z_{0}\in p(S^{1})
be as in (
9.1
), and let
\Omega
be as in (
9.2
), so that (
9.3
) holds.
Let
\Omega_{N}\Subset\Omega\backslash p(S^{1})
be an open relatively
compact simply connected non-empty
N
-dependent set such that the winding
number
d(z)\neq 0
is constant for all
z\in\Omega_{N}
, and such that there
exist constants
C_{\alpha},C_{\beta},C_{\gamma}>0
and
m^{0}_{\pm}\geqslant 0
,
independent of
N
, such that for
N>0
large enough (depending only on these constants)
and for all
z\in\Omega_{N}
the following holds:

•

we have that

(9.5)

p^{-1}(z)\cap(S^{1}+D(0,C_{\gamma}N^{-1}\log N))=\emptyset;

•

when
d>0
, then
0\leqslant m_{+}^{0}\leqslant d\leqslant m_{+}
and

(9.6)

0<|\zeta_{1}^{+}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}^{+}|<\frac{1}{C_{\beta}}<1-C_{\alpha}\frac{\log N}{N}\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1}^{+}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}|<1;

•

when
d<0
, then
0\leqslant m_{-}^{0}\leqslant-d\leqslant m_{-}
and

(9.7)

1<|\zeta_{1}^{-}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{-}^{0}}^{-}|<1+C_{\alpha}\frac{\log N}{N}<C_{\beta}\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{-}^{0}+1}^{+}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{-}}^{-}|<+\infty.

In (
9.6
) we work with the notation convention that when
m_{+}=m_{+}^{0}
then the inequalities before
1/C_{\beta}
are void, and when
m_{+}^{0}=0
, then the inequalities on the right hand side of
1-C_{\alpha}\log N/N
are
void. A similar convention is to be understood for (
9.7
).

Remark 9.2
.

1. Unless otherwise stated, all future estimates will only depend on

\Omega
and the constants
in Assumption
9.1
, and therefore will be uniform in
\Omega_{N}
. This will be understood
implicitly when we state that an estimate is uniform in
\Omega_{N}
.

2. We recall that we will work with

z\in\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N)
,
see
(
1.7
) and the assumptions of Theorem
1.6
.
In particular,
{\rm dist}(z,p(S^{1}))<C_{\ref {theo-location}}\log N/N
.
Let
z_{0}
be the point on
p(S^{1})
closest to
z
. Then, since
dp(z_{0})\neq 0

(as we work in
\mathcal{G}_{p,\varepsilon}
),
as well as the existence of
C_{\alpha}
as in (
9.6
) or (
9.7
),
e.g. that for
d>0
,
\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}\geqslant 1-C_{\alpha}\log N/N
.
To see the multiplicity count, i.e. that e.g.
m_{+}^{0}\leqslant d
when
d>0
,
note that away from the set
\mathcal{B}_{2}
,
if
{\sf g}(p)=1
then there is precisely one
\theta_{0}
with
p(e^{i\theta_{0}})=z_{0}
, and since
dp|_{\theta_{0}}\neq 0
, there is exactly one
root
\zeta_{m_{+}}
near
1
. When
{\sf g}(p)>1
, there are

{\sf g}(p)
such roots, while
|d|\geqslant{\sf g}(p)
if
d\neq 0
,
so again the same conclusion holds. On the other hand, by Remark
6.17

we have that for any eigenvalue
z
in the good set, with probability approaching one, the assumption (
9.5
) holds with
C_{\gamma}
as defined in Remark
6.17
.

Our aim is to construct
quasimodes
for
P_{N}-z
,
z\in\Omega_{N}
, these are
approximate
\ell^{2}
-normalized eigenvectors
\psi\in\mathds{C}^{N}
in the sense that

\|(P_{N}-z)\psi\|\to 0

when
N\to\infty
. Our focus will be on obtaining quantitative estimates for this decay.
The eigenvectors of
P_{[0,\infty[}
corresponding to the eigenvalue
z
, when

d>0
, and
shifted versions of the eigenvectors of
P_{]-\infty,0]}
corresponding to the eigenvalue

z
, when
d<0
, are excellent candidates for quasimodes (after truncation).

We will construct these quasimodes as follows:
Recall from Proposition
4.6
that these eigenvectors are exponential states which are
created from powers of the roots of
p(\zeta)-z
. Using assumption (
9.6
) and (
9.7
)
we will take care (whenever possible) to distinguish between eigenvectors which are built up only by
roots strictly away from the unit circle, and those eigenvectors which contain also a contribution
from roots close to the unit circle. After truncation we orthonormalize the states within the two
respective groups. As we shall see below,
we will need to slightly modify the behaviour at the boundary of the second group to obtain a sufficiently fast
decay. This way we will obtain an almost orthogonal
|d|
-dimensional system of
quasimodes, see Proposition
9.6
below.

Recall Proposition
4.6
. To construct quasimodes out of (
4.46
) respectively
(
4.47
), we begin with constructing a suitable system of solutions to (
4.44
) when
N_{+}>0

and its analog when
N_{-}>0
with
m_{+},N_{+},\zeta_{j}^{+}
replaced by
m_{-},N_{-},\zeta_{j}^{-}
and the corresponding vectors
\mathfrak{A}_{j}^{-}
indexed over
1\leqslant\nu\leqslant N_{-}
.

We begin with considering the case
N_{+}>0
. Notice that this implies that
0\notin p^{-1}(\Omega)
.
As discussed after (
9.2
), the roots
\zeta_{z}
of
p(\zeta)-z
depend smoothly on
z\in\Omega
,
so, after potentially slightly shrinking
\Omega
, there exists a constant
0<C<\infty
such that any root

\zeta_{z}
satisfies

(9.8)

1/C\leqslant|\zeta_{z}|,\text{ for all }z\in\Omega.

Let
z\in\Omega_{N}
, suppose that
d>0
and recall from (
4.34
) that
d=m_{+}-N_{+}
.
In view of (
9.6
), we find that
m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}\geqslant N_{+}>0
. For the sake of the presentation
we first suppose that
d>m_{+}^{0}\geqslant 1
. Recall the matrix
\mathfrak{A}
from (
4.44
) and write

\mathfrak{B}_{+}=(\mathfrak{A}_{1}^{+},\dots,\mathfrak{A}_{N_{+}}^{+})\in\mathds{C}^{N_{+}\times N_{+}},\quad\mathfrak{C}_{+}=(\mathfrak{A}_{1}^{+},\dots,\mathfrak{A}_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}^{+})\in\mathds{C}^{N_{+}\times(m_{+}-m_{+}^{0})}

By (
9.8
) we have that for all
j=1,\dots,m_{+}

(9.9)

\|\mathfrak{A}_{j}^{+}\|\leqslant C^{N_{+}}\sqrt{N_{+}}.

Notice that
\mathfrak{B}_{+}
is a Vandermonde type matrix, so

|\det\mathfrak{B}_{+}|=\prod_{k=1}^{N^{+}}|\zeta_{k}^{+}|^{-N^{+}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant N_{+}}|\zeta_{i}^{+}-\zeta_{j}^{+}|
.
Thus, by (
9.8
), (
9.3
), we see that
\mathfrak{B}_{+}
is invertible and, using (
9.9
), we
find

(9.10)

\|\mathfrak{B}_{+}\|,\|\mathfrak{B}_{+}^{-1}\|=O(1),

where the constant depends only on
\Omega
. Hence, by (
9.6
),
we see that
\mathfrak{C}_{+}
has rank
N_{+}
and has a kernel of dimension

\dim\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{C}_{+})=m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}-N_{+}=d-m_{+}^{0}>0
. Let
x_{1},\dots,x_{d-m_{+}^{0}}\in\mathds{C}^{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}
be an orthonormal system spanning
\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{C}_{+})
. Clearly,

(9.11)

\mathds{C}^{m_{+}}\ni a^{+}_{j}:=x_{j}\oplus 0_{m_{+}^{0}}\in\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{A})\text{ for }j=1,\dots,d-m_{+}^{0}.

For
j=d-m_{+}^{0}+1,\dots,d
, we put

(9.12)

a^{+}_{j}:=(-\mathfrak{B}_{+}^{-1}\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{j+N_{+}},\delta_{j})\in\mathds{C}^{m_{+}},

where
\delta_{k}\in\mathds{C}^{m_{+}-N_{+}}
is so that
\delta_{k}(n)=\delta_{k,n}
for
n\in[m_{+}-N_{+}]
. By construction
we have that the
a^{+}_{j}
are linearly independent and that they span
\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{A})
.
Writing
X_{+}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d-m_{+}^{0}})\in\mathds{C}^{(m_{+}-m_{+}^{0})\times(d-m_{+}^{0})}
and

\Delta_{+}=(\delta_{d-m_{+}^{0}+1},\dots,\delta_{d})\in\mathds{C}^{d\times m_{+}^{0}}
, we put

A^{+}_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}X_{+}\\
0\\
\end{pmatrix},\quad A^{+}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}-\mathfrak{B}_{+}^{-1}(\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1},\dots,\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}})\\
\Delta_{+}\\
\end{pmatrix},

where on the left hand side
0\in\mathds{C}^{m_{+}^{0}\times(d-m_{+}^{0})}
. Then,

(9.13)

A^{+}=(a^{+}_{1},\dots,a^{+}_{d})=(A^{+}_{1},A^{+}_{2}).

When
m_{+}^{0}=0
, then we have no solutions (
9.12
) and
A^{+}=A^{+}_{1}=X_{+}
. When
d=m_{+}^{0}
,
then we have no solutions (
9.11
) and all vectors
a_{j}^{+}
are of the form (
9.12
), so

A^{+}=A_{2}^{+}=\begin{pmatrix}-\mathfrak{B}_{+}^{-1}(\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{N_{+}+1},\dots,\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}})\\
1_{d}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Keeping in mind these special cases, we shall keep writing (
9.13
) for all cases of
d\geqslant m_{+}^{0}\geqslant 0
,
with the convention that
A_{1}^{+}
is void when
d=m_{+}^{0}
and that
A^{+}_{2}
is void when
m_{+}^{0}=0
.

When
d<0
(keeping in mind (
4.34
)) and
N_{-}>0
, we work under the
assumption (
9.7
) and we consider the
analogue of
\mathfrak{A}
from (
4.44
) with
m_{+},N_{+},\zeta_{j}^{+}
replaced by
m_{-},N_{-},\zeta_{j}^{-}

and the corresponding vectors
\mathfrak{A}_{j}^{-}
indexed over
1\leqslant\nu\leqslant N_{-}
. Notice that
since
N_{-}>0
, it follows that
\infty\notin p^{-1}(\Omega)
, so, after potentially slightly shrinking

\Omega
, we have that any root
\zeta_{z}
of
p(\zeta)-z

satisfies
|\zeta_{z}|\leqslant C
for all
z\in\Omega
. Thus, the analogue of (
9.9
) holds for
\mathfrak{A}_{j}^{-}
.
Putting
\mathfrak{B}_{-}=(\mathfrak{A}_{m_{-}-N_{-}+1}^{-},\dots,\mathfrak{A}_{m_{-}}^{-})
, we find by a
Van der Monde argument that the analogue of (
9.10
) holds for
\mathfrak{B}_{-}
.
Then, by the exact same steps as above, with the obvious modifications, we can construct
|d|
linearly independent solutions
a^{-}_{j}
, to
\mathfrak{A}a^{-}=0
, such that

(9.14)

A^{-}=(a^{-}_{1},\dots,a^{-}_{d})=\begin{pmatrix}-\mathfrak{B}_{-}^{-1}(\mathfrak{A}^{-}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{A}^{-}_{m_{-}^{0}})&X_{-}\\
\Delta_{-}&0\\
\end{pmatrix}=(A^{-}_{1},A^{-}_{2}),

where
\delta_{k}\in\mathds{C}^{m_{-}-N_{-}}
is so that
\delta_{k}(n)=\delta_{k,n}
for
n\in[m_{-}-N_{-}]
,
\Delta_{-}=(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{m_{-}^{0}})\in\mathds{C}^{|d|\times m_{-}^{0}}
,
and
X_{-}\in\mathds{C}^{(|d|-m_{-}^{0})\times(|d|-m_{-}^{0})}
is a matrix with orthonormal columns
spanning
\mathcal{N}((\mathfrak{A}_{m_{-}^{0}+1}^{-},\dots,\mathfrak{A}_{m_{-}}^{-}))
.

In what follows we identify
\ell^{2}([0,N-1])\simeq\mathds{C}^{N}
, so for the sake of consistency
we index vectors and the columns of matrices starting from
0
.

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
N\geqslant N_{0}>1
be integers,
z_{0}\in p(S^{1})
be as in (
9.1
),

\Omega\Subset\mathds{C}
be a sufficiently small open simply connected relatively
compact neighbourhood of
z_{0}
, satisfying (
9.2
), and

\Omega_{N}\Subset\Omega\backslash p(S^{1})
be as in Assumption
9.1
.
Let
N>0
be sufficiently large, depending only on
\Omega
and the
constants in Assumption
9.1
and the roots of
p(\zeta)-z
be denoted as in Lemma
4.1
. Write

(9.15)

\mathfrak{z}_{j}^{+}=(1,\zeta_{j}^{+},\dots,(\zeta_{j}^{+})^{N-1})^{t}\in\mathds{C}^{N},\quad j=1,\dots,m_{+},

and

(9.16)

\mathfrak{z}_{j}^{-}=((\zeta_{j}^{-})^{1-N},(\zeta_{j}^{-})^{2-N},\dots,1)^{t}\in\mathds{C}^{N},\quad j=1,\dots,m_{-},

with the convention that
{1}/{\infty}=0
. Moreover, let

\mathfrak{Z}^{\pm}=(\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{\pm},\dots,\mathfrak{z}_{m_{\pm}}^{\pm})
.

•

Suppose that
d>0
. When
N_{+}>0
, let

A^{+}=(a_{1}^{+},\dots,a_{d}^{+})
be as in (
9.13
), and when

N_{+}\leqslant 0
put
A^{+}=I_{m_{+}}\in\mathds{C}^{m_{+}\times m_{+}}
. Define

I_{+,1}:=[d-m_{+}^{0}]
and
I_{+,2}:=[d]\backslash[d-m_{+}^{0}]
.
Define

(9.17)

(\widetilde{u}_{1}^{+},\dots,\widetilde{u}^{+}_{d})=\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}(G_{+,1}^{-1/2}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2})

with

\mathfrak{L}_{+}=\mathrm{diag}(\|\mathfrak{z}_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1}^{+}\|,\dots,\|\mathfrak{z}_{m_{+}}^{+}\|)
,

G_{+,2}=(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}_{2}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1})^{*}(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}_{2}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1})
,
and
G_{+,1}=(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}_{1})^{*}(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}_{1})
, using the convention that

(\widetilde{u}_{1}^{+},\dots,\widetilde{u}^{+}_{d})=\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}G_{+,1}^{-1/2}
when

I_{+,2}=\emptyset
, and that

(\widetilde{u}_{1}^{+},\dots,\widetilde{u}^{+}_{d})=\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2}
when

I_{+,1}=\emptyset
.
Then,
uniformly for
z\in\Omega_{N}
,

(9.18)

\langle\widetilde{u}_{n}^{+}|\widetilde{u}_{m}^{+}\rangle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\delta_{n,m},&(n,m)\in I_{+,1}\times I_{+,1}\cup I_{+,2}\times I_{+,2}\\
\delta_{n,m}+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}),&(n,m)\in I_{+,1}\times I_{+,2}\cup I_{+,2}\times I_{+,1},\end{array}\right.

and

(9.19)

\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0},N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|\leqslant O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{e}^{-N_{0}\log C_{\beta}},&j\in I_{+,1},\\
\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{N_{0}}{N}\log N^{C_{\gamma}}},&j\in I_{+,2}.\end{array}\right.

•

Suppose that
d<0
. When
N_{-}>0
, let

A^{-}=(a_{1}^{-},\dots,a_{|d|}^{-})
be as in (
9.14
), and when

N_{-}\leqslant 0
put
A^{-}=I_{m_{-}}\in\mathds{C}^{m_{-}\times m_{-}}
.
Define

I_{-,2}:=[m_{-}^{0}]
and
I_{-,1}:=[-d]\backslash[m_{-}^{0}]
. Define

(9.20)

(\widetilde{u}_{1}^{-},\dots,\widetilde{u}^{-}_{|d|})=\mathfrak{Z}^{-}A^{-}(\mathfrak{L}_{-}^{-1}G_{-,1}^{-1/2}\oplus G_{-,2}^{-1/2})

with

\mathfrak{L}_{-}=\mathrm{diag}(\|\mathfrak{z}_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1}^{+}\|,\dots,\|\mathfrak{z}_{m_{+}}^{+}\|)
,

G_{-,1}=(\mathfrak{Z}^{-}A^{-}_{1}\mathfrak{L}_{-}^{-1})^{*}(\mathfrak{Z}^{-}A^{-}_{1}\mathfrak{L}_{-}^{-1})

and
G_{-,2}=(\mathfrak{Z}^{-}A^{-}_{2})^{*}(\mathfrak{Z}^{-}A^{-}_{2})
, using a similar notation convention
as above.
Then,
uniformly for
z\in\Omega_{N}
,

(9.21)

\langle\widetilde{u}_{n}^{-}|\widetilde{u}_{m}^{-}\rangle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\delta_{n,m},&(n,m)\in I_{+,1}\times I_{+,1}\cup I_{+,2}\times I_{+,2}\\
\delta_{n,m}+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}),&(n,m)\in I_{-,1}\times I_{-,2}\cup I_{-,2}\times I_{-,1},\end{array}\right.

and

(9.22)

\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,N-N_{0}-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{-}\|\leqslant O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{N_{0}}{N}\log N^{C_{\gamma}}},&j\in I_{-,2},\\
\mathrm{e}^{-N_{0}\log C_{\beta}},&j\in I_{-,1}.\end{array}\right.

Remark 9.4
.

When
d>0
, the normalized versions of the vectors defined in (
9.15
) will be termed as the pure states associated with
z
. Similarly for
d<0
the normalized version vectors (
9.16
) will be the pure states corresponding to
z
.

Thus, for
z
satisfying (
9.6
) we note that the pure states
\{\mathfrak{z}_{j}^{+}/\|\mathfrak{z}_{j}^{+}\|\}_{j=1}^{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}
are completely localized and its entries decay exponentially in
N
, while the remaining pure states decay and localize at a scale
N/\log N
. Similar conclusion holds for
z
’s satisfying (
9.7
).

Remark 9.5
.

For later use, let us record that for the Jordan block if
z
is inside the the unit disc then it has one pure state
\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}
, and
\widetilde{u}_{1}^{+}=\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}/\|\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}\|
.

We postpone the proof of Proposition
9.3

to after that of Proposition
9.6
.
Continuing, we notice that the
\widetilde{u}_{j}^{\pm}
defined in (
9.17
) resp. (
9.20
),
are the truncated kernel elements of
P_{[0,+\infty[}-z
resp.
P_{]-\infty,N-1]}-z
, separated into
two groups according to their decay behaviour and orthonormalized within their respective groups.
We could use them already as quasimodes for
P_{N}-z
. In fact,
it follows from (
9.19
) with
N_{0}=N-(N_{+}\lor 0+N_{-}\lor 0)
, that when
d>0

(9.23)

\|(P_{N}-z)\widetilde{u}^{+}_{j}\|\leqslant O(1)\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0},N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|\leqslant O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}},&j\in I_{+,1},\\
N^{-C_{\gamma}},&j\in I_{+,2},\end{array}\right.

and similarly from (
9.22
) when
d<0
.
Ultimately we want to use these quasimodes to obtain spectral gaps
between the

(|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0})
-th and the
(|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}+1)
-th, and between the

|d|
-th and
(|d|+1)
-th singular value of
P_{N}-z
. We will see, that these quasimodes will be sufficiently good for our purposes when
\gamma>2
. However, to obtain sufficiently fast decay
when
\gamma>1
we need to modify them.

Proposition 9.6
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
9.3
, we put
N_{0}=(1-1/\log N)(N-1)
, and we
have the following:

1. If
d>0
, let
\widetilde{u}^{+}_{j}
,
j=1,\dots,d
, be as in (
9.17
) and
define
\psi^{+}_{j}\in\mathds{C}^{N}
, by

(9.24)

\psi^{+}_{j}(\nu)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{u}^{+}_{j}(\nu),&j\in I_{+,1},\\
\mathbf{1}_{[0,N_{0}]}(\nu)\widetilde{u}^{+}_{j}(\nu)+\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0}+1,N-1]}(\nu)\frac{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{\nu}{N-1})}{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{N-1})}\widetilde{u}^{+}_{j}(\nu),&j\in I_{+,2}.\end{array}\right.

2. If
d<0
, let
\widetilde{u}^{-}_{j}
,
j=1,\dots,-d
, be as in (
9.20
) and
define
\psi^{-}_{j}\in\mathds{C}^{N}
, by

(9.25)

\psi^{-}_{j}(\nu)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbf{1}_{[0,N-N_{0}]}(\nu)\frac{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N-1-\nu}{N-1})}{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{N-1})}\widetilde{u}^{-}_{j}(\nu)+\mathbf{1}_{[N-N_{0}+1,N-1]}(\nu)\widetilde{u}^{-}_{j}(\nu),&j\in I_{-,2},\\
\widetilde{u}^{-}_{j}(\nu),&j\in I_{-,1}.\end{array}\right.

Then, for
i,j=1,\dots,|d|
, uniformly in
z\in\Omega_{N}
,

(9.26)

\langle\psi^{\mathrm{sign}(d)}_{i}|\psi^{\mathrm{sign}(d)}_{j}\rangle=\delta_{i,j}+\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{ when }i,j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}\\
O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}},&\text{ when }i,j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}\\
O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}}+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}),&\text{else},\end{array}\right.

and

(9.27)

\|(P_{N}-z)\psi_{i}^{\mathrm{sign}(d)}\|=O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}},&\text{ when }i\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}\\
\frac{\log N}{N}N^{-C_{\gamma}},&\text{ when }i\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}.\\
\end{array}\right.

We refer to the vectors
\widetilde{\psi}^{\pm}_{j}
as
quasimodes
.

Proof.

We will consider only the case when
d>0
, the case when
d<0
is similar.
Let
N>0
be large enough so that the conclusions of Proposition
9.3
hold.

1. First notice that

(9.28)

\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{N-1}\right)=\sin\frac{\pi}{2\log N}=\frac{\pi}{2\log N}(1+O((\log N)^{-2}).

Let
j=d-m_{+}^{0}+1,\dots,d
, then

\|\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}-\psi_{j}^{+}\|^{2}=\sum_{\nu=N_{0}+1}^{N-1}\left|1-\frac{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{\nu}{N-1})}{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{N-1})}\right|^{2}|\widetilde{u}^{+}_{j}(\nu)|^{2}\leqslant O((\log N)^{2})\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0}+1,N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|^{2}.

Applying (
9.19
), we get that

(9.29)

\|\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}-\psi_{j}^{+}\|\leqslant O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}}.

Remark 9.7
.

For later use we note here that the analogue of (
9.29
) in the case when
d<0
holds, i.e.

\|\widetilde{u}_{j}^{-}-\psi_{j}^{-}\|\leqslant O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}},\quad j\in I_{-,2}.

This, together with the
\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}
,
j=1,\dots,d
,
being normalized, see (
9.18
),
yields that

\|\psi_{j}^{+}\|=1+\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{ when }j\in I_{+,1}\\
O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}},&\text{ when }j\in I_{+,2}.\end{array}\right.

Using furthermore the fact that the
\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}
are almost orthogonal, see (
9.18
),
we find that

\begin{split}\langle\psi_{i}^{+}|\psi_{j}^{+}\rangle&=\langle\psi_{i}^{+}-\widetilde{u}_{i}^{+}|\psi_{j}\rangle+\langle\widetilde{u}_{i}^{+}|\psi_{j}^{+}-\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\rangle+\langle\widetilde{u}_{i}^{+}|\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\rangle\\
&=\delta_{i,j}+\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{ when }i,j\in I_{+,1},\\
O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}},&\text{ when }i,j\in I_{+,2}\\
O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}}+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}),&\text{else}.\end{array}\right.\end{split}

2. It remains to prove (
9.27
). Recall from (
9.17
) and Proposition
4.6
that
the

\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}=(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}(G_{+,1}^{-1/2}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2}))_{-,j}

are the truncated and normalized (in fact almost orthonormalized) kernel elements
of
P_{[0,+\infty[}-z
. Here, and in the sequel we denote by
A_{-,j}\in\mathds{C}^{n}

the
j
-th column of some
n\times m
matrix
A
as a vector in
\mathds{C}^{n}
.
Let
u_{k}\in\ell^{2}([0,+\infty[)
,
k=1,\dots,d
, be given by

(9.30)

u_{k}(\nu)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+})_{\nu,k},&0\leqslant\nu\leqslant N-1,\\
0,&\nu\geqslant N.\end{array}\right.

Then,

((P_{[0,+\infty[}-z)u_{k})(\nu)=0,\quad 0\leqslant\nu\leqslant N-1-N_{-}\lor 0.

By (
4.34
) we see that
N_{-}>0
since we work here with
d>0
.
To ease the notation we write
\widetilde{N}_{\pm}=N_{\pm}\lor 0
, and

\widetilde{a}_{k}=a_{k}-z\delta_{k,0}
when
a_{k}
exists, otherwise

\widetilde{a}_{k}=-z\delta_{k,0}
, so that

\sum_{k=-N_{-}}^{N_{+}}a_{k}\tau^{k}-z=\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\tau^{k}.

Thus, for
0\leqslant\nu\leqslant N-1-\widetilde{N}_{-}
,

(9.31)

\begin{split}(P_{N}-z)\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}(\nu)&=\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{[0,N-1]}(\nu-k)\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}(\nu-k)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qm5}}}{{=}}\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{[0,N-1]}(\nu-k)(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}(G_{+,1}^{-1/2}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2}))_{(\nu-k),j}\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{d}\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{[0,N-1]}(\nu-k)(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+})_{(\nu-k),n}(G_{+,1}^{-1/2}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2})_{n,j}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq-pkak1}}}{{=}}\sum_{n=1}^{d}(G_{+,1}^{-1/2}\oplus\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2})_{n,j}((P_{[0,+\infty[}-z)u_{n})(\nu)=0.\end{split}

Thus, for
0\leqslant\nu\leqslant N-1-\widetilde{N}_{-}
and
j\in I_{+,1}

(P_{N}-z)\psi_{j}^{+}(\nu)=(P_{N}-z)\widetilde{u}_{j}(\nu)=0.

Recall that
z\in\Omega\Subset\mathds{C}
is relatively compact. Hence,
applying (
9.19
) with
N_{0}=N-1-\widetilde{N}_{-}-\widetilde{N}_{+}
, we see
that, uniformly in
z\in\Omega_{N}
,

(9.32)

\|(P_{N}-z)\psi_{j}^{+}\|=O(1)\|\mathbf{1}_{[N-1-\widetilde{N}_{-}-\widetilde{N}_{+},N-1]}\psi_{i}^{+}\|=O(1)\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}},\penalty\ j\in I_{+,1},

Until further notice let
j\in I_{+,2}
. Equation (
9.31
) implies that
for
0\leqslant\nu\leqslant N_{0}-\widetilde{N}_{-}

(P_{N}-z)\psi_{j}^{+}(\nu)=(P_{N}-z)\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}(\nu)=0.

Next, by (
9.28
), we have for

N_{0}-(\widetilde{N}_{+}+\widetilde{N}_{-})<\nu\leqslant N_{0}+(\widetilde{N}_{+}+\widetilde{N}_{-})
that

(9.33)

\left|1-\frac{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{\nu}{N-1})}{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{N-1})}\right|\leqslant O(N^{-1}\log N)|N_{0}-\nu|=O(N^{-1}\log N).

Hence, for
N_{0}-\widetilde{N}_{-}<\nu\leqslant N_{0}+\widetilde{N}_{+}
, we get
by (
9.31
), (
9.33
) that

(9.34)

\begin{split}(P_{N}-z)\psi_{j}^{+}(\nu)&=\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\psi_{j}^{+}(\nu-k)\\
&=\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}(\nu-k)-\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\left(1-\frac{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{\nu-k}{N-1})}{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{N-1})}\right)\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}(\nu-k)\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0}+1,N-1]}(\nu-k)\\
&=O(N^{-1}\log N)\|\mathbf{1}_{I(\nu)}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|,\end{split}

where
I(\nu):=[\nu-\widetilde{N}_{+},\nu+\widetilde{N}_{-}]
.
For
N_{0}+\widetilde{N}_{+}<\nu\leqslant N-1-\widetilde{N}_{-}
we
get by (
9.31
) and a similar estimate as in (
9.33
), (
9.34
), that

(9.35)

\begin{split}(P_{N}-z)\psi_{j}^{+}(\nu)&=\frac{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{\nu}{N-1})}{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{N-1})}\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}(\nu-k)-\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\frac{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{\nu}{N-1})-\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{\nu-k}{N-1})}{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{N-1})}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}(\nu-k)\\
&=O(N^{-1}\log N)\|\mathbf{1}_{I(\nu)}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|.\end{split}

Now let
N-1-\widetilde{N}_{-}<\nu\leqslant N-1
. For
-\widetilde{N}_{-}\leqslant k\leqslant\widetilde{N}_{+}

we have that

\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{\nu-k}{N-1})\asymp N^{-1}
, so by (
9.28
) we get that

(9.36)

\begin{split}(P_{N}-z)\psi_{j}^{+}(\nu)&=\sum_{k=-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\widetilde{a}_{k}\frac{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{\nu-k}{N-1})}{\cos(\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{N-1})}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}(\nu-k)\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0}+1,N-1]}(\nu-k)\\
&=O(N^{-1}\log N)\|\mathbf{1}_{I(\nu)}\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0}+1,N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|.\end{split}

Combining (
9.31
), (
9.34
), (
9.35
) and (
9.36
) we obtain that

(9.37)

\begin{split}\|(P_{N}-z)\psi_{j}^{+}\|^{2}&\leqslant O((N^{-1}\log N)^{2})\sum_{\nu=N_{0}-\widetilde{N}_{-}}^{N-1}\|\mathbf{1}_{I(\nu)}\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0}-(\widetilde{N}_{+}+\widetilde{N}_{-}),N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|^{2}\\
&\leqslant O((N^{-1}\log N)^{2})\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0}-(\widetilde{N}_{+}+\widetilde{N}_{-}),N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|^{2}.\end{split}

Applying now (
9.19
) with
N_{0}=(1-1/\log N)N-(\widetilde{N}_{+}+\widetilde{N}_{-})
,
we get that

(9.38)

\|(P_{N}-z)\psi_{j}^{+}\|=O(N^{-1}\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}}

which concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proof of Proposition
9.3
.

We will only consider the case when
d>0
. The case
d<0
can be proven similarly
with the obvious modifications. In what follows, we will assume that
N>0
is sufficiently
large, depending only on
\Omega
and the constants in Assumption
9.1
,
without us mentioning this at every occurrence. Similarly, we note that all constants
C>0
and
error estimates will be uniform in
z\in\Omega_{N}
, and in fact they depend only on the above mentioned
parameters, so we shall only mention this at a few important points.

1. Since the roots of
p(\zeta)-z
depend smoothly on
z\in\Omega
, see the discussion
above (
9.3
), we have that either
0\in p^{-1}(z_{0})
or we may take
\Omega
sufficiently
small so that
0\notin p^{-1}(\Omega)
. In both cases
0\notin p^{-1}(\Omega_{N})
.
Hence, we have that the symbol
p(\zeta)-z
satisfies for all
z\in\Omega_{N}
the assumptions of
Case 1 of Lemma
4.1
, see also (
4.40
). We then know
that
m_{+}>0
since
d>0
, see (
4.34
).

2. Recall from (
8.24
) the
m_{+}\times m_{+}
matrices
V=V_{+}

and
\Lambda=\Lambda_{+}
(with
\widehat{m}_{0}=0
). Then, for
r=1,2
and
\psi_{1}\in\mathds{C}^{d-m_{+}^{0}}
,

\psi_{2}\in\mathds{C}^{m_{+}^{0}}
,

(9.39)

\|\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{r}^{+}\psi_{r}\|^{2}\geqslant\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor(N-2)/m_{+}\rfloor-1}\|V\Lambda^{jm_{+}}A_{r}^{+}\psi_{r}\|^{2}.

Let

s_{1}(V)\geqslant\cdots\geqslant s_{m_{+}}(V)\geqslant 0
denote the singular values of
V
. Since

|\det V|=\prod_{1}^{m_{+}}s_{j}(V)
, and
s_{1}(V)=\|V\|\leqslant m_{+}
,
it follows that

(9.40)

s_{m_{+}}(V)\geqslant\frac{|\det V|}{m_{+}^{m_{+}}}=\frac{\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant m_{+}}|\zeta_{i}^{+}-\zeta_{j}^{+}|}{m_{+}^{m_{+}}}\geqslant\frac{1}{C}.

Here, in the equality we used the formula for the determinant of the Van der Monde
matrix
V
. Moreover, the constant in the last inequality is uniform
N
and in
z\in\Omega_{N}

by (
9.3
).
Since
\Lambda
is a diagonal matrix, we get from (
9.39
) that

(9.41)

\|\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{r}^{+}\psi_{r}\|^{2}\geqslant s^{2}_{m_{+}}(V)\sum_{k=1}^{m_{+}}|(A_{r}^{+}\psi_{r})(k)|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor(N-2)/m_{+}\rfloor-1}|\zeta^{+}_{k}|^{2jm_{+}}.

Putting

(9.42)

d_{k}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor(N-2)/m_{+}\rfloor-1}|\zeta^{+}_{k}|^{2jm_{+}}=\frac{1-|\zeta^{+}_{k}|^{2\lfloor(N-2)/m_{+}\rfloor m_{+}}}{1-|\zeta^{+}_{k}|^{2m_{+}}},

and
D=\mathrm{diag}(d_{1},\dots,d_{m_{+}})
, we get from (
9.41
) that

(9.43)

\|\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{r}^{+}\psi_{r}\|\geqslant s_{m_{+}}(V)\|DA_{r}^{+}\psi_{r}\|.

Observe that the smallest singular value of
D
is
\min_{k}d_{k}\geqslant 1
. Recall (
9.13
). Provided that

d>m_{+}^{0}
, we notice that
A^{+}_{1}
is an isometry since the columns of
X_{+}
are orthonormal.
Thus, it follows from (
9.43
), (
9.40
) that

(9.44)

\|\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{1}^{+}\psi_{1}\|\geqslant\frac{1}{C}\|\psi_{1}\|,

so the smallest singular value of
G_{+,1}
is at least
1/C
, which,
G_{+,1}
being self-adjoint, implies that
it is bijective with inverse bounded in norm by
C
. In particular, we have that

(9.45)

\|A^{+}_{1}G_{+,1}^{-1/2}\|=\sqrt{C}.

3. Working with
m_{+}^{0}\geqslant 1
, we turn to
G_{+,2}
and we will show first
that for
n\geqslant d-m_{+}^{0}+1
the
u^{+}_{n}

are, up to a small error, given by
\mathfrak{z}_{n}/\|\mathfrak{z}_{n}\|
.
First, we compute

(9.46)

F_{n,m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}|\langle\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{n}\mid\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{m}\rangle|=\left|\sum_{0}^{N-1}(\zeta^{+}_{n}\overline{\zeta}^{+}_{m})^{\nu}\right|=\left(\frac{(1-|\zeta_{n}^{+}|^{2N})(1-|\zeta_{n}^{+}|^{2N})+|(\zeta_{n}^{+})^{N}-(\zeta_{m}^{+})^{N}|^{2}}{(1-|\zeta_{n}^{+}|^{2})(1-|\zeta_{m}^{+}|^{2})+|\zeta_{n}^{+}-\zeta_{m}^{+}|^{2}}\right)^{1/2}.

By (
9.6
), (
9.5
) and (
9.3
) we find that

(9.47)

F_{n,m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\asymp 1,&\text{ when }1\leqslant n,m\leqslant m_{+}-m_{+}^{0},\\
\asymp 1,&\text{ when }n\neq m,\\
\asymp\frac{N}{\log N},&\text{ when }m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1\leqslant n=m\leqslant m_{+}.\end{array}\right.

Second, notice that by (
9.9
), (
9.10
), we have that

T:=-\mathfrak{B}_{+}^{-1}(\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1},\dots,\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}})
has norm

\|T\|=O(1)
, depending only on
\Omega
. Recalling the definition of
\Delta_{+}
from the
discussion before (
9.13
), we see that

(9.48)

\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{2}^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}T\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}+\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1},

where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}=(\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+},\dots,\mathfrak{z}_{N_{+}}^{+})

and
\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}=(\mathfrak{z}_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1}^{+},\dots,\mathfrak{z}_{m_{+}}^{+})
. Using
(
9.47
), we get that

(9.49)

\begin{split}G_{+,2}&=\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}(\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+})^{*}\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}+\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}\left((\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+})^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}T+(\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}T)^{*}\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}+(\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}T)^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}T\right)\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}\\
&=1+O(1)\frac{\log N}{N},\end{split}

where the
O(1)
term denotes an
m_{+}^{0}\times m_{+}^{0}
matrix with norm bounded by

O(1)
, depending only on
\Omega
and the constants in Assumption
9.1
.
Hence, for
N>0
sufficiently large (depending only
on
\Omega
and the constants in Assumption
9.1
), we have that
G_{+,2}
is
invertible and

(9.50)

G_{+,2}^{-1/2}=1+O(1)\frac{\log N}{N}.

Hence, using again (
9.47
) and denoting by
M_{-,n}
the
n
-th column of a matrix, we
get that for
n\in I_{+,2}

(9.51)

\begin{split}\widetilde{u}^{+}_{n}&=\left(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{2}^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2}\right)_{-,n}\\
&=\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}T\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2}\right)_{-,n}+\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{Z}}^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2}\right)_{-,n}=\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{n}^{+}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{n}^{+}\|}+O(1)\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}},\end{split}

where the
O(1)
term denotes a vector in
\mathds{C}^{N}
whose
\ell^{2}
norm is bounded by
O(1)
.
In particular, (
9.44
) and (
9.50
) imply that (
9.17
) is well-defined.

Remark 9.8
.

For a later use we note that the analogue of (
9.51
) in the case when
d<0
holds, i.e.

\widetilde{u}^{-}_{n}=\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{n}^{-}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{n}^{-}\|}+O(1)\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}},\quad n\in I_{-,2}.

4. Since
(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{1}^{+}G_{+,1}^{-1/2})^{*}(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{1}^{+}G_{+,1}^{-1/2})=1
,
and
(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{2}^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}G_{+,2}^{-1/2})^{*}(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{2}^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}G_{+,2}^{-1/2})=1
, we confirm the first line of (
9.18
). To prove the second line, let
(n,m)\in I_{+,1}\times I_{+,2}
.
Equations (
9.51
) and (
9.17
) then yield that

(9.52)

\begin{split}\langle\widetilde{u}^{+}_{n}|\widetilde{u}^{+}_{m}\rangle&=\left\langle\mathfrak{Z}^{+}(A^{+}_{1}G_{+,1}^{-1/2})_{-,n}\Big|\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{m}^{+}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{m}^{+}\|}\right\rangle+O(1)\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}\\
&=\sum_{k=1}^{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}\langle\mathfrak{z}_{k}^{+}|\mathfrak{z}_{m}^{+}\rangle\|\mathfrak{z}_{m}^{+}\|^{-1}(A^{+}_{1}G_{+,1}^{-1/2})_{k,n}+O(1)\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qm12aa},\eqref{qm12c}}}{{\leqslant}}O(1)\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}.\end{split}

The other case of the second line of (
9.18
) follows from symmetry.

5. At last,
we turn to proving (
9.19
). Let
j\in I_{+,1}
.
Using Hölder’s inequality, we compute

(9.53)

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0},N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|^{2}&=\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}|(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}G_{+,1}^{-1/2})_{\nu,j}|^{2}\leqslant\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\left|\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}-m_{0}^{+}}(\mathfrak{Z}^{+})_{\nu,\mu}(A^{+}G_{+,1}^{-1/2})_{\mu,j}\right|^{2}\\
&\leqslant\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\left(\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}-m_{0}^{+}}|(\mathfrak{Z}^{+})_{\nu,\mu}|^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}-m_{0}^{+}}|(A^{+}G_{+,1}^{-1/2})_{\mu,j}|^{2}\right).\end{split}

Since the right most term in the last line is controlled by the Hillbert-Schmidt norm of the corresponding
matrices, we get by using (
9.45
) and summing the geometric series that

(9.54)

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0},N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|^{2}&\leqslant\|A^{+}G_{+,1}^{-1/2}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{HS}}\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\left(\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}-m_{0}^{+}}|(\mathfrak{Z}^{+})_{\nu,\mu}|^{2}\right)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qm12aa}}}{{\leqslant}}O(1)\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\left(\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}-m_{0}^{+}}|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2\nu}\right)=O(1)\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}-m_{0}^{+}}|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2N_{0}}\frac{1-|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2(N-N_{0})}}{1-|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2}}.\end{split}

Since
|\zeta_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}|\leqslant 1/C_{\beta}<1
, we have that
for
\mu=1,\dots,m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}
,

(9.55)

\frac{1-|\zeta^{+}_{\mu}|^{2(N-N_{0})}}{1-|\zeta^{+}_{\mu}|^{2}}\leqslant\frac{C_{\beta}}{C_{\beta}-1}.

Similarly we see that

(9.56)

|\zeta^{+}_{\mu}|^{2N_{0}}\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-2N_{0}\log C_{\beta}}.

Combining this with (
9.54
) and (
9.55
) yields that

\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0},N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|\leqslant O(1)\mathrm{e}^{-N_{0}\log C_{\beta}},

uniformly in
z\in\Omega_{N}
, and we conclude the first line in
(
9.19
).

It remains to prove the second line of (
9.19
). Recall the definition of
A_{2}^{+}
(
9.13
),
and notice that

(9.57)

\begin{split}A_{2}^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}&=\begin{pmatrix}-\mathfrak{B}^{-1}(\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1},\dots,\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}})\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}\\
\Delta_{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}\\
\end{pmatrix}\\
&=\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{+}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}-\mathfrak{B}^{-1}(\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1},\dots,\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}})\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}\\
\Delta_{+}\\
\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{+}^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{2}^{+}\end{split}

where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{+}:=1_{N_{+}}\oplus\mathrm{diag}(\|\mathfrak{z}_{N_{+}+1}^{+}\|,\dots,\|\mathfrak{z}_{m_{+}}^{+}\|)
. Furthermore, it follows from
(
9.9
), (
9.10
) and (
9.47
) that

(9.58)

\|\mathfrak{B}^{-1}(\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1},\dots,\mathfrak{A}^{+}_{m_{+}})\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}\|=O(1)\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}},

which implies that

(9.59)

\|\widetilde{A}_{2}^{+}\|=O(1).

Using the commutation relation (
9.57
), we compute for
j\in I_{+,2}
that

(9.60)

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0},N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|^{2}&=\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}|(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A_{2}^{+}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}G_{+,2}^{-1/2})_{\nu,j}|^{2}\leqslant\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\left|\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}}(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{+}^{-1})_{\nu,\mu}(\widetilde{A}_{2}^{+}G_{+,2}^{-1/2})_{\mu,j}\right|^{2}\\
&\leqslant\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\left(\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}}|(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{+}^{-1})_{\nu,\mu}|^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}}|(\widetilde{A}^{+}G_{+}^{-1/2})_{\mu,j}|^{2}\right).\end{split}

Thus, by (
9.59
), (
9.50
)

(9.61)

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0},N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|^{2}&\leqslant\|\widetilde{A}^{+}G_{+}^{-1/2}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\left(\sum_{\mu=1}^{m_{+}}|(\mathfrak{Z}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{+}^{-1})_{\nu,\mu}|^{2}\right)\\
&\leqslant O(1)\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\left(\sum_{\mu=1}^{N_{+}}|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2\nu}+\sum_{\mu=N_{+}+1}^{m_{+}}\frac{|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2\nu}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\mu}^{+}\|^{2}}\right).\end{split}

Using (
9.47
), we get by a computation similar to (
9.55
) and (
9.56
) that

(9.62)

\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\left(\sum_{\mu=1}^{N_{+}}|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2\nu}+\sum_{\mu=N_{+}+1}^{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}\frac{|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2\nu}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\mu}^{+}\|^{2}}\right)=O(1)\mathrm{e}^{-N_{0}\log C_{\beta}}.

On the other hand, using (
9.46
) and the fact that
|\zeta_{m_{+}}|<1
,
we find
\mu=m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1,\dots,m_{+}
that

(9.63)

\sum_{\nu=N_{0}}^{N-1}\frac{|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2\nu}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\mu}^{+}\|^{2}}=|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2N_{0}}\frac{1-|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2(N-N_{0})}}{1-|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2N}}\leqslant|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2N_{0}}.

By (
9.5
), we see that

(9.64)

|\zeta_{\mu}^{+}|^{2N_{0}}\leqslant\exp\left(2N_{0}\log\left(1-C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N}{N}\right)\right)\leqslant\exp\left(-2N_{0}C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N}{N}\right)\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{2N_{0}}{N}\log N^{C_{\gamma}}},

which, together with (
9.63
), (
9.62
) and (
9.61
) yields that
for
N>0
sufficiently large

(9.65)

\|\mathbf{1}_{[N_{0},N-1]}\widetilde{u}_{j}^{+}\|^{2}\leqslant O(1)\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{2N_{0}}{N}\log N^{C_{\gamma}}},

uniformly in
z\in\Omega_{N}
, and we conclude the first second line in (
9.19
).
∎

10.
Singular values and vectors

We provide in this section various estimates on singular values and vectors
of
P_{N}-z
. The section is divided to subsections, dealing respectively
with small singular values, singular vectors, large singular values, and the smallest singular value and Hilbert-Schmidt norm of the matrices appearing in the resolvent expansion of the matrices discussed in Section
3
.

10.1.
Small singular values

We will work under the same assumptions as discussed in the beginning of
Section
9
.
We will use the quasimodes constructed in Proposition
9.6

to obtain an
upper bound on the
|d|
-th
smallest singular values of
P_{N}-z

corresponding to the decay speed of the quasimodes. We will also show with the help
of Theorem
8.1
that the
(|d|+1)
-th singular
value of
P_{N}-z
is significantly larger than the
|d|
-th one,
which leads to a
spectral gap. This is known as
splitting phenomenon
, see for instance

[
15
, Section 9.2]
. This phenomenon has typically been investigated for
z
being at
a fixed distance from
p(S^{1})
or for fixed symbols. In
that case it
is known that
the smallest
|d|

singular values are of order
O(\mathrm{e}^{-N/C})

whereas the
(|d|+1)
-th
singular value is bounded from
below by some small
N
independent constant. What is new here is that we allow the
spectral parameter
z
to be at an
N
-dependent distance from
p(S^{1})
,
which leads to a significantly smaller spectral gap.

Moreover, we will show that due to the assumptions (
9.6
), (
9.7
),
there exists a second spectral gap between the first
(|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0})

singular values, which are exponentially small in
N
, and the
(|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}+1)
-th
singular value, which is at most polynomially small in
N
.

Both spectral gaps will be crucial in describing the localization of
the eigenvectors of
P^{\delta}_{N}
.

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
z_{0}\in p(S^{1})
be as in (
9.1
) and let

\Omega\Subset\mathds{C}
be a sufficiently small open simply connected relatively
compact neighborhood of a point
z_{0}
satisfying (
9.2
).
Let
\Omega_{N}\Subset\Omega\backslash p(S^{1})
be as in Assumption

9.1
. For
z\in\Omega_{N}
let
t_{1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant t_{N}
denote
the eigenvalues of
\sqrt{(P_{N}-z)^{*}(P_{N}-z)}
.
Then there exists a constant
0<C<\infty
such that, for all
N
large enough (depending only
on
\Omega
and the constants in Assumption
9.1
) and for all
z\in\Omega_{N}

(10.1)

0\leqslant t_{1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant t_{|d|}\leqslant C\frac{\log N}{N}N^{-C_{\gamma}},

and

(10.2)

t_{|d|+1}\geqslant\frac{1}{C}\frac{\log N}{N}.

Additionally, if
|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}>0
, then

(10.3)

0\leqslant t_{1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant t_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}\leqslant C\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}},

and if
m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}>0

(10.4)

\frac{1}{C}\frac{\log N}{N}N^{-C_{\alpha}}\leqslant t_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}+1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant t_{|d|}\leqslant C\frac{\log N}{N}N^{-C_{\gamma}}.

Remark 10.2
.

If in Assumption
9.1
we have that
|\zeta_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1}^{+}|=|\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}|
(in case
d>0
) or

|\zeta_{m_{-}^{0}+1}^{-}|=|\zeta_{m_{-}}^{-}|
(in case
d<0
) then clearly one can take
C_{\alpha}=C_{\gamma}+\epsilon
for any
\epsilon>0
,
improving upon (
10.4
). By (
5.85
), this will always be the case for us.

Proof.

Recall that for a square
N\times N
matrix
A
,
t_{j}(A)

denote the eigenvalues of
\sqrt{A^{*}A}
(ordered non-decreasingly) and
s_{j}(A)

denote the singular values (ordered non-increasingly), see
(
1.13
).
By the singular value decomposition of the square matrix

A
, we have that

t_{j}(A)=t_{j}(A^{*})
and

s_{j}(A)=s_{j}(A^{*})
. Recall
the Ky Fan inequalities, see for instance

[
32
]
: for two square matrices
A,B
we have

(10.5)

\begin{split}&s_{n+m-1}(A+B)\leqslant s_{n}(A)+s_{m}(B),\\
&s_{n+m-1}(AB)\leqslant s_{n}(A)s_{m}(B).\end{split}

Further, if
A
is an invertible
N\times N
matrix, then

s_{n}(A^{-1})=\frac{1}{s_{N-n+1}(A)},\quad n=1,\dots,N.

From (
4.6
) we know that the symbol of the adjoint
\mathrm{Op}(p)^{*}

is given by
\overline{\widetilde{p}}(\omega)=\overline{p}(1/\omega)
. So
in view of Lemma
4.1
, the roots of
\overline{p}(1/\omega)-\overline{z}
, or
equivalently the roots of
p(1/\overline{\omega})-{z}
are given by

\omega^{\mp}_{j}=1/\overline{\zeta}_{j}^{\,\pm}
,
where
\zeta_{j}^{\pm}
are the roots of
p(\zeta)-z
. Hence, the discussion and results
of Section
4
valid for

p(\zeta)-z
remain valid for
\overline{p}(1/\omega)-\overline{z}
with the roles of

m_{+},N_{+}
, and
m_{-},N_{-}
exchanged. Furthermore, the roles of the assumption (
9.6
)
and (
9.7
) are then interchanged. This argument will allow us to reduce the number
cases of symbols
p-z
we need to consider, since we may always pass to studying the singular
values of the adjoint
(P_{N}-z)^{*}
. In particular, in the rest of the proof we may and will
assume that
d>0
for
z\in\Omega_{N}
.

In what follows, we will work with
N>0
sufficiently
large, depending only on
\Omega
and the constants in Assumption
9.1
,
without us mentioning this dependence at every occurrence. Similar, we note that all
error estimates will be uniform in
z\in\Omega_{N}
, and in fact they depend only on the above mentioned
parameters, so we shall only mention this at a few important points.

1. Let
z\in\Omega_{N}
. We begin with the upper bound on
t_{d}
.
Let
\psi^{+}_{j}
,
j=1,\dots,d
be as in (
9.24
),
and note that for
N>0
sufficiently large, they are linearly independent. Indeed
if they were not, then there would exist an
0\neq a=(a_{1},\dots,a_{d})\in\mathds{C}^{d|}

such that
a_{1}\psi^{+}_{1}+\dots+a_{d}\psi^{+}_{d}=0
.
Let
j_{0}
be such that
|a_{j_{0}}|\geqslant|a_{j}|
for
j\neq j_{0}
, with
|a_{j_{0}}|>0
.
Since the

\psi^{+}_{j}
are almost-orthonormal by (
9.26
), we find that

(10.6)

1+O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}}=-\sum_{j\in[d],j\neq j_{0}}\frac{a_{j}}{a_{j_{0}}}\langle\psi^{+}_{j}|\psi^{+}_{j_{0}}\rangle=O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}}+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}),

a contradiction.

Let
