K_{d}\subset\mathds{C}^{N}
denote the linear subspace of
\mathds{C}^{N}
spanned by the
\psi_{j}^{+}
and
notice that it has dimension
d
, for
N>0
sufficiently large. For any
\psi\in K_{d}
,
we
write
\psi=\sum_{j=1}^{d}a_{j}\psi^{+}_{j}
. If
\|\psi\|=1
,
then we get by (
9.26
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that for
N>0
sufficiently large,

1=\sum_{j=1}^{d}|a_{j}|^{2}\|\psi^{+}_{j}\|^{2}+\ \sum_{i\neq j}a_{i}\overline{a}_{j}\langle\psi^{+}_{i}|\psi^{+}_{j}\rangle=\|a\|^{2}(1+\hat{\epsilon}_{N}),

where
\hat{\epsilon}_{N}=O(1)(N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N+(\log N/N)^{1/2})
and the constant is uniform in
z\in\Omega_{N}
and independent of
\psi
.
Thus, for
N>0
large enough,

\|a\|^{2}=(1+\hat{\epsilon}_{N})
,
uniformly in the choice of
z\in\Omega_{N}
and
\psi
.
The min-max principle and (
9.27
)
yield that

\begin{split}t_{d}^{2}&=\min\limits_{L:\dim L=d}\max\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\in L\\
\|\psi\|=1\end{subarray}}\|(P_{N}-z)\psi\|^{2}\leqslant\max\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\in K_{d}\\
\|\psi\|=1\end{subarray}}\|(P_{N}-z)\psi\|^{2}\\
&\leqslant\max\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\in K_{d}\\
\|\psi\|=1\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{1}^{d}|a_{j}|\cdot\|(P_{N}-z)\psi^{+}_{j}\|\right)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qn4}}}{{\leqslant}}O(1)\left(\frac{\log N}{N}N^{-C_{\gamma}}\right)^{2},\end{split}

and we conclude the upper bound in (
10.1
) and (
10.4
).

Suppose that
d>m_{+}^{0}
and recall (
9.6
) and (
9.7
).
Letting
K_{d-m_{+}^{0}}
denote
the linear subspace of
\mathds{C}^{N}
spanned by

\psi^{+}_{1},\dots,\psi^{+}_{d-m_{+}^{0}}
, and recalling
from (
9.26
) that these vectors are orthonormal,
we deduce similarly as above from the min-max principle and
(
9.27
), that

t_{d-m_{+}^{0}}^{2}\leqslant\max\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\in K_{d-m_{+}^{0}}\\
\|\psi\|=1\end{subarray}}\|(P_{N}-z)\psi\|^{2}\leqslant\sum_{1}^{d-m_{+}^{0}}\|(P_{N}-z)\psi^{+}_{j}\|^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qn4}}}{{\leqslant}}O(\mathrm{e}^{-2N\log C_{\beta}}),

and we conclude (
10.3
).

2. We may write
\Omega
as the disjoint union

\Omega=\Omega^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,(p(S^{1})\cap\Omega)\,\dot{\cup}\,\Omega^{\prime\prime}

so that
\Omega_{N}\subset\Omega^{\prime}
. In particular
\mathrm{ind}_{p(S^{1})}(z)=d

for all
z\in\Omega^{\prime}
.

The rest of the proof will be concerned with the lower bounds on
t_{d+1}
and
t_{d-m_{+}^{0}+1}

for
z\in\Omega_{N}
,
and we will have to consider the two cases of
N_{\pm}>0
or
N_{+}\leqslant 0
.
Note that by (
4.15
),
these are the only cases we need to consider,
since
d>0
while
by Remark

4.2
and (
4.34
), we have that
d\leqslant 0
if
N_{-}<0
.
We note here that in both cases

\infty\notin p^{-1}(\Omega)
, see (
4.15
) and Lemma
4.1
.

Recall from the
discussion after (
9.2
) that all roots
\zeta_{z}
of
p(\zeta)-z

depend smoothly on
z\in\Omega
. So, when either
N_{\pm}>0
, or
N_{+}\leqslant 0
and

0\notin p^{-1}(z_{0})
, we see that
\{0,\infty\}\notin p^{-1}(\Omega)
for
\Omega
sufficiently
small. On the other hand, when
N_{+}\leqslant 0
and
0\in p^{-1}(z_{0})
, then by the smooth
dependence of the roots on
z
that
0\notin p^{-1}(\Omega^{\prime})
, after possibly shrinking
\Omega
.
Hence, for
\Omega
sufficiently small, there exists a constant
C>0
such
that any root
\zeta_{z}
satisfies

(10.7)

0<|\zeta_{z}|\leqslant C,\text{ for all }z\in\Omega^{\prime}.

Since the symbol
p(\zeta)-z
for
z\in\Omega^{\prime}
satisfies the assumptions
of Case 1 of Lemma
4.1
, we may order its roots as in (
4.26
). So, in combination
with (
10.7
) we get that

(10.8)

0<|\zeta_{1}^{+}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}|<1<|\zeta_{1}^{-}|\leqslant\cdots\leqslant|\zeta_{m_{-}}^{-}|\leqslant C,\text{ for all }z\in\Omega^{\prime}.

By (
4.34
) we know that
m_{+}\geqslant d>0
, so we work in (
10.8
) with the
convention that when
m_{-}=0
then only the estimates on the existing roots
in (
10.8
) apply.

Recall Assumption
9.1
, and as in Theorem
8.1
, let

\Theta
denote the Heaviside function, and let

(10.9)

\widehat{m}_{0}\in\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{0,m_{+}^{0}\},&m_{+}^{0}>0\\
\{0\},&m_{+}^{0}=0.\end{array}\right.

To shorten the notation we will write
\Theta:=\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})
whenever convenient.
The case
\widehat{m}_{0}=0
will be used to prove the lower bound in (
10.2
),
and the case
\widehat{m}_{0}=m_{+}^{0}
for the lower bound in (
10.4
).
Write
\widetilde{N}_{+}=N_{+}\lor 0
. For
\zeta\in S^{1}
and
z\in\Omega^{\prime}
,
we can write the symbol as

(10.10)

p(\zeta)-z=\zeta^{-\widetilde{N}_{+}}\left(\sum_{0}^{\widetilde{N}_{+}+N_{-}}a_{N_{+}-j}\,\zeta^{j}-z\zeta^{\widetilde{N}_{+}}\right)=\zeta^{m_{+}-\widetilde{N}_{+}-m^{0}_{+}\Theta}q(\zeta)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{at9.0}}}{{=}}\zeta^{d-m^{0}_{+}\Theta}q(\zeta),

where

(10.11)

q(\zeta)=\sum_{-m_{-}-m^{0}_{+}\Theta}^{m_{+}-m^{0}_{+}\Theta}q_{j}\,\zeta^{-j}:=a_{-N_{-}}\prod_{1}^{m_{+}-m^{0}_{+}\Theta}(1-\zeta^{+}_{j}/\zeta)\prod_{m_{+}-m^{0}_{+}\Theta+1}^{m_{+}}(\zeta-\zeta^{+}_{j})\prod_{1}^{m_{-}}(\zeta-\zeta^{-}_{j})

Here
q_{m_{+}-m^{0}_{+}\Theta}=a_{-N_{-}}\prod_{1}^{m_{+}}(-\zeta_{j}^{+})\prod_{1}^{m_{-}}(-\zeta_{j}^{-})
,
and
q_{-m_{-}-m^{0}_{+}\Theta}=a_{-N_{-}}
. So, by (
10.8
) and (
4.15
), we conclude
that there exists some constant
C>0
such that for all
z\in\Omega^{\prime}

q_{m_{+}-m^{0}_{+}\Theta}\neq 0,\quad|q_{-m_{-}-m^{0}_{+}\Theta}|\geqslant 1/C,\quad|q_{j}|\leqslant C.

Equation (
10.11
) shows that there are no roots of
q
on
S^{1}
and, by performing a
computation as in (
4.34
), that
\mathrm{ind}_{q(S^{1})}(0)=\widehat{m}_{0}
for all
z\in\Omega_{N}
. This,
together with (
10.7
), (
9.3
), (
9.5
) and (
9.6
) shows that
q
,
\Omega_{N}

and
\Omega^{\prime}
satisfy the assumptions of Theorem
8.1
with
\widehat{m}_{0}
as in
(
10.9
),
C_{0}=C_{\alpha}
,
C_{1}=C_{\beta}
,
C_{2}=C_{\gamma}
,
and therefore there
exists a constant
C>0
such that for
N>0
sufficiently large and all

z\in\Omega_{N}
,

(10.12)

s_{N}(P_{N}(q))\geqslant\frac{1}{C}\frac{\log N}{N}N^{-C_{\alpha}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})}.

When
d=m_{+}^{0}>0
then
s_{N-j+1}=t_{j}
, and
we immediately conclude the lower
bound in (
10.4
) using that

\widehat{m}_{0}=m_{+}^{0}
. Hence, from now on we may
and will assume that

d>m_{+}^{0}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})
, see also Assumption
9.1
.

Recall the definition of
\chi_{n}
from (
4.10
).
As noted after (
4.12
) we have that

H(\chi_{n})=0
when
n\leqslant 0
and that
H(\chi_{n})
has rank
n
,
when
n>0
. So by (
4.14
) and (
10.10
) we get that

(10.13)

P_{N}(p-z)P_{N}(\chi_{m^{0}_{+}\Theta-d})=P_{N}(q)-\Pi_{N}H(p-z)H(\chi_{d-m^{0}_{+}\Theta})\Pi_{N},

where the second term on the right hand side has rank
d-m^{0}_{+}\Theta
.
Since
s_{1}(P_{N}(\chi_{m^{0}_{+}\Theta-d}))=\|P_{N}(\chi_{m^{0}_{+}\Theta-d})\|=1
, we
get by (
10.13
) and the second Ky Fan
inequality in (
10.5
) that
for any
n=1,\dots,N
,

(10.14)

s_{n}(P_{N}(p-z))=s_{n}(P_{N}(p-z))\,s_{1}(P_{N}(\chi_{m^{0}_{+}\Theta-d}))\geqslant s_{n}(P_{N}(q)-\Pi_{N}H(p-z)H(\chi_{d-m^{0}_{+}\Theta})\Pi_{N}).

By interleaving inequalities for singular values, see e.g.

[
12
, Problem III.6.4]
,
for any matrices

A,K\in\mathds{C}^{N\times N}

with
K
of rank
m\in\{0,\dots,N-1\}
,

(10.15)

s_{N-m}(A)\geqslant s_{N}(A+K).

Applying this estimate with

A=P_{N}(q)-\Pi_{N}H(p-z)H(\chi_{d-m^{0}_{+}\Theta})\Pi_{N},\penalty\ K=\Pi_{N}H(p-z)H(\chi_{d-m^{0}_{+}\Theta})\Pi_{N},

and
m=d-m^{0}_{+}\Theta
, yields together with (
10.14
) and (
10.12
)
that for
N>0
large enough (so that (
10.12
) holds)

s_{N-d+m^{0}_{+}\Theta}(P_{N}(p-z))\geqslant s_{N}(P_{N}(q))\geqslant\frac{1}{C}\frac{\log N}{N}N^{-C_{\alpha}\Theta(\widehat{m}_{0})},

for all
z\in\Omega_{N}
. Since
s_{N-j+1}=t_{j}
, we get (
10.2
) by taking
\widehat{m}_{0}=0
,
and we obtain
the lower bound in (
10.4
), when
d>m_{+}^{0}>0
,
by taking
\widehat{m}_{0}=m_{+}^{0}
.
This ends the proof of Proposition
10.1
.
∎

10.2.
Singular vectors

Throughout this section we will continue to work with
z\in\Omega_{N}
satisfying
Assumption
9.1
and we
will denote the orthonormal eigenvectors of
(P_{N}-z)^{*}(P_{N}-z)

corresponding to the eigenvalues
0\leqslant t_{1}^{2}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant t_{N}^{2}
by

e_{1},\dots,e_{N}
. Using the spectral gap from Proposition
10.1

we will show that the quasimodes from Proposition
9.6
span, up to a small
error, the same space as the eigenvectors
e_{1},\dots,e_{|d|}
. More precisely
we will show that the eigenvectors
e_{1},\dots,e_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}
are
well approximated by a linear combination of the quasimodes

\psi_{1},\dots,\psi_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}
, whereas the eigenvectors

e_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}+1},\dots,e_{|d|}
are well approximated by a linear combination
of
\psi_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}+1},\dots,\psi_{|d|}
.

Proposition 10.3
.

Consider the setup as in Proposition
10.1
.
Let
\Pi_{\kappa}=\mathbf{1}_{[0,\kappa^{2}]}((P_{N}-z)^{*}(P_{N}-z))
,
\kappa\geqslant 0
,
z\in\Omega_{N}
, be
the spectral projector onto the eigenspace of
(P_{N}-z)^{*}(P_{N}-z)
corresponding to the
eigenvalues
0\leqslant t_{1}^{2}\leqslant\dots\leqslant t_{j}^{2}\leqslant\kappa^{2}
. Let
\psi_{j}^{\pm}
and
I_{\pm,1},I_{\pm,2}

be as in Proposition
9.6
, let
N>0
be sufficiently large (depending only on
the
\Omega
and the constants in Assumption
9.1
), and put

\widetilde{e}_{j}:=\frac{\Pi_{t_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}}\psi_{j}^{\,\mathrm{sign}(d)}}{\|\Pi_{t_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}}\psi_{j}^{\,\mathrm{sign}(d)}\|},\quad j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1},

and

\widetilde{e}_{j}:=\frac{\Pi_{t_{|d|}}\psi_{j}^{\,\mathrm{sign}(d)}}{\|\Pi_{t_{|d|}}\psi_{j}^{\,\mathrm{sign}(d)}\|},\quad j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}.

Then, uniformly in
z\in\Omega_{N}
,

\|\widetilde{e}_{j}-\psi_{j}^{\,\mathrm{sign}(d)}\|=O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}}{\log N}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}},&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1},\\
N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N,&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2},\end{array}\right.

and

(10.16)

\langle\widetilde{e}_{i}|\widetilde{e}_{j}\rangle=\delta_{i,j}+\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}}{\log N}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}},&\text{ when }i,j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1},\\
O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}},&\text{ when }i,j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2},\\
O(\log N)N^{-C_{\gamma}}+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}),&\text{else}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

To ease the notation we drop the
\pm
superscripts and let
\psi_{j}
be either
\psi_{j}^{+}
or
\psi_{j}^{-}

depending on
\mathrm{sign}(d)
. Furthermore, we write
B_{z}:=(P_{N}-z)^{*}(P_{N}-z)
and we let
N\gg 1
.
In what follows all constants will be uniform in
N\gg 1
and
z\in\Omega_{N}
even when we do not
state this fact explicitly.

By (
9.27
), we have that

(10.17)

\langle B_{z}\psi_{j}|\psi_{j}\rangle=\|(P_{N}-z)\psi_{j}\|^{2}=O(1)\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{e}^{-2N\log C_{\beta}},&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}\\
\left(\frac{\log N}{N}N^{-C_{\gamma}}\right)^{2},&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}.\end{array}\right.

Let
\kappa=t_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}
for
j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}
,
and
\kappa=t_{|d|}
for
j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}
. Let

\Pi=\Pi_{\kappa}
and
\Pi^{\perp}=\mathbf{1}_{]\kappa^{2},\infty[}(B_{z})
, and notice
that both are selfadjoint projectors onto disjoint eigenspaces and that
\Pi+\Pi^{\perp}=1
.
By the spectral theorem and the fact that
B_{z}\geqslant 0
,

(10.18)

\langle B_{z}\psi_{j}|\psi_{j}\rangle=\langle B_{z}\Pi\psi_{j}|\Pi\psi_{j}\rangle+\langle B_{z}\Pi^{\perp}\psi_{j}|\Pi^{\perp}\psi_{j}\rangle\geqslant\|\Pi^{\perp}\psi_{j}\|^{2}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}t_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}+1}^{2},&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1},\\
t_{|d|+1}^{2},&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}.\end{array}\right.

Combining (
10.17
), (
10.18
) and (
10.2
), (
10.4
) we get that

\|\Pi^{\perp}\psi_{j}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{p1}}}{{\leqslant}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}O(1)\left(\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}}t_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}+1}^{-1}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sgv2a}}}{{\leqslant}}O(1)\frac{N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}}{\log N}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}},&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1},\\
O(1)\left(t_{|d|+1}^{-1}\frac{\log N}{N}N^{-C_{\gamma}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sgv2}}}{{\leqslant}}O(1)N^{-C_{\gamma}},&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}.\end{array}\right.

Hence,
by (
9.26
),

|1-\|\Pi\psi_{j}\||\leqslant\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\|\psi_{j}-\Pi\psi_{j}\|=\|\Pi^{\perp}\psi_{j}\|=O(1)\frac{N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}}{\log N}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}},&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}\\
\|\psi_{j}-\Pi\psi_{j}\|+O(1)N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N=O(1)N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N,&j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}\end{array}\right.=:\varepsilon_{j}.

Thus, for
N>0
sufficiently large, so that
\varepsilon_{j}\ll 1
,

\|\widetilde{e}_{j}-\psi_{j}\|=\left\|\frac{\Pi\psi_{j}}{1+O(\varepsilon_{j})}-\psi_{j}\right\|\leqslant O(\varepsilon_{j}),

which, together with (
9.26
) and

\langle\widetilde{e}_{i}|\widetilde{e}_{j}\rangle=\langle\psi_{i}|\psi_{j}\rangle+\langle\widetilde{e}_{i}-\psi_{i}|\psi_{j}\rangle+\langle\psi_{i}|\widetilde{e}_{j}-\psi_{j}\rangle+\langle\widetilde{e}_{i}-\psi_{i}|\widetilde{e}_{j}-\psi_{j}\rangle,

lets us conclude the proofs of the proposition.
∎

Notice that (
10.16
) implies by a similar argument as before (
10.6
),
that for
N>0
large enough, the vectors
\widetilde{e}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{e}_{|d|}
are
linearly independent.
Hence, we conclude the following.

Corollary 10.4
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
10.3
, we have that
the
\widetilde{e}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{e}_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}

span the range
\mathcal{R}(\Pi_{t_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}})
and that

\widetilde{e}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{e}_{|d|}
span
\mathcal{R}(\Pi_{t_{|d|}})
.

Remark 10.5
.

By Remark
9.5
, (
9.29
), and Proposition
10.3
it follows that for the Jordan block with
z
inside the unit disc such that (
9.5
) holds we have that

\left\|\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{1}^{+}\|}-e_{1}\right\|=O(N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N).

In what follows we suppose that
N>0
is sufficiently large, so that the conclusion of
Proposition
10.3
holds. Furthermore, all error terms will be uniform in

z\in\Omega_{N}
even if we don’t state this explicitly.

In view of Proposition
10.3
and Corollary
10.4
, we know
that there exist

a_{1},\dots,a_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}\in\ell^{2}(I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1})

and
b_{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}+1},\dots,b_{|d|}\in\ell^{2}([|d|])
such that

e_{j}=\sum_{\nu\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}}a_{j}(\nu)\widetilde{e}_{\nu},\penalty\ \penalty\ j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1},\quad\text{and}\quad e_{j}=\sum_{1}^{|d|}b_{j}(\nu)\widetilde{e}_{\nu},\penalty\ \penalty\ j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}.

Let
n,m\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}
.
Using (
10.16
) and the fact that the
e_{j}
are orthonormal, we get that

\begin{split}\delta_{n,m}&=\langle e_{n}|e_{m}\rangle=\sum_{\nu,\mu}a_{n}(\nu)\overline{a}_{m}(\mu)\langle\widetilde{e}_{\nu}|\widetilde{e}_{\mu}\rangle=\langle a_{n}|a_{m}\rangle+O\!\left(\|a\|^{2}N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}}/\log N\right),\end{split}

where the constant in the error estimate is independent of
a
. We deduce that

\|a_{n}\|=1+O(N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}\mathrm{e}^{-N/C}/\log N)
, and

(10.19)

\langle a_{n}|a_{m}\rangle=\delta_{n,m}+O\left(N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}}/\log N\right).

Similarly, we deduce from (
10.16
) that for
n,m\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}

(10.20)

\langle b_{n}|b_{m}\rangle=\delta_{n,m}+O(N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N)+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}),

where the constants in the error estimate are independent of
b
. Decompose

b_{j}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\widetilde{b}_{j}\oplus\widehat{b}_{j}\in\ell^{2}(I_{+,1})\oplus\ell^{2}(I_{+,2}),&\mathrm{sign}(d)>0,\\
\widehat{b}_{j}\oplus\widetilde{b}_{j}\in\ell^{2}(I_{-,2})\oplus\ell^{2}(I_{-,1}),&\mathrm{sign}(d)<0.\end{array}\right.

Exploiting the orthogonality of the
e_{j}
and using (
10.19
), (
10.20
), we deduce
similarly as above that for
n\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}
and
m\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}
,

(10.21)

\langle a_{n}|\widetilde{b}_{m}\rangle=O(N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N)+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}).

By a similar argument as before (
10.6
), we deduce from (
10.19
) that for

N>0
sufficiently large (depending only on the
\Omega
and the constants in Assumption

9.1
), the
a_{j}
are linearly independent and span
\ell^{2}(I_{\pm,1})
.
Thus, for each
\widetilde{b}_{j}
there exists a
d_{j}\in\mathds{C}^{|d|-m_{\mathrm{sign}(d)}^{0}}

such that
\widetilde{b}_{j}=\sum_{\nu}d_{j}(\nu)a_{\nu}
. Hence, by (
10.21
), (
10.19
),

(10.22)

\|\widetilde{b}_{m}\|^{2}=\sum_{\nu,\mu}d_{m}(\nu)\overline{d_{m}(\mu)}\langle a_{\nu}|a_{\mu}\rangle=\|d_{m}\|^{2}\left(1+O\!\left(N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}}/\log N\right)\right).

Combining this with (
10.21
), (
10.22
), (
10.19
), we deduce that

\|d_{k}\|=O(N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N)+O((\log N/N)^{1/2})+O\!\left(N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}}/\log N\right),

where the error terms are independent of
a
,
b
, and
d
. Notice that the error term
which is exponentially small in
N
may be absorbed into the other two terms. Hence,

\|\widetilde{b}_{m}\|=O(N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N)+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}).

Thus, for
j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}
,

e_{j}=\sum_{\nu\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}}\widehat{b}_{j}(\nu)\psi_{\nu}^{\,\mathrm{sign}(d)}+O(N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N)+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}).

In view of (
9.29
), (
9.51
) and Remarks
9.8
and
9.7
, we
may replace
\psi_{\nu}^{\,\mathrm{sign}(d)}
in the above expression by

{\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{\mathrm{sign}(d)}}/{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{\mathrm{sign}(d)}\|}
,
see also Proposition
9.3
.

Summing up what we have proven so far, we obtain in view of Propositions
10.3
and
9.6
the following. Recall
that for
z\in\Omega_{N}
,

e_{1},\dots,e_{N}
are an orthonormal set of eigenvectors of
(P_{N}-z)^{*}(P_{N}-z)

corresponding to the eigenvalues
0\leqslant t_{1}^{2}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant t_{N}^{2}
.

Proposition 10.6
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
10.3
,
we have that for
j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}
,

e_{j}=\sum_{\nu\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}}a_{j}(\nu)\psi_{\nu}^{\,\mathrm{sign}(d)}+O(1)N^{(1+C_{\alpha})}\mathrm{e}^{-N\log C_{\beta}}/\log N,

where
a_{j}\in\ell^{2}(I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1})

satisfy (
10.19
), uniformly in
z\in\Omega_{N}
.
Further, for
j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}
,

(10.23)

e_{j}=\sum_{\nu\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}}b_{j}(\nu)\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{\mathrm{sign}(d)}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{\mathrm{sign}(d)}\|}+O(N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N)+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}).

where
b_{j}\in\ell^{2}(I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2})

satisfy

(10.24)

\langle b_{n}|b_{m}\rangle=\delta_{n,m}+O(\log NN^{-C_{\gamma}})+O((\log N/N)^{1/2}),

uniformly in
z\in\Omega_{N}
.

Remark 10.7
.

We recall, see points (i), (ii) of Lemma
6.16
and
(
5.85
), that for
\nu\neq\nu^{\prime}\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}

we have that
|\zeta^{\mathrm{sign}(d)}_{\nu}\cdot\overline{\zeta^{\mathrm{sign}(d)}_{\nu^{\prime}}}-1|
is bounded
away from
0
by a constant
c>0
independent of
N
, while
for
\nu=\nu^{\prime}\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}
it is of order
\log N/N

by (
9.5
)-(
9.7
).

Next, we turn to proving localization estimates of the singular vectors.

Theorem 10.8
.

Consider the setup and notation as in Proposition
10.1
.
Then, there exist constants
0<C,C^{\prime}<\infty

such that for
N>0
sufficiently large (depending only on
\Omega
and the constants in
Assumption
9.1
) we have that for any
z\in\Omega_{N}
, for any discrete interval

I=[\ell_{0},\ell_{1}]\subset[0,N-1]
, with
|I|\geqslant 2(1+m_{+}^{0})
, and for
j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),2}

(10.25)

\|e_{j}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}\leqslant C\min\left\{|I|\frac{\log N}{N},\frac{1}{C^{\prime}}\right\}\mathrm{e}^{-2C_{\gamma}\mathfrak{d}\frac{\log N}{N}}+CN^{-2C_{\gamma}}(\log N)^{2}+C\frac{\log N}{N},

and

(10.26)

\|e_{j}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}\geqslant\frac{1}{C}\min\left\{|I|\frac{\log N}{N},\frac{1}{C^{\prime}}\right\}\mathrm{e}^{-2C_{\alpha}\mathfrak{d}\frac{\log N}{N}}-CN^{-2C_{\gamma}}(\log N)^{2}-C\frac{\log N}{N},

and for
j\in I_{\mathrm{sign}(d),1}
,

(10.27)

\|e_{j}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}\leqslant C\mathrm{e}^{-2\mathfrak{d}\log C_{\beta}}+CN^{2(1+C_{\alpha})}\mathrm{e}^{-2N\log C_{\beta}}(\log N)^{-2}.

Here,

\mathfrak{d}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\ell_{0}&\text{when }d>0,\\
N-\ell_{1}&\text{when }d<0.\end{array}\right.

We remark that we do not use (
10.26
) in the rest of the paper
(instead, we use (
10.23
)). Its inclusion here is to contrast with
(
10.25
).

Proof.

We shall only consider the case when the winding number
d>0
, since the case when
d<0
is
similar. Throughout we will assume that
N>0
is sufficiently large (depending only on
\Omega
and
the constants in Assumption
9.1
) so that the conclusions of Propositions
10.6
,

10.3
,
9.6
, and
9.3
hold, and we have that all error terms are
uniform in
z\in\Omega_{N}
without us mentioning this explicitly.

1. We begin by considering the case where
d-m_{+}^{0}>0
and
j\in I_{+,1}
.
By Propositions
10.6
and
9.6
, we find that

(10.28)

\|e_{j}\|_{\ell^{2}(I)}^{2}\leqslant 2\left\|\sum_{\nu\in I_{+,1}}a_{j}(\nu)\psi_{\nu}^{+}\right\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}+O(1)\frac{N^{2(1+C_{\alpha})}}{(\log N)^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-2N\log C_{\beta}}.

Recall from Proposition
9.3
that
\psi_{\nu}^{+}
is given by the
\nu
-th column of

\mathfrak{Z}^{+}A^{+}_{1}G_{+,1}^{-1/2}
. Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we get that

(10.29)

\begin{split}\left\|\sum_{\nu\in I_{+,1}}a_{j}(\nu)\psi_{\nu}\right\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}&=\sum_{\mu\in I}\left|\sum_{\nu\in I_{+,1}}\sum_{\eta=1}^{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}\mathfrak{Z}^{+}_{\mu,\eta}(A^{+}_{1}G_{+,1}^{-1/2})_{\eta,\nu}a_{j}(\nu)\right|^{2}\\
&=\sum_{\mu\in I}\left|\sum_{\eta=1}^{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}\mathfrak{Z}^{\pm}_{\mu,\eta}(A^{+}_{1}G_{+,1}^{-1/2}a_{j})(\eta)\right|^{2}\leqslant\|A^{+}_{1}G_{+,1}^{-1/2}a_{j}\|^{2}\sum_{\eta=1}^{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\eta}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}.\end{split}

where
\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\eta}
is defined as in Proposition
9.3
. For
\eta=1,\dots,m_{+}
, we have
that

(10.30)

\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\eta}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}=\sum_{\nu=\ell_{0}}^{\ell_{1}}|\zeta^{+}_{\eta}|^{2\nu}=|\zeta_{\eta}^{+}|^{2\ell_{0}}\frac{1-|\zeta_{\eta}^{+}|^{2|I|}}{1-|\zeta_{\eta}^{+}|^{2}}.

By (
9.6
) we have that
|\zeta_{\eta}^{+}|\leqslant 1/C_{\beta}
for
\eta=1,\dots,{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}
. Thus,

(10.31)

|\zeta_{\eta}^{+}|^{2\ell_{0}}\leqslant\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\eta}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}\leqslant\frac{C_{\beta}^{2}}{C_{\beta}^{2}-1}|\zeta_{\eta}^{+}|^{2\ell_{0}}.

Combining (
9.45
), (
10.19
), with (
10.28
) and (
10.29
), we conclude
(
10.27
).

2. Next, we let
j\in I_{+,2}
, so we may assume that
m_{+}^{0}>0
.
By Propositions
10.6
and
9.6
, we find that

(10.32)

\|e_{j}\|_{\ell^{2}(I)}^{2}\leqslant 2\left\|\sum_{\nu\in I_{+,2}}b_{j}(\nu)\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\|}\right\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}+\varepsilon_{N},

where, to ease the notation, we put

\varepsilon_{N}=O((\log N)^{2})N^{-2C_{\gamma}}+O(\log N/N)
. For the sake
of the presentation we will keep this notation for the error term and we allow for the
constant in the estimate to change while remaining uniform in
z\in\Omega_{N}
.

From Proposition
10.6
, we see that the coefficients
b_{j}
are uniformly
bounded in
\ell^{2}
by
O(1)
. Thus by a similar computation as in (
10.29
), (
10.31
),
we get together with (
9.50
), (
9.59
) that

(10.33)

\|e_{j}\|_{\ell^{2}(I)}^{2}\leqslant O(1)\sum_{\nu\in I_{+,2}}\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|^{2}}+\varepsilon_{N}.

Recall (
9.6
) and (
9.5
).
Let
\nu\in I_{+,2}
, and suppose first that
|I|\ll N/\log N
, we find by Taylor expansion that

(10.34)

2|I|C_{\alpha}\frac{\log N}{N}+O\!\left(|I|\frac{\log N}{N}\right)^{2}\leqslant 1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2|I|}\leqslant 2|I|C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N}{N}+O\!\left(|I|\frac{\log N}{N}\right)^{2}.

Thus,

(10.35)

\frac{C_{\alpha}}{C_{\gamma}}|I|\left(1+O\!\left(|I|\frac{\log N}{N}\right)\right)\leqslant\frac{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2|I|}}{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2}}\leqslant\frac{C_{\gamma}}{C_{\alpha}}|I|\left(1+O\!\left(|I|\frac{\log N}{N}\right)\right).

Plugging the estimates (
10.34
), (
10.35
) into (
10.30
), and using
(
9.47
) as well, we get that there exists a constant
C>0
such that, for
N>0
large enough,

(10.36)

\frac{1}{C}|I|\frac{\log N}{N}\mathrm{e}^{-2C_{\alpha}\ell_{0}(1+O(\frac{\log N}{N}))\frac{\log N}{N}}\leqslant\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|^{2}}\asymp|I|\frac{\log N}{N}|\zeta_{\nu}|^{2\ell_{0}}\leqslant C|I|\frac{\log N}{N}\mathrm{e}^{-2C_{\gamma}\ell_{0}\frac{\log N}{N}}.

When
N/(C\log N)\leqslant|I|\leqslant N
for some
C\gg 1
, then we see by Taylor expansion that

\frac{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2|I|}}{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2}}\asymp\frac{N}{\log N}.

Hence, by (
9.47
) and (
10.30
), we get that there exists a constant

C>0
such that, for
N>0
large enough,

(10.37)

\frac{1}{C}\mathrm{e}^{-2C_{\alpha}\ell_{0}(1+O(\frac{\log N}{N}))\frac{\log N}{N}}\leqslant\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|^{2}}\asymp|\zeta_{\nu}|^{2\ell_{0}}\leqslant C\mathrm{e}^{-2C_{\gamma}\ell_{0}\frac{\log N}{N}}.

By (
10.33
), (
10.36
) and (
10.37
) we conclude (
10.25
).

3. Next we prove the lower bound (
10.26
), so we work still with

j\in I_{+,2}
and
m_{+}^{0}>0
. Similar to (
10.32
), we find by
Propositions
10.6
and
9.6
that

\|e_{j}\|_{\ell^{2}(I)}^{2}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sum_{\nu\in I_{+,2}}b_{j}(\nu)\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\|}\right\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}-\varepsilon_{N}.

Let

V=\begin{pmatrix}1&\dots&1\\
\zeta_{m_{+}-m^{0}_{+}+1}^{+}&\dots&\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
(\zeta_{m_{+}-m^{0}_{+}+1}^{+})^{m_{+}-m^{0}_{+}-1}&\dots&(\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+})^{m_{+}-m^{0}_{+}-1},\end{pmatrix}

\Lambda=\mathrm{diag}(\zeta_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}+1}^{+},\dots,\zeta_{m_{+}}^{+})
and let

\mathfrak{L}_{+}
be as in Proposition
9.3
. By a similar argument as for
(
9.40
), we see that
s_{m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}}(V)\geqslant 1/C
for some
C>0
, depending
only on
\Omega
. Let
|I|\geqslant 2(1+m_{+}^{0})
. Then,

(10.38)

\begin{split}\left\|\sum_{\nu\in I_{+,2}}b_{j}(\nu)\frac{\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|}\right\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}(I)}&\geqslant\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor(|I|-1)/m_{+}^{0}\rfloor-1}\|V\Lambda^{\ell_{0}+km_{+}^{0}}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}b\|^{2}\geqslant\frac{1}{C^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor(|I|-1)/m_{+}^{0}\rfloor-1}\|\Lambda^{\ell_{0}+km_{+}^{0}}\mathfrak{L}_{+}^{-1}b\|^{2}\\
&\geqslant\frac{1}{C^{2}}\sum_{\nu\in I_{+,2}}|b_{j}(\nu)|^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor(|I|-1)/m_{+}^{0}\rfloor-1}\frac{|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(\ell_{0}+km_{+}^{0})}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|^{2}}\end{split}

Let
\nu\in I_{+,2}
. A direct computation yields that

(10.39)

\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor(|I|-1)/m_{+}^{0}\rfloor-1}|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(\ell_{0}+km_{+}^{0})}=|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2\ell_{0}}\frac{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2m_{+}^{0}(\lfloor(|I|-1)/m_{+}^{0}\rfloor-1)}}{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2m_{+}^{0}}}\geqslant|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2\ell_{0}}\frac{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(|I|-1-m_{+}^{0})}}{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2m_{+}^{0}}}

Recall (
9.5
) and (
9.6
).
Suppose first that
2(1+m_{+}^{0})\leqslant|I|\ll N/\log N
, then
|I|/2\leqslant|I|-1-m_{+}^{0}\leqslant|I|
, and
by Taylor expansion we find that

1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(|I|-1-m_{+}^{0})}\geqslant|I|C_{\alpha}\frac{\log N}{N}+O\!\left(|I|\frac{\log N}{N}\right)^{2}.

and similarly

1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2m_{+}^{0}}\leqslant 2m_{+}^{0}C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N}{N}+O\!\left(\frac{\log N}{N}\right)^{2}.

Thus,

(10.40)

\frac{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(|I|-1-m_{+}^{0})}}{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2m_{+}^{0}}}\geqslant\frac{C_{\alpha}}{2m_{+}^{0}C_{\gamma}}|I|\left(1+O\!\left(|I|\frac{\log N}{N}\right)\right).

Combining (
10.40
), (
10.39
) and (
10.38
) with (
9.47
),
yields that there exists a constant
C>0
such that for
N>0
large enough and all

z\in\Omega_{N}
,

(10.41)

\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor(|I|-1)/m_{+}^{0}\rfloor-1}\frac{|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(\ell_{0}+km_{+}^{0})}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|^{2}}\geqslant\frac{1}{C}|I|\frac{\log N}{N}|\zeta_{\nu}|^{2\ell_{0}}\geqslant\frac{1}{C}|I|\frac{\log N}{N}\mathrm{e}^{-2C_{\alpha}\ell_{0}(1+O(\frac{\log N}{N}))\frac{\log N}{N}}.

When
N/(C\log N)\leqslant|I|\leqslant N
for some
C\gg 1
, then we see by Taylor expansion that

\frac{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(|I|-1-m_{+}^{0})}}{1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2m_{+}^{0}}}\asymp\frac{1}{C}\frac{N}{\log N}.

We then deduce similarly to (
10.41
) that there exists a constant
C>0
such
that for
N>0
large enough and all
z\in\Omega_{N}
,

(10.42)

\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor(|I|-1)/m_{+}^{0}\rfloor-1}\frac{|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(\ell_{0}+jm_{+}^{0})}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{\nu}\|^{2}}\geqslant\frac{1}{C}\mathrm{e}^{-2C_{\alpha}\ell_{0}(1+O(\frac{\log N}{N}))\frac{\log N}{N}}.

From (
10.24
) we know that for
N>0
sufficiently large
\|b_{j}\|\geqslant 1/2
. Hence,
(
10.41
), (
10.42
) together with (
10.38
) imply (
10.26
), which concludes
the proof of the theorem.
∎

10.3.
Large singular values

The next result gives estimates on the Hilbert-Schmidt and trace norm of
E

from (
2.9
).

Proposition 10.9
.

Let
z_{0}\in p(S^{1})
be as in (
9.1
) and let

\Omega\Subset\mathds{C}
be a sufficiently small open relatively
compact convex neighbourhood of
z_{0}
satisfying (
9.2
).
Let
N>1
be sufficiently large, and let

\Omega_{N}\Subset\Omega\backslash p(S^{1})

satisfy (
9.5
). Let
d
be as in (
9.4
)
and let
t_{j}
be as in Proposition
10.1
,
then, uniformly in
N
and
z\in\Omega_{N}
,

(10.43)

\sum_{j=|d|+1}^{N}t_{j}^{-2}=O\!\left(\frac{N^{2}}{\log N}\right),

and

(10.44)

\sum_{j=|d|+1}^{N}t_{j}^{-1}=O(N{\log N}).

Proof.

1. Let
z_{0}
and
\Omega
as in the assumptions of the proposition and let
z\in\Omega_{N}
.
Let
\widetilde{N}=N_{+}\lor 0+N_{-}\lor 0
and notice that
\widetilde{N}\geqslant|d|
,
see Lemma
4.1
and (
4.34
).
By (
4.20
), (
10.15
) and (
1.13
), we see that

(10.45)

t_{j}(P_{N}-z)\geqslant t_{j-\widetilde{N}}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z),\quad j=1+\widetilde{N},\dots,N.

From (
4.20
) we deduce that the set of eigenvalues of
(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z)^{*}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z)

is given by

(10.46)

\{t_{j}^{2}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z);j=1,\dots,N\}=\{|p(\widehat{\zeta})-z|^{2};\widehat{\zeta}\in S_{N}\},

where
S_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{\mathrm{e}^{2\pi ij/N};j=0,\dots,N-1\}
.
Note that
t_{1}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z)\geqslant\mathrm{dist\,}(z,p(S^{1}))>0
,
since
z\in\Omega_{N}
. Hence, combining (
10.45
) and (
10.2
),
we get for
\nu=1,2
,

(10.47)

\sum_{j=|d|+1}^{N}\frac{1}{t_{j}^{\nu}(P_{N}-z)}\leqslant\sum_{j=1+\widetilde{N}}^{N}\frac{1}{t_{j}^{\nu}(P_{N}-z)}+\widetilde{N}\left(\frac{CN}{\log N}\right)^{\nu}\leqslant\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{t_{j}^{\nu}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z)}+\widetilde{N}\left(\frac{CN}{\log N}\right)^{\nu}.

It remains to deal with the traces
\mathrm{tr\,}((P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z)^{*}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z))^{-\nu/2}
for
\nu=1,2
.

2. We know by (
4.16
) that there
are finitely many, say
N_{0}<+\infty
, points in
p^{-1}(z_{0})
. We will enumerate them
in the following way: for
j=1,\dots,N_{0}^{\prime}
, with
N_{0}^{\prime}\geqslant 1
, let
\zeta_{j}\in p^{-1}(z_{0})

be so that
\zeta_{j}\in S^{1}
, and for
j=N_{0}^{\prime}+1,\dots,N_{0}
, let
\zeta_{j}\in p^{-1}(z_{0})

be so that
\zeta_{j}\notin S^{1}
. If
N_{0}^{\prime}=N_{0}
then the latter set of points is empty.

By (
9.2
) and the Implicit function theorem, it follows that, after potentially
shrinking
\Omega
(while keeping it convex), there exist complex open neighbourhoods

Z_{j}
of
\zeta_{j}
, such that
p:Z_{j}\to\Omega
is a diffeomorphism and such that

Z_{j}\cap S^{1}=\emptyset
for
j=N_{0}^{\prime}+1,\dots,N_{0}
. Furthermore, after potentially further
shrinking
\Omega
and the sets
Z_{j}
, we can arrange so that

p(S^{1}\cap Z_{j})=\Omega\cap p(S^{1})=\Gamma
for all
j=1,\dots,N_{0}^{\prime}
. Let

\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega

be a smaller open convex neighbourhood of
z_{0}
such that
\overline{\Omega^{\prime}}\subset\Omega
,
and put
Z_{j}^{\prime}:=(p\!\upharpoonright_{Z_{j}\to\Omega})^{-1}(\Omega^{\prime})\subset Z_{j}
. A convenient
choice is

\Omega^{\prime}=\{z\in\mathds{C};\mathrm{dist\,}(z,\Gamma^{\prime})\leqslant 1/C_{\Omega^{\prime}},\pi(z)\in\Gamma^{\prime}\},

where
0<C_{\Omega^{\prime}}<\infty
is some sufficiently large constant,

\Gamma^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
is a connected non-empty subsegment, containing
z_{0}
,
so that the endpoints of the segments
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
are separated by some
fixed positive distance, i.e. for some large constant
C^{\prime}>0
we have that

\mathrm{dist\,}(\partial\Gamma,\partial\Gamma^{\prime})\geqslant 1/C^{\prime}
. Furthermore,
\pi(z)
denotes the point
in
p(S^{1})
with
|\Pi(z)-z|=\mathrm{dist\,}(z,p(S^{1}))
. Notice that this point is unique if

C_{\Omega^{\prime}}
is large enough.

We note that
there exists a constant
C>0

(depending only on
\Omega
and
\Omega^{\prime}
)
such that for all
z\in\Omega^{\prime}
and all
\zeta\in S^{1}\backslash\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{0}^{\prime}}(Z_{j}\cap S^{1})

(10.48)

|p(\zeta)-z|\geqslant\mathrm{dist\,}(\partial\Omega,\partial\Omega^{\prime})\geqslant 1/C.

Furthermore, observe that since two consecutive points in
S_{N}
differ by an angle of
2\pi/N
, we have that

(10.49)

\#\{S_{N}\cap Z_{j}\}=N\int_{(p\upharpoonright_{Z_{j}\to\Omega})^{-1}(\Omega)}L_{S^{1}}(d\theta)+O(1),\quad j=1,\dots,N_{0}^{\prime},

where
L_{S^{1}}
denotes the normalized Lebesgue measure on
S^{1}
.

Let
j\in\{1,\dots,N_{0}^{\prime}\}
,
z\in\Omega_{N}^{\prime}:=\Omega_{N}\cap\Omega^{\prime}
,
and
\zeta_{0}^{j}\in Z_{j}^{\prime}
be the unique point
in
Z_{j}^{\prime}
such that
p(\zeta_{0}^{j})=\Pi(z)
. For
N>1
sufficiently large
(depending only on
\Omega
and
\Omega^{\prime}
) there exists a point
\widehat{\zeta}^{j}_{*}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N}
which is
the closest point in
Z_{j}\cap S_{N}
to
\zeta_{0}^{j}
. There may be two such points, in which case we
pick one of them. Then,

(10.50)

|\zeta_{0}^{j}-\widehat{\zeta}_{*}^{j}|\leqslant|1-\mathrm{e}^{2\pi i/N}|\leqslant O(N^{-1}).

By Taylor expansion around
\zeta^{j}_{0}
, we find that

|p(\widehat{\zeta}^{j}_{*})-z|\leqslant|p(\zeta^{j}_{0})-z|+O(N^{-1}),

where the constant is independent of
z
. In view of (
10.46
), we have that

t_{1}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z)=\min\limits_{\widehat{\zeta}\in S_{N}}|p(\widehat{\zeta})-z|.

Setting
\widetilde{t}:=|p(\zeta^{j}_{0})-z|
, as
p(\zeta_{0}^{j})=\Pi(z)
and hence
|p(\zeta_{0}^{j})-z|={\rm dist}(z,p(S^{1}))
, we see that

(10.51)

\widetilde{t}\leqslant t_{1}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z)\leqslant\min\limits_{j=1,\dots,N_{0}^{\prime}}|p(\widehat{\zeta}^{j}_{*})-z|\leqslant\max\limits_{j=1,\dots,N_{0}^{\prime}}|p(\widehat{\zeta}^{j}_{*})-z|\leqslant\widetilde{t}+O(N^{-1}),

where the constant is independent of
z
. By Taylor expansion and (
9.2
) we get that
for
\widehat{\zeta}^{j}\in Z_{j}

(10.52)

\begin{split}|p(\widehat{\zeta}^{j})-z|&=|p(\zeta^{j}_{0})+(\partial_{\zeta}p)(\zeta^{j}_{0})(\widehat{\zeta}^{j}-\zeta^{j}_{0})+O(|\widehat{\zeta}^{j}-\zeta^{j}_{0}|^{2})-z|\\
&\geqslant\frac{1}{C}|\widehat{\zeta}^{j}-\zeta^{j}_{0}|(1-C_{1}|\widehat{\zeta}^{j}-\zeta^{j}_{0}|)-\widetilde{t}.\end{split}

Here the constant
0<C<\infty
only depends on
\Omega
, see (
9.2
), and the constant
0<C_{1}<\infty

depends only on
C
and the second derivative of
p
in the
Z_{j}
’s, which is uniformly bounded.
Suppose that

(10.53)

|\widehat{\zeta}^{j}-\zeta^{j}_{0}|\leqslant\frac{1}{2C_{1}},\quad|\widehat{\zeta}^{j}-\zeta^{j}_{0}|\geqslant 8C\widetilde{t},

where
C,C_{1}
are as in (
10.52
). Hence, we find by (
10.52
), (
10.53
) that

(10.54)

|p(\widehat{\zeta}^{j})-z|\geqslant\frac{1}{4C}|\widehat{\zeta}^{j}-\zeta^{j}_{0}|.

3. We turn to proving the two estimates claimed in the proposition. We let
\Omega
and
\Omega^{\prime}

be as in Step 2. Recall (
10.51
) and note that if
\widetilde{t}\geqslant 1/C
for some arbitrarily large
but fixed constant
C
, then

\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{t_{j}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z)},\penalty\ \penalty\ \sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{t_{j}^{2}(P_{\mathds{Z}/N\mathds{Z}}-z)}=O(N),

and we conclude (
10.43
) and (
10.44
) in view of (
10.47
).

Continuing, we may assume from now on that
\widetilde{t}\leqslant 1/C_{0}
, for some arbitrarily large but
fixed constant
C_{0}
. Since the roots of
p(\zeta)-z
depend smoothly on
z\in\Omega
, see the
discussion after (
9.2
), it follows from (
9.5
) that there exists a constant
C>0
such that
for all
N>0
,

\mathrm{dist\,}(\Omega_{N},p(S^{1}))\geqslant\frac{1}{C}\frac{\log N}{N},

Hence, we may from now on assume that

(10.55)

\frac{1}{C}\frac{\log N}{N}\leqslant\widetilde{t}\leqslant\frac{1}{C_{0}}.

for some large
C>0
, independent of
N>0
and
z\in\Omega_{N}
. Let
\nu\in\{1,2\}
.
Then, in view of (
10.46
), (
10.48
) we get

(10.56)

\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{1}{t_{j}^{\nu}(P_{\mathds{Z}/n\mathds{Z}}-z)}=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{0}^{\prime}}\sum_{\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N}}\frac{1}{|p(\widehat{\zeta})-z|^{\nu}}+O(N),

where the constant in the error term depends only on
\Omega
and
\Omega^{\prime}
.
Using (
10.49
), we see that by potentially shrinking
\Omega
, and the sets
Z_{j}
and

\Omega^{\prime}
accordingly, we may suppose that the first estimate in (
10.53
) holds for all

\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N}
,
j=1,\dots,N_{0}^{\prime}
. Next, pick a
j\in\{1,\dots,N_{0}^{\prime}\}
, while keeping
in mind that
N_{0}^{\prime}<\infty
, and split the corresponding inner sum on the right hand side of (
10.56
) into
two parts: one where the second inequality in (
10.53
) holds and one where it does not, so

(10.57)

\sum_{\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N}}\frac{1}{|p(\widehat{\zeta})-z|^{\nu}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N},\\
|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta_{0}^{j}|\geqslant 8C\widetilde{t}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|p(\widehat{\zeta})-z|^{\nu}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N},\\
|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta_{0}^{j}|<8C\widetilde{t}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|p(\widehat{\zeta})-z|^{\nu}}.

We begin with treating the second sum on the right hand side. Since the points in
S_{N}
differ by an
angle of
2\pi/N
, we see that for
N>1

\#\{\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N};|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta_{0}^{j}|<8C\widetilde{t}\}=O(N\widetilde{t}\,),

where the constant depends only on the symbol
p
,
\Omega
and the constant
C
from (
10.52
).
Hence, by (
10.46
) and (
10.51
),

(10.58)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N},\\
|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta_{0}^{j}|<8C\widetilde{t}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|p(\widehat{\zeta})-z|^{\nu}}=O(N\widetilde{t}^{\,1-\nu}).

Next, we deal with the first sum on the right hand side of (
10.57
). Since for this term
(
10.53
) holds, we get by (
10.54
) that

(10.59)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N},\\
|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta_{0}^{j}|\geqslant 8C\widetilde{t}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|p(\widehat{\zeta})-z|^{\nu}}\leqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N},\\
|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta_{0}^{j}|\geqslant 8C\widetilde{t}\end{subarray}}\frac{(4C)^{\nu}}{|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta^{j}_{0}|^{\nu}}

By (
10.50
) and (
10.55
) we find that there exist constants

0<C_{2},C_{2}^{\prime}<\infty
such that
for
N>0
large enough (depending only on the constants in the
referenced inequalities)
we have that for all
\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N}

with
|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta_{0}^{j}|\geqslant 8C\widetilde{t}
and for all
z\in\Omega_{N}
, such that
(
10.55
) holds,

(10.60)

|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta^{j}_{0}|\geqslant\frac{1}{C_{2}}|\widehat{\zeta}-\widehat{\zeta}^{j}_{*}|\geqslant\frac{1}{C_{2}^{\prime}}\widetilde{t}.

By rotational invariance, we may assume that
{\widehat{\zeta}}^{j}_{*}=1
. Then, we have that

\widehat{\zeta}_{k}=\mathrm{e}^{2\pi ik/N}
,
k=1,\dots,M_{1}
are the points in
Z_{k}
to the left of
1
and

\widehat{\zeta}_{k}=\mathrm{e}^{2\pi ik/N}
,
k=-1,\dots,-M_{2}
are the points in
Z_{k}
to the right of
1
.
By (
10.49
) it is clear that after potentially further shrinking
\Omega
and taking
N>0
large enough,
we have that
M_{1},M_{2}\leqslant N/C_{3}
, for
C_{3}\gg 1
. Hence, we find that there exists a constant
0<C_{4}<\infty

such that

(10.61)

|\widehat{\zeta}_{k}-\zeta^{j}_{*}|=|\mathrm{e}^{\pm 2\pi ik/N}-1|\geqslant\frac{1}{C_{4}N}|k|,\quad-M_{2}\leqslant k\leqslant M_{1},\penalty\ k\neq 0.

On the other hand, we see by (
10.55
) that if
|k|\leqslant B^{-1}\log N
,
B\gg 1
, then

(10.62)

|\widehat{\zeta}_{k}-{\widehat{\zeta}}^{j}_{*}|=|\mathrm{e}^{\pm 2\pi ik/N}-1|\ll\widetilde{t},

for
N>1
. Combining (
10.61
), (
10.62
) with (
10.59
) and (
10.60
), we conclude that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widehat{\zeta}\in Z_{j}\cap S_{N},\\
|\widehat{\zeta}-\zeta_{0}^{j}|\geqslant 8C\widetilde{t}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|p(\widehat{\zeta})-z|^{\nu}}\leqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=-M_{2}\\
|k|\geqslant B^{-1}\log N\end{subarray}}^{M_{1}}\frac{(4CC_{2}C_{4}N)^{\nu}}{|k|^{\nu}}\leqslant\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}O(N\log N),&\nu=1\\
O(N^{2}(\log N)^{-1}),&\nu=2.\end{array}\right.

Together with (
10.47
), (
10.55
), (
10.56
),
(
10.57
) and (
10.58
),
we conclude (
10.43
) and (
10.44
).
∎

10.4.
Smallest singular value and Hilbert-Schmidt norms

In this short section we provide bounds on minimal singular
values and Hilbert-Schmidt norms of matrices appearing in the Grushin problem.
Recall the definition of
E(\cdot)
from (
2.9
).

Lemma 10.10
.

Consider the setup and notation as in Proposition
10.1
. Fix
M\in\mathds{N}
and
\gamma>1
. Assume that the entries of
Q
are i.i.d. with zero mean, unit variance, and finite fourth moment.
If
\gamma>3/2
then

\mathbf{P}\left(\inf_{z\in\Omega_{N}}s_{\min}(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))\leqslant 1/2\right)\leqslant N^{-(\gamma-3/2)/2},

for all large
N
. Otherwise, if
\gamma\in(1,3/2]
then given any
\alpha_{0}>0
there exists
\beta=\beta(\alpha_{0},\gamma)<\infty
, such that

\mathbf{P}\left(s_{\min}(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))\leqslant N^{-\beta}\right)\leqslant N^{-\alpha_{0}},

uniformly for all
z\in\Omega_{N}
and all large
N
.

Proof.

1. We first consider the easier case
\gamma>3/2
.
By Proposition
10.1
, applied with
M=|d|
, we have that
\|E(z)\|=O(N)
uniformly for
z\in\Omega_{N}
. As the entries of
Q
have zero mean and finite fourth moments,
applying
[
42
, Theorem 2]
and Markov’s inequality we obtain that

\mathbf{P}\left(N^{-\gamma}\sup_{z\in\Omega_{N}}\|QE(z)\|\geqslant 1/2\right)\leqslant\mathbf{P}\left(\|Q\|\geqslant cN^{\gamma-1}\right)\leqslant c^{-1}N^{-(\gamma-1)}\cdot\mathds{E}\|Q\|\leqslant N^{-(\gamma-3/2)/2},

for all large
N
, and some
c>0
. Thus, by the triangle inequality we derive that

\mathbf{P}\left(\inf_{z\in\Omega_{N}}s_{\min}(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))\leqslant 1/2\right)\leqslant\mathbf{P}\left(N^{-\gamma}\sup_{z\in\Omega_{N}}\|QE(z)\|\geqslant 1/2\right)\leqslant N^{-(\gamma-3/2)/2}.

This yields the result for
\gamma>3/2
.

2. We turn to the case of
\gamma\in(1,3/2]
. The proof uses
a change of basis, a lower bound on the smallest singular value of
matrices with i.i.d. entries shifted by a deterministic matrix, and an upper bound on the maximal singular value of a matrix with i.i.d. entries.

To carry out these steps we introduce additional
notation. Let
\mathsf{E}(z)
and
\mathsf{F}(z)
be the two
unitary matrices whose columns are
\{e_{1}(z),e_{2}(z),\ldots,e_{N}(z)\}
and
\{f_{1}(z),f_{2}(z),\ldots,f_{N}(z)\}
, respectively (Recall the definitions of
e_{i}
’s and
f_{j}
’s from Section
2.1
applied to
P=P_{N}
). Further define
\mathsf{E}_{1}(z)
and
\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)
to be the

N\times M
and
N\times(N-M)
matrices consisting of the first

M
columns and the remaining
(N-M)
columns of
\mathsf{E}(z)
, respectively. Similarly define
\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)
and
\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)
. Finally, let
\mathsf{T}(z)
be the diagonal matrices with entries
\{t_{M+1}(z)^{-1},t_{M+2}(z)^{-1},\ldots,t_{N}(z)^{-1}\}
, where
t_{j}(z)=t_{j}
,
j\in[N]
are as in Proposition
10.1
.

Equipped with this notation, upon recalling the definition of
E(z)
, we see that

I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}QE(z)=I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z)\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)^{*}.

Fix
x\in S^{N-1}
. As the columns of
\mathsf{F}(z)
form a basis of
\mathds{C}^{N}
,
there exist
c_{1}\in\mathds{C}^{M}
and
c_{2}\in\mathds{C}^{N-M}
such that

x=\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)c_{1}+\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)c_{2}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\|c_{1}\|^{2}+\|c_{2}\|^{2}=1.

Therefore, using the orthonormality property of the columns of
\mathsf{F}(z)
we derive that

(10.63)

\|(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))x\|^{2}=\|\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)c_{1}+\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)c_{2}+N^{-\gamma}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z)c_{2}\|^{2}\\
=\left\|\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)(c_{1}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z)c_{2})+\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)(I_{N-M}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z))c_{2}\right\|^{2}\\
=\left\|c_{1}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z)c_{2}\right\|^{2}+\left\|(I_{N-M}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z))c_{2}\right\|^{2}\\
=\left\|\begin{bmatrix}I_{M}&N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z)\\
0&I_{N-M}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z)\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}c_{1}\\
c_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\|^{2},

where in the second step we have used that
\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)^{*}+\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)^{*}=I_{N}
and in the third step we have again used the fact that the columns of
\mathsf{F}(z)=\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)&\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)\end{bmatrix}
form an orthonormal basis of
\mathds{C}^{N}
. So (
10.63
) shows that it is enough to find a lower bound on the smallest singular value of the
2\times 2
block matrix appearing in its
rhs
.

To this end, we note that any block matrix
A
of the form
A=\begin{bmatrix}I&A_{1}\\
0&A_{2}\end{bmatrix}
is invertible iff
A_{2}
is, and in that case

A^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}I&-A_{1}A_{2}^{-1}\\
0&A_{2}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}.

Hence, using the triangle inequality we obtain that

\|A^{-1}\|\leqslant 1+(\|A_{1}\|+1)\cdot\|A_{2}^{-1}\|.

Recalling the fact that
\|c_{1}\|^{2}+\|c_{2}\|^{2}=1
, using the above and (
10.63
) we therefore deduce that

(10.64)

\displaystyle(s_{\min}(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}QE(z)))^{-1}

\displaystyle=\|(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+(N^{-\gamma}\|\mathsf{A}_{1}(z)\|+1)\cdot\|(I_{N-M}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{A}_{2}(z))^{-1}\|,

where for brevity we write

\mathsf{A}_{1}(z):=\mathsf{F}_{1}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathsf{A}_{2}(z):=\mathsf{F}_{2}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}_{2}(z)\mathsf{T}(z).

Using
[
42
]
, Proposition
10.1
, and Markov’s inequality again we further have that

(10.65)

\mathbf{P}\left(N^{-\gamma}\|\mathsf{A}_{1}(z)\|+1\geqslant 2N^{2\alpha_{0}+1/2}\right)\leqslant t_{M+1}(z)^{-1}\cdot N^{-(\gamma+2\alpha_{0}+1/2)}\cdot\mathds{E}\|Q\|=O(N^{-2\alpha_{0}}),

where we have also used the facts that
t_{M+1}(z)^{-1}=O(N)
and
\gamma>1
. Hence, by (
10.64
), we observe that in order to find a lower bound on the smallest singular value of
I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}QE(z)
it remains to find the same for
I_{N-M}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{A}_{2}(z)
. Upon using Weyl’s inequality for singular values it is straightforward to notice that

(10.66)

s_{\min}(I_{N-M}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{A}_{2}(z))\geqslant s_{\min}(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{A}(z)),

where

\mathsf{A}(z):=\mathsf{F}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}(z)\widehat{\mathsf{T}}(z)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\widehat{\mathsf{T}}(z):=\begin{bmatrix}I_{M}&0\\
0&\mathsf{T}(z)\end{bmatrix}.

On the other hand

\displaystyle s_{\min}(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{A}(z))

\displaystyle\geqslant s_{\min}(\widehat{\mathsf{T}}(z))\cdot s_{\min}(\widehat{\mathsf{T}}(z)^{-1}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{F}(z)^{*}Q\mathsf{E}(z))

(10.67)

\displaystyle=t_{N}(z)^{-1}\cdot N^{-\gamma}\cdot s_{\min}(N^{\gamma}\mathsf{F}(z)\widehat{\mathsf{T}}(z)^{-1}\mathsf{E}(z)^{*}+Q)

Since
P_{N}
has a bounded norm and
\Omega_{N}
is a bounded set we have that
\|\widehat{\mathsf{T}}(z)^{-1}\|=t_{N}(z)=O(1)
. As
\mathsf{E}(z)
and
\mathsf{F}(z)
are unitary matrices, an application of
[
69
, Theorem 2.1]
together with (
10.67
) now yield that

(10.68)

\mathbf{P}\left(s_{\min}(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}\mathsf{A}(z))\leqslant N^{-\beta_{0}}\right)\leqslant N^{-2\alpha_{0}},

for all large
N
, where
\beta_{0}<\infty
is some constant depending only on
\alpha_{0}
and
\gamma
. Finally, using (
10.64
) and (
10.66
), the probability bounds (
10.65
) and (
10.68
), and a union bound we deduce that

\mathbf{P}\left(s_{\min}(I_{N}+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))\leqslant\frac{1}{3}N^{-(2\alpha_{0}+\beta_{0}+1/2)}\right)=O(N^{-2\alpha_{0}}),

for all large
N
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Next we derive bounds on the Hilbert-Schmidt norms of some matrices. This will allow us to control the error terms in the resolvent expansion of various terms appearing in the Grushin problem.

Proposition 10.11
.

Consider the setup and notation as in Proposition
10.1
. Additionally assume that
\Omega
is convex. Fix
\gamma>1
,
\kappa\in\mathds{N}
and
d\in[-\widetilde{m},\widetilde{m}]\cap\mathds{Z}\setminus\{0\}
. Let Assumption
1.1
holds. Then for any
\upalpha_{0}>0
, we have,
uniformly in
z\in\Omega_{N}
and all
N
large, that

(10.69)

\mathbf{P}\left(N^{-\gamma\kappa}\|(E(z)Q)^{\kappa}E_{+}(z)\|_{{\rm HS}}\geqslant N^{-\frac{(\gamma-1)\kappa}{2}}\right)\leqslant N^{-\upalpha_{0}},

(10.70)

\mathbf{P}\left(N^{-\gamma\kappa}\|(E(z)Q)^{\kappa}E(z)\|_{{\rm HS}}\geqslant N^{1-\frac{(\gamma-1)\kappa}{2}}\right)\leqslant N^{-\upalpha_{0}},

(10.71)

\mathbf{P}\left(N^{-\gamma\kappa}\|E_{-}(z)(QE(z))^{\kappa}\|_{{\rm HS}}\geqslant N^{-\frac{(\gamma-1)\kappa}{2}}\right)\leqslant N^{-\upalpha_{0}},

and

(10.72)

\mathbf{P}\left(N^{-\gamma(\kappa+1)}\|E_{-}(z)(QE(z))^{\kappa}QE_{+}(z)\|_{{\rm HS}}\geqslant N^{-\gamma-\frac{(\gamma-1)\kappa}{2}}\right)\leqslant N^{-\upalpha_{0}}.

To prove Proposition
10.11
we will rely on bounds on moments of Hilbert-Schmidt norm of matrices appearing in (
10.69
)-(
10.72
), as well as bounds on
\|E_{\pm}(\cdot)\|_{{\rm HS}}
and on the sum of the inverse of the non-zero singular values of
E(\cdot)
.

Proposition 10.12
.

Fix
\kappa\in\mathds{N}
. Let
\{\mathsf{E}^{(\ell)}\}_{\ell=1}^{\kappa}
and
\{\mathsf{F}^{(\ell)}\}_{\ell=1}^{\kappa}
be two collections of
N\times N
deterministic matrices, possibly with complex-valued entries, such that

(10.73)

\max\left\{\max_{\ell=1}^{\kappa}\|\mathsf{E}^{(\ell)}\|_{{\rm HS}},\max_{\ell=1}^{\kappa}\|\mathsf{F}^{(\ell)}\|_{{\rm HS}}\right\}\leqslant 1.

Define the product

(10.74)

\widehat{\mathsf{E}}(\kappa):=\mathsf{E}^{(1)}Q\mathsf{F}^{(1)}\cdot\mathsf{E}^{(2)}Q\mathsf{F}^{(2)}\cdots\mathsf{E}^{(\kappa)}Q\mathsf{F}^{(\kappa)}.

If the entries of
Q
satisfies Assumption
1.1
then for any
h_{0}\in\mathds{N}

\mathbf{E}\|\widehat{\mathsf{E}}(\kappa)\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2h_{0}}\leqslant C_{\ref {prop:mom-HS}}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{\kappa h_{0}},

where
C_{\ref {prop:mom-HS}}<\infty
is some constant depending only on
\kappa
and
h_{0}
.

The proof of Proposition
10.12
is straightforward. It relies on bounds on moments of quadratic forms of independent random variables, derived in
[
75
]
.

Proof of Proposition
10.12
.

We begin with an auxiliary computation. Let
A,B
be deterministic matrices. Then

W:=\|AQB\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2}=\sum_{i,j,k,k^{\prime},\ell,\ell^{\prime}}A_{i,k}\overline{A_{i,k^{\prime}}}Q_{k,\ell}\overline{Q_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}B_{\ell,j}\overline{B_{\ell^{\prime},j}}=\sum_{k,\ell,k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}Q_{kl}\overline{Q_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}C_{k,\ell,k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}},

where

C_{k,\ell,k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}:=\sum_{i,j}A_{ik}\overline{A_{ik^{\prime}}}B_{\ell,j}\overline{B_{\ell^{\prime},j}}.

This, in particular, shows that
W
is a quadratic form in the entries of
Q
. Hence,
using
[
75
, Theorem 2]
1
1

1

Whittle in
[
75
]
works with
real
linear and quadratic forms of
real
valued random variables. Upon splitting the sums into real and imaginary parts, analogous bounds can be derived in the complex case. Hence, without loss of generality we continue to use
[
75
]
for complex quadratic forms of complex valued random variables.
and Assumption
1.1
, for any
s\geqslant 2
, we find that

\mathbf{E}|W-\mathbf{E}W|^{s}\leqslant C(s)\left(\sum_{k,k^{\prime},\ell,\ell^{\prime}}|C_{k,\ell,k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}|^{2}\right)^{s/2}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{s}\leqslant C(s)\|A\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2s}\|B\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2s}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{s},

for some absolute constant
C(s)<\infty
, depending only on
s
, where in the last step we used that

|C_{k,\ell,k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}|\leqslant\|a_{k}\|\cdot\|a_{k^{\prime}}\|\cdot\|b_{\ell}\|\cdot\|b_{\ell^{\prime}}\|,

and
a_{k}
and
b_{k}
are the
k
-th column and row of
A
and
B
, respectively.

On the other hand we note that

|\mathbf{E}W|\leqslant\mathfrak{C}_{1}\sum_{k,\ell}\|a_{k}\|^{2}\|b_{\ell}\|^{2}=\|A\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2}\|B\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{1}.

Thus

(10.75)

\mathbf{E}|W|^{s}\leqslant 2^{s-1}(\mathbf{E}|W-\mathbf{E}W|^{s}+|\mathbf{E}W|^{s})\leqslant 2^{s}C(s)\mathfrak{C}_{s}\cdot\|A\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2s}\|B\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2s},

where we also used that by Jensen’s inequality it follows
\mathfrak{C}_{1}^{s}\leqslant\mathfrak{C}_{s}
. Next by Hölder’s inequality we obtain that

\mathbf{E}\|\widehat{\mathsf{E}}(\kappa)\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2h_{0}}\leqslant\mathbf{E}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\|\mathsf{E}^{(j)}Q\mathsf{F}^{(j)}\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2h_{0}}\right)\leqslant\left(\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathbf{E}\|\mathsf{E}^{(j)}Q\mathsf{F}^{(j)}\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2\kappa h_{0}}\right)^{1/\kappa}\leqslant\max_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathbf{E}\|\mathsf{E}^{(j)}Q\mathsf{F}^{(j)}\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2\kappa h_{0}}.

Now the desired bound follows from (
10.75
), upon setting
s=\kappa h_{0}
,
A=\mathsf{E}^{(j)}
, and
B=\mathsf{F}^{(j)}
, for
j\in[\kappa]
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Equipped with Proposition
10.12
,
we now prove Proposition
10.11
.

Proof of Proposition
10.11
.

Recall that by Proposition
10.9
, there
exists a constant
C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}
so that

(10.76)

\sum_{j=|d|+1}^{N}t_{j}(z)^{-2}\leqslant C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}\frac{N^{2}}{\log N}\qquad\mbox{ and }\qquad\sum_{j=|d|+1}^{N}t_{j}(z)^{-1}\leqslant C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N{\log N}.

For
j\in[\kappa]
we let

\mathsf{F}^{(j)}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N\log N}}\sum_{i=|d|+1}^{N}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t_{i}(z)}}e_{i}(z)\circ e_{i}^{*}(z)

and

\mathsf{E}^{(j+1)}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N\log N}}\sum_{i=|d|+1}^{N}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t_{i}(z)}}e_{i}(z)\circ f_{i}^{*}(z).

Further let

\mathsf{E}^{(1)}:=|d|^{-1/2}E_{-}(z)\qquad\mbox{ and }\qquad\mathsf{F}^{(\kappa+1)}:=|d|^{-1/2}E_{+}(z).

By Proposition
10.9
and (
2.9
)-(
2.10
) it follows that (
10.73
) holds with these choices of
\{\mathsf{E}^{(j)}\}_{j=1}^{\kappa+1}
and
\{\mathsf{F}^{(j)}\}_{j=1}^{\kappa+1}
. On the other hand note that
E(z)=C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N\log N\cdot\mathsf{F}^{(j)}\mathsf{E}^{(j+1)}
for
j\in[\kappa]
. Therefore, applying Proposition
10.12
we now derive that

\mathbf{E}\left[\|\widetilde{\mathsf{E}}\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2h_{0}}\right]\leqslant d^{2h_{0}}\cdot(C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N\log N)^{2h_{0}\kappa}\cdot C_{\ref {prop:mom-HS}}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{\kappa h_{0}}.

where
\widetilde{\mathsf{E}}:=E_{-}(z)(QE(z))^{\kappa}QE_{+}(z)
.
Hence applying Markov’s inequality with
h_{0}=\lceil\frac{2\upalpha_{0}}{\kappa(\gamma-1)}\rceil
we obtain that

\mathbf{P}\left(N^{-\gamma(\kappa+1)}\|E_{-}(z)(QE(z))^{\kappa}QE_{+}(z)\|_{{\rm HS}}\geqslant N^{-\gamma-\frac{(\gamma-1)\kappa}{2}}\right)\leqslant N^{-(\gamma+1)\kappa h_{0}}\mathbf{E}\left[\|\widetilde{\mathsf{E}}\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2h_{0}}\right]\leqslant N^{-\upalpha_{0}},

for all large
N
. This concludes the proof of (
10.72
). To obtain (
10.71
) we let

\mathsf{E}_{\star}:=\mathsf{E}^{(1)}Q\mathsf{F}^{(1)}\cdot\mathsf{E}^{(2)}Q\mathsf{F}^{(2)}\cdots\mathsf{E}^{(\kappa-1)}Q\mathsf{F}^{(\kappa-1)}\cdot\mathsf{E}^{(\kappa)}Q\mathsf{F}_{\star}^{(\kappa)},

where
\{\mathsf{E}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{\kappa}
and
\{\mathsf{F}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{\kappa-1}
are as above, and
\mathsf{F}^{(\kappa)}:=C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}^{-1/2}N^{-1}({\log N})^{1/2}\cdot E(z)
. Notice that, by (
10.76
), we have
\|\mathsf{F}_{\star}^{(\kappa)}\|_{\rm HS}\leqslant 1
. Therefore, applying applying Proposition
10.12
again we obtain that

\mathbf{E}\left[\|\mathsf{E}_{\star}\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2h_{0}}\right]\leqslant d^{h_{0}}\cdot(C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N\log N)^{2h_{0}\kappa}\cdot C_{\ref {prop:mom-HS}}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{\kappa h_{0}}.

Now (
10.71
) follows by choosing
h_{0}
as
above and applying Markov’s inequality.
The proofs of (
10.69
)-(
10.70
) are similar.
For (
10.69
) we take

\mathsf{E}^{(1)}=E(z)/(C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N\log N)

and
\mathsf{F}^{(\kappa+1)}=E_{+}(z)/\sqrt{|}d|
, while
for (
10.70
) we take
\mathsf{F}^{(\kappa+1)}=E(z)/(C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N\log N)
. Further details are omitted.
∎

The next corollary will come handy in the proof of Theorem

1.6
.

Corollary 10.13
.

In the setup of Proposition
10.11
, we have
that for any
\upalpha_{0}>0
, for all
N
large,

(10.77)

\mathbf{P}\big(N^{-\gamma}\|E(z)(I+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))^{-1}QE_{+}(z)\|_{\rm HS}\geqslant N^{-(\gamma-1)/4}\big)\leqslant N^{-\upalpha_{0}},

(10.78)

\mathbf{P}\big(N^{-2\gamma}\|E_{-}(z)(I+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))^{-1}QE(z)QE_{+}(z)\|_{\rm HS}\geqslant N^{-\gamma-(\gamma-1)/4}\big)\leqslant N^{-\upalpha_{0}},

(10.79)

\mathbf{P}\big(\|E_{-}(z)(I+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))^{-1}\|_{\rm HS}>2\sqrt{M}\big)\leqslant N^{-\upalpha_{0}},

and

(10.80)

\mathbf{P}\big(\|E(z)(I+N^{-\gamma}QE(z))^{-1}\|_{\rm HS}>2C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N/\sqrt{\log N}\big)\leqslant N^{-\upalpha_{0}}.

Proof.

The proof uses
the resolvent expansion and Proposition
10.11
. First let us prove (
10.77
).

Using the resolvent expansion, we write,
with
\hat{Q}=N^{-\gamma}Q
and
k
positive integer
and omitting throughout the argument
z
,

(10.81)

(I+\hat{Q}E)^{-1}=\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}(-\hat{Q}E)^{i}+(-\hat{Q}E)^{k}(I+\hat{Q}E)^{-1}.

Considering the first term in the right hand side of
(
10.81
), we have by (
10.69
) that, for
i\geqslant 0
,

(10.82)

\|E(-\hat{Q}E)^{i}\hat{Q}E_{+}\|_{\rm HS}\leqslant N^{-(\gamma-1)(i+1)}/2,

with probability
1-N^{-2\upalpha_{0}}
, for all
N
large.
On the other hand, by (
10.70
) and the fact that
for any two matrices
A,B
one has

\|BA\|_{\rm HS}\leqslant\|A\|\cdot\|B\|_{\rm HS}
, we
obtain that

(10.83)

\|E(-\hat{Q}E)^{k}(I+\hat{Q}E)^{-1}QE_{+}\|_{\rm HS}\leqslant N^{1-\frac{(\gamma-1)k}{2}}\cdot\|(I+\hat{Q}E)^{-1}\|\cdot\|Q\|\cdot\|E_{+}\|,

on a set of event with probability at least
1-N^{-2\upalpha_{0}}
.
Now by the second part of
Lemma
10.10
,
[
42
]
(which gives
\mathbf{E}\|Q\|=O(N^{1/2})
),
and (
2.10
), we further obtain that
\|(I+\hat{Q}E)^{-1}\|\cdot\|Q\|\cdot\|E_{+}\|\leqslant N^{\beta}

for some finite
\beta
, with probability at least
1-N^{-2\upalpha_{0}}
.
By choosing
k
large enough, we conclude
that on these events, the right hand side of (
10.83
)
can be made arbitrarily small. Combining this with (
10.81
)
and (
10.82
), we obtain (
10.77
).
The proof of (
10.78
), being similar, is omitted.

To see (
10.79
), we use the resolvent
expansion (
10.81
) and write

E_{-}(I+N^{-\gamma}QE)^{-1}=E_{-}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}E_{-}(\hat{Q}E)^{i}+E_{-}(\hat{Q}E)^{k}(I+\hat{Q}E)^{-1}.

Recall that
\|E_{-}\|_{\rm HS}=\sqrt{M}
. As above,
the other terms are controlled
by (
10.71
) and Lemma
10.10
. This completes the proof of (
10.79
). To prove (
10.80
) we observe that by (
10.76
) one has that
\|E\|_{{\rm HS}}\leqslant C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}N/\sqrt{\log N}
(without loss of generality assume
C_{\ref {prop:HS-bd}}\geqslant 1
). Therefore, arguing similarly as above one derives (
10.80
). Further details are omitted.
∎

Remark 10.14
.

The reader may inspect the proofs of Proposition
10.11
and Corollary
10.13
to observe that (
10.77
) continues to hold even if we can replace the term
N^{-(\gamma-1)/4}
by
N^{-(1-\upepsilon)\cdot(\gamma-1)}
for any
\upepsilon>0
. This improvement will be necessary later in Section
11
to argue that the eigenvectors of a randomly perturbed Jordan block is completely delocalized in sup-norm.

Remark 10.15
.

In Sections
9
and
10.1
-
10.4
we worked
with Assumption
9.1
. Since
p(S^{1})
is compact,
it follows that Assumption
9.1
holds uniformly in

z\in\Omega_{\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N}
(recall (
1.7
)). This observation will be used in Section
11
, in the
proofs of
Theorem
1.6
and Corollary
1.7
.

11.
Proofs of Theorem
1.6
and Corollary
1.7

We start with the proof of Theorem
1.6
.

11.1.
Proof of Theorem
1.6

We begin by creating a net
\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
in

\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N)
, of spacing

N^{-\theta}
and cardinality at most

O(N^{2\theta-1}\log N)
, with
\theta>\gamma\vee 4
.
Note that
for any
\hat{z}\in\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N)
,
there exists a
z^{*}=z^{*}(\hat{z})\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
with

|z^{*}-\hat{z}|\leqslant N^{-\theta}
.

1. We begin with the proof of the first point in
Theorem
1.6
, and proceed as in the sketch of Section
3
.
Let
\hat{z}\in\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N)

be an eigenvalue of
P_{N,\gamma}^{Q}
, with associated right eigenvector

v=v(\hat{z})
. Recall that
\delta=N^{-\gamma}
.
For any
z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
,
define
P^{\delta}_{z}\,:=P_{N,\gamma}^{Q}-z
,
and note that since

P^{\delta}_{z}-P^{\delta}_{\hat{z}}\,=(\hat{z}-z)I_{N}
, we have that

(11.1)

P^{\delta}_{z}v=(\hat{z}-z)v.

Set
M=|{\rm ind}_{p(S^{1})}(z)|=|d(z)|
(this yields

t_{M+1}\,\gtrsim\,\log N/N
and

\alpha
bounded below by a constant multiple of
\log N/N
, see Proposition
10.1
).

Consider the Grushin problem associated with
P^{\delta}_{z}
, see
Section
2
, and recall (
3.1
).
We then obtain, similarly to
(
3.2
) and using (
11.1
), that

(11.2)

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{i=M+1}^{N}(e_{i}(z)^{*}v)\cdot e_{i}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I-E_{+}(z)R_{+}(z))v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I-E_{+}^{\delta}(z)R_{+}(z))v-E(z)(I+\delta QE(z)^{-1}\delta QE_{+}(z)R_{+}(z)v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E^{\delta}(z)(\hat{z}-z)v+E(z)(I+\delta QE(z))^{-1}\delta QE_{+}(z)R_{+}(z)v.

We next control the right hand side of (
11.2
),
for
z=z^{*}
. As discussed in Section
3
, the control is simpler
when
\gamma>3/2
. Indeed, in that case,
by (
2.10
) and Proposition
10.1
we have that

\|E(z)\|=O(N/\log N)
uniformly in

\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
(see also Remark
10.15
). Now by the first part of Lemma
10.10
, we have,
with probability approaching
1
as
N\to\infty
, that for all

z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
,
\|(I+\delta QE(z))^{-1}\|\leqslant 2
. Therefore,
by (
2.17
),
\|E^{\delta}(z)\|=O(N/\log N)
uniformly in
\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
with the same probability.
Hence, for
z=z^{*}
,

(11.3)

\|E^{\delta}(z^{*})(\hat{z}-z^{*})v\|\leqslant O(N/\log N)\cdot N^{-\theta}=o(1).

It remains to control the second term in (
11.2
).

By construction,
\|E_{+}\|,\|R_{+}\|\leqslant 1
,
and
\|Q\|=O(N^{1/2+\upepsilon})
, for any
\upepsilon>0
, with
probability approaching
1
as
N\to\infty
. Applying Lemma
10.10
again we
obtain that with
z=z^{*}
,

(11.4)

\|E(z^{*})(I+\delta QE(z^{*}))^{-1}\delta QE_{+}(z^{*})R_{+}(z^{*})v\|\leqslant N^{-\gamma}O(N^{1/2}\cdot N/\log N)=o(1).

Together with (
11.3
),
we conclude from (
11.2
)
that if
\gamma>3/2
then for
z^{*}
with
|z^{*}-\hat{z}|=O(N^{-\theta})
,

(11.5)

\left\|\sum_{i=M+1}^{N}(e_{i}(z^{*})^{*}v)\cdot e_{i}(z^{*})\right\|=o(1).

We turn next to the case
\gamma\in(1,3/2]
. In that case,
we apply Corollary
10.13
and obtain that for all

z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
, with probability larger than
1-N^{-\upalpha_{0}}
,
we have that

(11.6)

\|E^{\delta}(z)\|=O(N/\sqrt{\log N})\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\|E(z)(I+\delta QE(z))^{-1}\delta QE_{+}(z)R_{+}(z)v\|=o(1).

Together with (
11.3
), this yields (
11.5
)
also for
\gamma\in(1,3/2]
.

For
z\in\mathds{C}
we define

(11.7)

w_{z}:=E_{+}(z)R_{+}(z)v.

We will show below that
\|v-w_{z^{*}}\|=o(1)
and
\|w_{z^{*}}-w_{\hat{z}}\|=o(1)
. This will yield (
1.9
) with
w=w_{\hat{z}}
. The localization estimate (
1.10
) then will
follow from
(
10.25
) and (
10.27
) in
Theorem
10.8
. The assertion that
w
is a random linear combination of
\{e_{j}(\hat{z})\}_{j\in[M]}
follows from its definition in (
11.7
).

Turning to proving that
\|v-w_{z^{*}}\|=o(1)
, we note that,
by
(
11.2
),
v-w_{z^{*}}
equals the left hand side of
(
11.2
), whose norm is
o(1)
by (
11.5
).

It remains to show that
\|w_{z^{*}}-w_{\hat{z}}\|=o(1)
. This follows from a standard perturbative argument. Indeed, let

B_{z}=(P_{N}-zI)^{*}(P_{N}-zI)
.
Using Proposition
10.1
, the
M
-th singular value of
both
B_{\hat{z}}
and
B_{z^{*}}
are
O(N^{-C_{\gamma}}\log N/N)

while the
(M+1)
-th is at least
C\log N/N
,
for appropriate
C,C_{\gamma}
independent of
N
. Let

\Pi_{z}=\Pi_{z,M}
be the spectral projector of
B_{z}
on the
first
M
eigenvalues. Then, choosing
\Gamma=\partial D(0,C\log N/2N)
,
we can write

\Pi_{z}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\Gamma}(B_{z}-\lambda)^{-1}d\lambda,

and an easy computation (using the Ky-Fan inequalities and the spectral gap)
gives that
\|\Pi_{\hat{z}}-\Pi_{z^{*}}\|=O(N^{-\theta}N^{2}/\log N)=o(N^{-2})

since
\theta>4
. By (
11.7
) we notice that
w_{z}=\Pi_{z}v
.
We therefore obtain that
\|w_{\hat{z}}-w_{z^{*}}\|=o(N^{-2})=o(1)
, as claimed.
This
completes the proof of
the first point of Theorem
1.6
, for all
\gamma>1
.

2. We next turn to the proof of the second part of Theorem
1.6
.
Fix

z_{0}\in\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N)

and let
\hat{z}
be an eigenvalue of
P_{N,\gamma}^{Q}
as in the second
part of the theorem. Recall that
z^{*}\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
is such that
is such that
|\hat{z}-z^{*}|\leqslant N^{-\theta}
.

The starting point of the proof
is the observation that by (
3.1
), (
11.1
),
the definition of
E_{-+}^{\delta}(z)
, and the
resolvent expansion, we
have that for any
z\in\mathds{C}
,

(11.8)

\displaystyle E_{-}^{\delta}(z)(\hat{z}-z)v=-E_{-+}^{\delta}(z)R_{+}(z)v

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!=-E_{-+}(z)R_{+}(z)v+\delta E_{-}(z)QE_{+}(z)R_{+}(z)v-\delta^{2}E_{-}(z)(I+\delta QE(z))^{-1}QE(z)QE_{+}(z)R_{+}(z)v.

(Compare with (
3.3
).)
Using the definition of
E_{-+}(z)
and of
R_{+}(z)
, we rewrite
the first two terms in the right hand side of (
11.8
) as

(11.9)

\displaystyle-E_{-+}(z)R_{+}(z)v+\delta E_{-}(z)QE_{+}(z)R_{+}(z)v

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{i=1}^{M}t_{i}\cdot(e_{i}(z)^{*}v)\cdot\delta_{i}+\delta\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{M}(e_{j}(z)^{*}v)\cdot(f_{i}(z)^{*}Qe_{j}(z))\right]\delta_{i}.

Next, recall that
E_{-}^{\delta}=E_{-}(I+\delta QE)^{-1}
. Thus,
using (
10.79
), we obtain that

(11.10)

\|E_{-}^{\delta}(z)(\hat{z}-z)v\|\leqslant 2\sqrt{M}|\hat{z}-z|,

with probability
1-N^{-2\upalpha_{0}}
, for any
\upalpha_{0}>0
, when
N
is
large enough.
By (
10.78
), we also have with the same probability that

(11.11)

\|\delta^{2}E_{-}(z)(I+\delta QE(z))^{-1}QE(z)QE_{+}(z)R_{+}(z)v\|\ \leqslant N^{-\gamma-(\gamma-1)/4}.

Upon choosing
\upalpha_{0}
sufficiently large, by a union bound, (
11.10
) and (
11.11
)
hold simultaneously for all
z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
. Thus, we have
that with probability
1-N^{-3\upalpha_{0}/2}
,

(11.12)

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{M}t_{i}\cdot(e_{i}(z)^{*}v)\cdot\delta_{i}+\delta\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{M}(e_{j}(z)^{*}v)\cdot(f_{i}(z)^{*}Qe_{j}(z))\right]\delta_{i}\right\|\leqslant 2\sqrt{M}|\hat{z}-z|+N^{-\gamma-(\gamma-1)/4}.

3. Recall next Proposition
10.1
, and that
|d(z)|=M

if
z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\cap D(z_{0},C_{0}\log N/N)
.
In what follows, we assume that
|z^{*}-\hat{z}|<N^{-\theta}
, so that the
above conditions hold for
z=z^{*}
and
\hat{z}
.
Let
M_{0}=|d(z)|-m^{0}_{\mbox{\rm sign}(d(z))}.
Assume, for the time being, that
M_{0}>0
.
Recall from (
10.3
) that if
M_{0}>0
then
for
j\in[M_{0}]
,
|t_{j}(z)|\leqslant Ce^{-cN}

for some
c>0
.

Let
A=A(z)
denote the
M_{0}\times M
matrix
with entries (in the
\delta_{i}
basis)

A_{ij}:=A_{ij}(z):=f_{i}(z)^{*}Qe_{j}(z)
and, for
j\in[M]
, set

\alpha_{j}:=e_{j}(z^{*})^{*}v
. Write
\hat{A}
for the

M_{0}\times M_{0}
submatrix of
A
consisting of its first
M_{0}
columns.
We obtain from (
11.12
) and
(
10.3
)
that

(11.13)

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{M_{0}}\delta_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{M}A_{ij}(z^{*})\alpha_{j}\right\|\leqslant N^{\gamma}\big(e^{-cN}+2\sqrt{M}|z^{*}-\hat{z}|+N^{-\gamma-(\gamma-1)/4}\big)=o(1),

since
\theta>\gamma
.
We have the following lemma, whose proof is postponed.

Lemma 11.1
.

For every
\eta>0
, there exist

0<c_{\eta},C_{\eta}<\infty
depending on
\eta
and
C_{0}
only,
so that, with probability at least
1-\eta
,
for all
z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\cap D(z_{0},C_{0}\log N/N)
,
we have that
s_{\min}(\hat{A}(z))>c_{\eta}
and that
s_{\max}(A(z))<C_{\eta}
.

Continuing with the proof of Theorem
1.6
,
we obtain from
(
11.13
) that with
a=\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M_{0}}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}}
,

(11.14)

\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{M_{0}}\delta_{j}\sum_{j=M_{0}+1}^{M}A_{ij}\alpha_{j}\right\|\geqslant ac_{\eta}-o(1).

Recall now that the norm of the projection of
v
on

\mbox{\rm span}(e_{j},j\in[M])
is
1-o(1)

by the first part of the theorem. The orthogonality of the
e_{j}
’s then gives that
\|\alpha\|=1+o(1)
, where
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{M})
,
and together with Lemma
11.1
, (
11.14
), also that,
with
b=\sqrt{\sum_{j=M_{0}+1}^{M}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}}
,

C_{\eta}b\geqslant c_{\eta}a-o(1)=c_{\eta}\sqrt{1-b^{2}}-o(1).

It follows that, with
M_{0}>0
, for some
c_{0}>0
independent of
N
,

(11.15)

b^{2}=\sum_{j=M_{0}+1}^{M}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}>c_{0}.

For

M_{0}=0
,
the lower bound (
11.15
) also holds,
by the first part of Theorem
1.6
.

We are now ready to complete the proof of (
1.11
).
To this end, we assume in the sequel that

d>0
, the case
d<0
requiring only notation changes. Set

(11.16)

w^{\prime}:=\sum_{j=M_{0}+1}^{M}\alpha_{j}e_{j}\quad\text{ and }\quad w^{\prime\prime}:=\sum_{j=M_{0}+1}^{M}\alpha_{j}\hat{e}_{j},

with
\alpha_{j}=e_{j}(z^{*})^{*}v
as before and

(11.17)

\hat{e}_{j}:=\sum_{\nu=M_{0}+1}^{M}b_{j}(\nu)\frac{\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\|},

with
b_{j}(\nu)
’s as in (
10.23
).

Since
\|\alpha\|=1+o(1)
, using the triangle inequality,
the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and (
10.23
), we observe that

(11.18)

\|w^{\prime}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}([\ell,\ell^{\prime}])}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\|w^{\prime\prime}\|^{2}_{\ell^{2}([\ell,\ell^{\prime}])}-\epsilon_{N},

where
\epsilon_{N}=O(\log N/N)+O(N^{-2C_{\gamma}}(\log N)^{2})
.

We next derive a lower bound on
\|w^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{2}([\ell,\ell^{\prime}])}^{2}
.
Set

\zeta_{\nu\nu^{\prime}}:=\zeta_{\nu}^{+}\overline{\zeta_{\nu^{\prime}}^{+}}
.
Recall Proposition
9.3
and note that

(11.19)

\langle\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\mid\mathfrak{z}_{\nu^{\prime}}^{+}\rangle_{\ell^{2}([\ell,\ell^{\prime}])}=\zeta_{\nu\nu^{\prime}}^{\ell}\frac{1-\zeta_{\nu\nu^{\prime}}^{\ell^{\prime}-\ell+1}}{1-\zeta_{\nu\nu^{\prime}}},\qquad\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in[M]\setminus[M_{0}].

Note that

\frac{1}{(1-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2})\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\|^{2}}=1+o(1)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\|^{2}\,\asymp\,N/\log N.

Thus, by Remark
10.7
, from (
11.19
) we obtain that

(11.20)

\frac{\langle\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\mid\mathfrak{z}_{\nu^{\prime}}^{+}\rangle_{\ell^{2}([\ell,\ell^{\prime}])}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\|\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\|}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}O(\log N/N)&\mbox{ if }\nu\neq\nu^{\prime},\\
(1+o(1))(|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2\ell}-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(\ell^{\prime}+1)})&\mbox{ if }\nu=\nu^{\prime}.\end{array}\right.

On the other hand, since
\nu,\nu^{\prime}>M_{0}
,
for
q\in[N]
,

|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2q}=|\zeta_{\nu^{\prime}}^{+}|^{2q}=e^{-cq\log N/N(1+o(1))}
for some constant
c>0

independent of
\nu
, and therefore,
for
\ell,\ell^{\prime}=O(N/\log N)
,

(11.21)

(|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2\ell}-|\zeta_{\nu}^{+}|^{2(\ell^{\prime}+1)})\geqslant c^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime}-\ell)\cdot\frac{\log N}{N},

for some
c^{\prime}>0
. Hence, recalling (
11.16
)-(
11.17
),
from (
10.23
),
(
11.20
) and
(
11.21
), we deduce that for any
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[1,N]
,

(11.22)

\|w^{\prime\prime}\|_{\ell^{2}([\ell,\ell^{\prime}])}^{2}=\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=M_{0}+1}^{M}\overline{\alpha}_{j}{\alpha}_{j^{\prime}}\sum_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}=M_{0}+1}^{M}\overline{b_{j}(\nu)}{b_{j^{\prime}}(\nu^{\prime})}\cdot\frac{\langle\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\mid\mathfrak{z}_{\nu^{\prime}}^{+}\rangle_{\ell^{2}([\ell,\ell^{\prime}])}}{\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu}^{+}\|\cdot\|\mathfrak{z}_{\nu^{\prime}}^{+}\|}\\
\geqslant\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=M_{0}+1}^{M}\overline{\alpha}_{j}{\alpha}_{j^{\prime}}\sum_{\nu=M_{0}+1}^{M}\overline{b_{j}(\nu)}{b_{j^{\prime}}(\nu^{\prime})}\cdot c^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime}-\ell)\cdot\frac{\log N}{N}+O(\log N/N)\\
=c^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime}-\ell)\cdot\frac{\log N}{N}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=M_{0}+1}^{M}\alpha_{j}\overline{\alpha}_{j^{\prime}}\langle b_{j}|b_{j^{\prime}}\rangle+O(\log N/N)\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:ao1}}}{{=}}c^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime}-\ell)\cdot\frac{\log N}{N}\left(o(1)+\sum_{j=M_{0}+1}^{M}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\right)+O(\log N/N)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq-lb}}}{{\geqslant}}\frac{c_{0}c^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime}-\ell)}{2}\cdot\frac{\log N}{N}+O(\log N/N).

In the range of
\ell,\ell^{\prime}
of the statement of Theorem
1.6
,
the first term in the right hand side of (
11.22
)
is of order larger that
\epsilon_{N}+O(\log N/N)
, and therefore, for such
\ell,\ell^{\prime}
, by (
11.18
)

(11.23)

\|w^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{2}([\ell,\ell^{\prime}])}\geqslant c_{1}(\ell^{\prime}-\ell)\log N/N,

for some
c_{1}>0
. It remains to show that (
11.23
) continues to hold with
w^{\prime}
replaced by
w=w_{\hat{z}}
.

To see this, note that
by (
10.27
), we have that
\|w_{z^{*}}-w^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{2}([C\log N,N])}=O(N^{-2})
,
for some
C<\infty
. Recall from
above that
\|w_{\hat{z}}-w_{z^{*}}\|=o(N^{-2})
. Thus, by the triangle inequality we indeed have that (
11.23
) holds for
w
with
\ell\geqslant C\log N
and
\ell^{\prime}
as in the statement of Theorem
1.6
. On the other hand, if
1\leqslant\ell\leqslant C\log N
we observe that
\ell^{\prime}-\ell\leqslant 2(\ell^{\prime}-C\log N)
for any
\ell^{\prime}\geqslant N^{c_{0}}
. Thus, upon shrinking
c_{1}
in (
11.23
), that lower bound continues to hold for
w
and such values of
\ell
.
The proof of (
1.12
) is now immediate from (
1.9
).
The assertion about the constant
c_{0}
follows from Remark
6.17
and the definition of
\epsilon_{N}
.
This finally completes the proof.
∎

11.2.
Proof of Lemma
11.1

Throughout the proof, we assume for notational simplicity that
d>0
, the general case posing no
new difficulties. We begin by rewriting
\hat{A}(z)
, see (
11.13
). Fix
M_{1}=m_{+}-m_{+}^{0}

and note that
M_{1}\geqslant M_{0}
with equality if
N_{+}=0
.
Let
E_{0}(z)
denote the
N\times M_{0}
matrix with columns
e_{j}(z)
, and
F_{0}(z)
the
N\times M_{0}
matrix with columns
f_{j}(z)
.
It follows from Lemma
9.3
and Proposition

10.3
that
the columns of
E_{0}(z)
are almost orthonormal (with inner product
o(1)
between different columns)
and with
|e_{j}(z)(k)|\leqslant e^{-ck}
,
k=1,\ldots,N
for some
c>0
.
Similarly, the columns of
F_{0}(z)
are almost orthonormal,
with
|f_{j}(z)(k)|\leqslant e^{-c(N-k)}
,
k=1,\ldots,N
.
Recall that
\hat{A}(z)=F_{0}(z)^{*}QE_{0}(z)
.

We begin by fixing a particular
z
as in the statement of the lemma, and write
\bar{A}=\hat{A}(z)
,
\bar{E}_{0}=E_{0}(z)
,
\bar{F}_{0}=F_{0}(z)
.
Note that

\mathbf{E}\|\bar{A}\|_{{\rm HS}}^{2}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{M_{0}}\mathbf{E}|\bar{A}_{ij}|^{2}\leqslant\mathfrak{C}_{2}\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{M_{0}}\|e_{i}(z)\|^{2}\cdot\|f_{j}(z)\|^{2}\right)\leqslant 2\mathfrak{C}_{2}M_{0}^{2},

for all large
N
. Therefore,
one can find a constant

C(\eta)
so that

(11.24)

\mathbf{P}(s_{\max}(\bar{A})>C(\eta)/2)\leqslant\eta/4.

We next control
s_{\min}(\bar{A})
.
We use the inequality

(11.25)

s_{\min}(\bar{A})\geqslant\frac{|\det(\bar{A})|}{(s_{\max}(\bar{A}))^{M_{0}-1}}.

To control the determinant in (
11.25
), we
begin by considering the matrix
\bar{E}_{0}
. Because the columns of
E_{0}

decay exponentially and the columns of
E_{0}
are almost orthogonal,
there exists
a
K>0
so that the
K\times M_{0}
sub-matrix
\bar{E}_{0,K}

consisting of the first
K
rows of
\bar{E}_{0}

has columns with inner product smaller than
1/M_{0}^{2}
, and therefore
singular values all larger than
1/2
. It follows from

[
49
, Lemma 4.3]
(see also
[
34
]
)
that there exists an
M_{0}\times M_{0}
sub-matrix of

\bar{E}_{0,K}
, denoted
\bar{E}_{0,K,M_{0}}
,
with singular values all larger than
1/2\sqrt{1+(K-M_{0})M_{0}}
.
In particular, there exists a (nonrandom) constant
c_{0}
so that

\mathrm{det}(\bar{E}_{0,K,M_{0}})\geqslant c_{0}
.
Similarly, with
\bar{F}_{0,K}
denoting the sub-matrix of
F_{0}
consisting from its last
K
rows,
there exists an
M_{0}\times M_{0}
sub-matrix of
\bar{F}_{0,K}
, denoted

\bar{F}_{0,K,M_{0}}
, with

\mathrm{det}(\bar{F}_{0,K,M_{0}})\geqslant c_{0}
.

Returning to the control of
the determinant in (
11.25
), we
note that the latter is
a homogeneous polynomial in the entries of
Q
, and apply Lemma

6.12
, as follows.
Let
I=\{i_{1}\,<i_{2}<\cdots<\,i_{M_{0}}\}\subset[K]

denote the
list of rows of
\bar{E}_{0}
participating in
\bar{E}_{0,K,M_{0}}
. Similarly,
let
J=\{j_{1}\,<j_{2}<\cdots<\,j_{M_{0}}\}\subset[N]\setminus[N-K]

denote the list of rows of
\bar{F}_{0}

participating in
\bar{F}_{0,K,M_{0}}
.
Let
U_{\ell}=Q_{i_{\ell},j_{\ell}}
,
\ell=1,\ldots M_{0}
, then by the Cauchy-Binet formula,

(11.26)

\det(\bar{A})=\sum_{\mathcal{I}\subset[M_{0}]}Z_{\mathcal{I}}\prod_{\ell\in\mathcal{I}}U_{\ell}

for some random variables
Z_{\mathcal{I}}
(which depend only on
Q_{a,b}
with

(a,b)\not\in\cup_{\ell=1}^{M_{0}}(i_{\ell},j_{\ell})
).
Crucially,
|Z_{[M_{0}]}|=|\det(\bar{E}_{0,K,M_{0}})\cdot\det(F_{0,K,M_{0}})|\geqslant c_{0}^{2M_{0}}=:c_{\star}
.

By Lemma
6.12
,
we obtain that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,c_{\star}e^{-1})
,

(11.27)

\mathbf{P}\left(\det(\bar{A})\leqslant\varepsilon\right)\leqslant\bar{C}_{\ref {lem:anti-conc}}\cdot\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{c_{\star}}\right)^{(1+\upeta)}\cdot\left(\log\left(\frac{c_{\star}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)^{M_{0}-1}.

Combining (
11.27
) with (
11.25
) and
(
11.24
), we obtain that there exists a
c_{\eta}>0
so that

(11.28)

\mathbf{P}(s_{\min}(\bar{A})>2c_{\eta})\geqslant 1-\eta/4.

Next, let
z^{\prime}\neq z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\cap D(z_{0},C_{0}\log N/N)
. Since
|dp(z)|
is bounded below uniformly in a fixed small (
N
-independent)
neighborhood of
z_{0}
, we have that the roots of
p-z
and
p-z^{\prime}
satisfy,
for all
j\leqslant N_{-}+N_{+}
,
that
|\zeta_{j}^{+}(z)-\zeta_{j}^{+}(z^{\prime})|\leqslant C_{1}\log N/N
. Using
Propositions
9.3
,
9.6
, and
10.3
,
this implies in turn that there exists a constant
C
so that, for
j=1,\ldots,M_{0}
,

(11.29)

\|e_{j}(z)-e_{j}(z^{\prime})\|\leqslant C|z-z^{\prime}|+Ce^{-N/C}\leqslant\,2C|z-z^{\prime}|.

Indeed, Proposition
9.3
yields the first inequality
for the
\widetilde{u}_{\ell}^{+}
,
\ell=1,\ldots,M_{1}
, Proposition
9.6

allows one to transfer this to the quasimodes
\psi_{j}^{+}
, and finally Proposition
10.3
transfers it to the
e_{j}
’s. The right most inequality in (
11.29
) follows from the fact that
\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}
is a net of mesh size
N^{-\theta}
. By the same argument the same bound continues to hold when
e_{j}
’s are replaced by
f_{j}
’s.

Denote
\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}:=\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\cap D(z_{0},C_{0}\log N/N)
. Since
\hat{A}_{i,j}(\cdot)
,
i,j\in[M_{0}]
, are linear in the entries of
Q
,
applying
[
75
, Theorem 2]
together with (
11.29
) we obtain that, for any
h\in\mathds{N}
,

\max_{i,j\in[M_{0}]}\max_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}}\mathbf{E}|\hat{A}_{i,j}(z)-\hat{A}_{i,j}(z^{\prime})|^{2h}\leqslant C_{h}\cdot\left(\frac{\log N}{N}\right)^{2h},

for some constant
C_{h}<\infty
, independent of
N
, where we also used that
|z-z^{\prime}|=O(\log N/N)
for all
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma.z_{0}}
. Therefore,
choosing
h
sufficiently large so that
|\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}|N^{-h}=o(1)
, applying Markov’s inequality,
taking a union bound over
{i,j\in[M_{0}]}
and
