{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}}
and recalling Assumption
1.1
,
we deduce that, on an event of probability approaching one,

(11.30)

s_{\max}(\hat{A}(z)-\hat{A}(z^{\prime}))\leqslant\|\hat{A}(z)-\hat{A}(z^{\prime})\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leqslant c_{\eta},

uniformly for all
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}
.
By the Ky Fan inequalities,
s_{\min}(\hat{A}(z^{\prime}))\geqslant s_{\min}(\hat{A}(z))-s_{\max}(\hat{A}(z)-\hat{A}(z^{\prime}))
. So we obtain the claim for
s_{\min}(\hat{A}(z^{\prime}))
.

Finally, we turn to the control of
s_{\max}(A(z))
, for all
z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}
.
Let
\widetilde{E}(z)
and
\widetilde{E}_{0}(z)
be
N\times(M-M_{0})
matrix
whose columns are
\mathfrak{z}_{j+M_{0}}^{+}(z)/\|\mathfrak{z}_{j+M_{0}}^{+}(z)\|

(as in Proposition
9.3
) and
e_{j+M_{0}}(z)
, respectively,
for
j=1,\dots,M-M_{0}
. As in Proposition
9.6
, let

\widetilde{F}(z)
be the
N\times M_{0}
matrix with
columns
\psi_{i}^{-}(z)
,
i=1,\dots,M_{0}
. Set
\widetilde{A}(z):=\widetilde{F}(z)^{*}Q\widetilde{E}(z)
.
Recalling the definition of
A
from the discussion above (
11.13
),
we find that in the new notation,
A(z)=F_{0}(z)^{*}Q\widetilde{E}_{0}(z)
.
Further let
\widetilde{B}(z)
and
\widetilde{C}(z)
be the matrices whose columns are
\{b_{j+M_{0}}\}
and
\{a_{j}\}
, as defined in Proposition
10.6
.
By Proposition
10.6
we have that

\|\widetilde{E}(z)\widetilde{B}(z)-\widetilde{E}_{0}(z)\|_{{\rm HS}},\|\widetilde{F}(z)C(z)-F_{0}(z)\|_{{\rm HS}}=O(N^{-c})\quad\text{ and }\quad\|\widetilde{E}_{0}(z)\|_{{\rm HS}},\|F_{0}(z)\|_{{\rm HS}}\leqslant 2M,

for all
z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}
and all large
N
,
where
c>0
is some constant.
Therefore, applying Proposition
10.12
, the triangle inequality,
and proceeding as in the proof of (
11.30
), we deduce that

\max_{z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}}\|A(z)-\widetilde{B}(z)^{*}\widetilde{A}(z)\widetilde{C}(z)\|\leqslant\max_{z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}}\|A(z)-\widetilde{B}(z)^{*}\widetilde{A}(z)\widetilde{C}(z)\|_{{\rm HS}}=o(1),

on an event with probability approaching one, as
N\to+\infty
.
Moreover, by (
10.19
) and (
10.24
) we have that

\|\widetilde{B}(z)\|
and
\|\widetilde{C}(z)\|
are uniformly bounded for all

z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}
. Therefore, it suffices to bound
\sup_{z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}}\|\widetilde{A}(z)\|
, and hence in fact it is enough to prove that for fixed
i\in[M_{0}],j\in[M]\setminus[M_{0}]
,

(11.31)

\lim_{x\to\infty}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\left(\sup_{z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}}|\widetilde{A}_{ij}(z)|>x\right)=0.

Toward this goal, we note that
for
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}
, one has that for some constant
C_{0}<\infty
,
independent of
N
,

\left\|\frac{\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{j}(z)}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{j}(z)\|}-\frac{\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{j}(z^{\prime})}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{j}(z^{\prime})\|}\right\|\leqslant C_{0}|z-z^{\prime}|\cdot\frac{N}{\log N},\quad j\in[M]\setminus[M_{0}],

and by Proposition
10.6
the bound (
11.29
) continues to hold with
e_{j}(\cdot)
replaced by
\psi_{j}^{-}(\cdot)
for
j\in[M_{0}]
. Therefore, upon using Assumption

1.1
and applying
[
75
, Theorem 2]
, we find that
for some constant
C_{h}
independent of
N
,

(11.32)

\mathbf{E}|\widetilde{A}_{i,j}(z)-\widetilde{A}_{i,j}(z^{\prime})|^{h}\leqslant C_{h}\Big(\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|N}{\log N}\Big)^{h}.

We now apply a Komogorov-type argument. Namely, for
k=0,1,\ldots
, let

\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0},k}
denote a minimal subset of

\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}
so that for any

z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}
there
exists

z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0},k}
so that

|z-z^{\prime}|\leqslant 2^{-k}\log N/N
. Note that
|\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0},k}|=O(2^{2k})
. Now, fix a constant
x
and introduce the event

\mathcal{A}_{k}=\{\exists\,z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0},k}:|z-z^{\prime}|\leqslant 2^{-k+1}\log N/N,|\widetilde{A}_{i,j}(z)-\widetilde{A}_{i,j}(z^{\prime})|>x/j^{2}\},

and set
\mathcal{B}=\cap_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{k}^{\complement}
,
where
2^{k_{0}-1}\leqslant C_{0}N/\log N\leqslant 2^{k_{0}}
. On the event
\mathcal{B}
we
have that

\max_{z\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}}|\widetilde{A}_{i,j}(z)|\leqslant 10x.

On the other hand, by Markov’s inequality and
(
11.32
), for some constant
C_{h}^{\prime}
independent of
N
,

(11.33)

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{B}^{\complement})\leqslant C_{h}^{\prime}x^{-h}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}2^{2k}2^{-hk}.

Choosing
h=4
, this implies
(
11.31
) and completes
the proof of the lemma.
∎

11.3.
Proof of Corollary
1.7

We begin by applying Theorem
1.5
with
\mu
replaced by
\mu/8
. This yields the existence of
constants
\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}}
such that

(11.34)

\mathbf{P}\big(\mathcal{N}_{\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N),N,\gamma}<(1-\mu/8)N\big)\to_{N\to\infty}0.

We now claim the following.
Fix
\bar{C}<\infty
,
z_{0}\in\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N)
, and
\mu>0
. Then there exist
0<\mu_{1},\mu_{2}<\infty
such that, for all large
N
,

(11.35)

\mathbf{P}\left(\exists i\in[N]\text{ such that }\mathrm{supp\,}_{\mu_{1}}(v(\lambda_{i}^{N}))<\mu_{2}N/\log N\text{ and }\lambda_{i}^{N}\in\mathds{D}_{z_{0}}\right)\leqslant\mu/(4\bar{C}),

where
\mathds{D}_{z_{0}}:=D(z_{0},C_{\ref {theo-location}}\log N/(2N))\cap\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N)
. To prove (
11.35
) we borrow some notation from the proof of Theorem
1.6
. Let
\hat{z}\in\mathds{D}_{z_{0}}
and
z^{*}=z^{*}(\hat{z})\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}:=\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\cap D_{z_{0}}
such that
|\hat{z}-z_{*}|\leqslant N^{-\theta}
.
Recall from the proof of Theorem
1.6
that
w_{z}=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\alpha_{j}e_{j}(z)
, for
z\in\mathds{C}
,
w^{\prime}=w^{\prime}(z^{*})=\sum_{j=M_{0}+1}^{M}\alpha_{j}e_{j}(z^{*})
, and
w=w_{\hat{z}}
. Also recall that
\|w-w_{z^{*}}\|=o(1)
. Applying Lemma
11.1
, with
\eta=\mu/(4\bar{C})
, and upon choosing
\mu_{1}
sufficiently small, we obtain that, simultaneously for all
z^{*}
in
\mathcal{N}_{\gamma,z_{0}}
, and hence simultaneously for all
\hat{z}\in\mathds{D}_{z_{0}}
, and any
I\subset[N]
such that
\|v\|_{\ell^{2}(I)}>1-\mu_{1}
,

\sum_{j=M_{0}+1}^{M}|\alpha_{j}|\|e_{j}\|_{\ell^{2}(I)}\geqslant\|w^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{2}(I)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq-main1}}}{{\geqslant}}\|v\|_{\ell^{2}(I)}-\|w_{z^{*}}-w^{\prime}\|-o(1)\geqslant 1-\mu_{1}/2-\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{M_{0}}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq-lb}}}{{\geqslant}}\mu_{1},

with probability at least
1-\mu/(4\bar{C})
, where
v=v(\hat{z})
is the right eigenvector corresponding to
\hat{z}
. On the other hand by (
10.25
) it follows that for any such
I
we must have that
|I|\geqslant\mu_{2}N/\log N
, for some
\mu_{2}>0
. This, indeed proves (
11.35
).

We continue with the proof of
the corollary.
Introduce the following notation.
For
z_{0}\in\mathds{C}
set

\mathcal{J}_{z_{0}}:=\left\{\#\{i\in[N]:\lambda_{i}^{N}\in\mathds{D}_{z_{0}}\}\leqslant C_{\ref {thm-thintube2}}C_{\ref {theo-location}}^{4}\log N\right\},

\mathcal{C}_{z_{0}}:=\left\{\exists i\in[N]\text{ such that }\mathrm{supp\,}_{\mu_{1}}(v(\lambda_{i}^{N}))<\mu_{2}N/\log N\text{ and }\lambda_{i}^{N}\in\mathds{D}_{z_{0}}\right\},

and for
i\in[N]

\mathbb{I}_{i}:={\bf I}(\mathrm{supp\,}_{\mu_{1}}(v(\lambda_{i}^{N}))<\mu_{2}N/\log N).

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a net of
\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N)
of mesh size
C_{\ref {theo-location}}\log N/(2N)
. It is clear that
|\mathcal{R}|\leqslant CC_{\ref {theo-location}}^{-1}N/\log N
, for some
C<\infty
. Note also that by Theorem
7.4
,
for any
z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}
,

(11.36)

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{J}_{z_{0}}^{c})\leqslant N^{-2}.

We can now complete the proof. Indeed, for all large
N
,

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbf{E}[\mathbb{I}_{i}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq-finalloc-new}}}{{\leqslant}}\sum_{i}\mathbf{E}[\mathbb{I}_{i}\cdot{\bf I}(\lambda_{i}^{N}\in\Omega(\varepsilon_{\ref {theo-location}},C_{\ref {theo-location}},N)]+N\mu/4\\
\leqslant\sum_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{i:\lambda_{i}^{N}\in\mathds{D}_{z_{0}}}\mathbb{I}_{i}\right]+N\mu/4\leqslant N\sum_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{J}_{z_{0}}^{c})+\sum_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}}\mathbf{E}\left[\sum_{i:\lambda_{i}^{N}\in\mathds{D}_{z_{0}}}\mathbb{I}_{i}\cdot{\bf I}(\mathcal{J}_{z_{0}})\right]+N\mu/4\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cJ-z}}}{{\leqslant}}C_{\ref {thm-thintube2}}C_{\ref {theo-location}}^{4}\log N\sum_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{R}}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{C}_{z_{0}})+N\mu/2\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:cor-pre}}}{{\leqslant}}N\mu,

where in the penultimate step we used hat
|\mathcal{R}|=o(N)
, and in last step we chose
\overline{C}=CC_{\ref {thm-thintube2}}C_{\ref {theo-location}}^{3}
, and used the bound on
|\mathcal{R}|
. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 11.2

(Sup-norm delocalization)
.

We borrow notation from the proof of Theorem
1.6
. Let
P_{N}
be the Jordan block. By Remark
5.5
, for any fixed
\gamma^{\prime}<\gamma
, all eigenvalues of
P_{N,\gamma}^{Q}
are inside the disc
D(0,1-(\gamma^{\prime}-1)\log N/N)
with probability approaching one. Therefore, for any eigenvalue
\hat{z}
and its approximating net point
z^{*}
we can take
C_{\gamma}=\gamma^{\prime}-1
(see (
9.5
)). On the other hand, by Remark
10.14
we observe that the
o(1)
term in (
11.6
) can be replaced by
O(N^{-(\gamma^{\prime}-1)})
and hence the same can be done for (
11.5
). Furthermore, in this case we have only one pure state, recall Remarks
9.4
and
9.5
. Thus
M=1
. Therefore, by Remark
10.5
we next derive that

\left\|\frac{\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}\|}-v\right\|_{\infty}\leqslant\left\|\frac{\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}\|}-v\right\|\leqslant\|v-\alpha_{1}e_{1}\|+|1-|\alpha_{1}||+\left\|\frac{\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}\|}-e_{1}\right\|=O(N^{-(\gamma^{\prime}-1)}\log N).

Since,
\gamma>3/2
, upon choosing
\gamma^{\prime}
appropriately, it is now immediate that

\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}\asymp\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}\|_{\infty}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}\|}\leqslant\|v\|_{\infty}\leqslant 2\frac{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}\|_{\infty}}{\|\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{1}\|}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{N}}.
